## The Plays of Roswitha2 / 罗斯维塔戏剧集

===Original===
By Christopher St. John

This translation of the six plays of Roswitha (there are really seven, for the two parts of *Gallicanus* practically constitute two separate dramas) was begun in the year 1912 and completed in 1914. The lively interest provoked by the stage performance of one of the translations (that of the play *Paphnutius*) by the Pioneer Players in January 1914 led me to think that the publication of the whole theatre of Roswitha in English would be welcomed by all students of the drama. Unfortunately, the war delayed publication, and the manuscript was entirely destroyed by a fire at the publisher’s premises in Dublin during the Irish insurrection of Easter 1916.

===Chinese===
克里斯托弗·圣约翰（Christopher St. John）著

罗斯维塔（Roswitha）六部剧作的这一译本（其实共有七部，因为《加利卡努斯》（Gallicanus）的两部分几乎构成两部各自独立的戏剧）始译于 1912 年，完成于 1914 年。1914 年 1 月，先驱剧团（Pioneer Players）将其中一部译作（即《帕夫努提乌斯》（Paphnutius））搬上舞台，所激起的热烈反响令我想到：将罗斯维塔的全部剧作用英语出版，会受一切戏剧研究者的欢迎。不幸的是，战争使出版延宕，而手稿又于 1916 年复活节爱尔兰起义期间，在都柏林出版商的场所毁于一场大火，荡然无存。

===Original===
The work of collating the various Latin texts of Roswitha’s plays and producing a translation which should preserve some of the naive simplicity of the original had been a difficult one, and to begin it all over again was a heartbreaking task. The consciousness that the interest in Roswitha provoked by the performance of *Paphnutius* had waned did not alleviate the heaviness of spirit in which the work of replacing the burned manuscript was undertaken.

===Chinese===
校勘罗斯维塔剧作的诸种拉丁文本，产出一个能存原作几分质朴天真的译本，本是一桩艰辛的工作；而要将这一切从头再来，更是一桩令人心碎的差事。《帕夫努提乌斯》的演出曾激起世人对罗斯维塔的兴趣，如今这兴趣已然消退——意识到这一点，并未能减轻重制被焚手稿时心头的沉重。

===Original===
Those readers who are unable or unwilling to compare the translations with the original should be warned that Roswitha’s dialogue is characterized by a simplicity and conciseness hardly attainable in any tongue but Latin. The difficulty of finding equivalents for the terse phrases employed tempts the translator to “write them up.” Although I have aimed at producing a readable translation for lovers of the drama in all its forms rather than an exact paraphrase for scholars, I have tried to resist this temptation at the risk of making the dialogue seem at times almost ludicrously bald. Except in a few cases where the use of “thou” seemed dramatically fit, “tu” has been rendered by “you.” Roswitha’s style is colloquial, and the constant employment of the singular pronoun would misrepresent its character. The Latin is not obsolete, and it would surely be a mistake to translate it into an obsolete vernacular. Although the author’s syntax is decadent, and there is a tendency to make every sentence analytical, her use of words is classical, and her Latin in this respect superior to the scholastic Latin of the Middle Ages. The only principle observed in my translation has been the general one laid down by Edward Fitzgerald: “The live dog is to be preferred to the dead lion—in translation at any rate,” and if this has involved a loss of dignity, I hope there may be some compensating gain in ease and force.3 In regard to the names of the characters in the plays, when there were well-known English equivalents such as “Hadrian” and “Constantine” I have not hesitated to use them, but when there were none I have given the Latin names. There is a good precedent for this inconsistency. We speak of “Rome” and “Venice,” but we do not try to Anglicize Perugia or Assisi.

===Chinese===
那些无法或不愿以译文对照原文的读者，应当预先得到提醒：罗斯维塔的对白以简洁凝练见长，这种品质除拉丁文外，几乎非任何语言所能企及。要为她那些精炼的短语寻找对等表达，其困难诱使译者去“添笔渲染”。尽管我志在为喜爱一切形态戏剧的读者提供一个可读的译本，而非为学者提供精确的释文，我仍竭力抵制这一诱惑，哪怕冒着自己笔下的对白有时显得近乎可笑地直露的风险。除少数几处以“thou”更合戏剧效果之外，“tu”一律译作“you”。罗斯维塔的文风本是口语，若一味搬用单数人称代词，反会失其本色。她的拉丁文并非死文字，把它译成一种过时的白话，无疑是错误。作者的句法固然已趋衰颓，且有一种把每个句子都化作分析式的倾向，但她的用词却是古典的，单就这一点而言，她的拉丁文胜过中世纪的经院拉丁文。我在翻译中遵循的唯一原则，便是爱德华·菲茨杰拉德（Edward Fitzgerald）立下的那条通则：“活狗胜过死狮——至少在翻译上是如此。”倘若这有损庄严，我希望在流畅与力量上能有补偿。3至于剧中人物的名字，凡有通行英语对应形式者，如“哈德良”（Hadrian）与“君士坦丁”（Constantine），我便毫不迟疑地采用；凡没有的，我便径用拉丁名字。这种不一贯自有良好的先例：我们说“罗马”与“威尼斯”，却并不设法把佩鲁贾（Perugia）或阿西西（Assisi）也英国化。

===Original===
The plays are all founded on well-known legends, which Roswitha follows very closely as regards the facts. But she shows great originality in her use of the facts and in her development of characters often merely indicated in the legends. Three of the plays, *Gallicanus*, *Dulcitius*, and *Sapientia*, deal with the conflict between infant Christianity and Paganism, martyrdoms under the Emperors Hadrian, Diocletian, and Julian the Apostate being the chief incidents. *Gallicanus*, which comes first in the manuscript, shows considerable skill in dramatic construction. Incident follows rapidly on incident. The scene lies alternately in Rome and on the battlefield, yet the action is kept quite clear. The story is easily followed, although Roswitha, like all good dramatists, eschews narrative. Gallicanus, one of the Emperor Constantine’s generals, claims the hand of the Emperor’s daughter as a reward for undertaking a dangerous campaign against the Scythians. The Emperor knows that Constance has taken a solemn vow of chastity, but he dares not offend Gallicanus by a refusal, on account of the value of his military services. So he temporizes, and consults Constance, who shows great shrewdness in dealing with the situation. She sends her almoners, John and Paul, to accompany Gallicanus on the Scythian expedition, in the hope that they will convert him to Christianity before he returns to marry her. The stratagem succeeds. Gallicanus, saved from defeat at a critical moment in the battle by the intervention of a heavenly host, becomes a Christian, and on his return to Rome shows respect for Constance’s resolution to remain in the virgin state, and renounces her. But he admits that the renunciation is bitter—Roswitha often shows such touches of sympathy with natural human desires—and we are made to feel that, although the dramatist was in no doubt that the life of chastity, poverty, and obedience is the highest life, she understood how hard it is for those who embrace it to believe that the yoke will be easy and the burden light.

===Chinese===
这些剧作皆以著名传说为本，就事实而言，罗斯维塔紧紧依循传说。但在运用这些事实、发展传说中往往一笔带过的人物方面，她显示出极大的独创性。《加利卡努斯》、《杜尔基提乌斯》（Dulcitius）与《萨皮恩提亚》（Sapientia）三剧处理的是基督教初起时与异教的冲突，主要情节是哈德良（Hadrian）、戴克里先（Diocletian）与叛教者尤利安（Julian the Apostate）诸帝治下的殉道。《加利卡努斯》在抄本中位居第一，显示出相当可观的戏剧结构功力。事件一个紧接一个。场景在罗马与战场之间交替转换，动作却始终清晰了然。故事易于追随，尽管罗斯维塔同一切优秀剧作家一样回避叙述。加利卡努斯是君士坦丁皇帝麾下大将，他以出征迎击斯基泰人这场凶险战役为条件，求娶皇帝之女。皇帝深知康斯坦丝（Constance）已立下庄重的守贞誓言，却因加利卡努斯军功可贵而不敢以拒绝相犯。于是他敷衍周旋，与康斯坦丝商议，而她应付这一局面时显得极为精明老练。她派自己的施赈员约翰（John）与保罗（Paul）随加利卡努斯同征斯基泰，指望二人在将军班师迎娶她之前使他皈依基督教。这一计成功了。加利卡努斯在战局危急之际得天军之助而免于败绩，皈依了基督教，回到罗马后尊重康斯坦丝终身守贞的决心，弃绝了娶她之念。但他承认这弃绝是苦涩的——罗斯维塔常有这种体恤自然人性的笔触——我们由此体会到：尽管剧作家毫不怀疑贞洁、清贫与顺服的生活才是最高的生活，她也深知，凡拥抱这生活的人，要相信“轭是容易的，担子是轻省的”何其困难。

===Original===
The second play, *Dulcitius*, is poorly constructed and, as a whole, less interesting than any of the plays. Yet it has some features which repay close study. It is the only play of Roswitha’s obviously designed to provoke laughter, and if the level of the opening scenes had been maintained would be a very droll religious farce. Here we have the usual tale of martyrdom interspersed with incidents of buffoonery. The conventional cruel and bloody executioners are replaced by comic soldiers and a comic governor. Unfortunately, the farcical vein is suddenly abandoned, perhaps because Roswitha’s Abbess thought such fooling undignified in a nun! There must be some explanation of the sudden disappearance of the comic character of Dulcitius from the play. However, even as it stands *Dulcitius* is worth a great deal, since it affords the best proof we have that Roswitha’s plays were written for representation. There is indirect proof in the fact that we know that plays were acted at Gandersheim, as at other monasteries, on great occasions, but here is direct evidence. All the fun of *Dulcitius* lies in the action. No dramatist who had not in mind the effect on *spectators* could have conceived the scene in which the foolish governor, black as a sweep from his amorous encounter with the kitchen pots and pans which he mistakes for young women, is chased away from the palace gates, asking the while if there is anything amiss with his fine and handsome appearance. Stage directions, or *didascalia*, are very rarely found in old dramatic texts, but when Magnin compared Roswitha’s original text4 with the first printed edition he found several which had been omitted by Celtes.

===Chinese===
第二部剧《杜尔基提乌斯》结构薄弱，整体而论是诸剧中最乏味的一部。然而它有若干经得起细读的特点。这是罗斯维塔唯一一部显然有意逗笑的剧作，倘若开篇几场的水准能保持到底，它会是一部非常滑稽的宗教闹剧。这里仍是惯见的殉道故事，只是穿插了插科打诨的情节。传统的残忍嗜血的刽子手，换成了滑稽的士兵与滑稽的总督。可惜闹剧的笔调戛然而止，或许是因为罗斯维塔的院长修女认为修女写这般胡闹有失庄重！杜尔基提乌斯这一滑稽角色从剧中突然消失，必有其缘故。然而，即便照现在这个样子，《杜尔基提乌斯》也价值极高，因为它为我们提供了现存最好的证据，证明罗斯维塔的剧作是为上演而写的。间接的证据是：我们知道甘德斯海姆（Gandersheim）同其他修道院一样，逢重大节庆便演剧；而此处却是直接的证据。《杜尔基提乌斯》的全部妙趣尽在动作之中。倘若心中不惦念着对观众的效果，便没有哪个剧作家能构想出这样一场戏：愚蠢的总督把厨房的锅罐瓢盆误认作年轻女子，与之缠绵过后黑得像扫烟囱的，被人从宫门赶走，嘴里还问自己那堂堂的仪表可有什么不妥。舞台指示（didascalia）在古代戏剧文本中极为罕见，但马尼南（Magnin）将罗斯维塔的原始文本4与第一个印刷版本对勘时，发现了几条被策尔蒂斯（Celtes）刊落的舞台指示。

===Original===
*Callimachus*, *Abraham*, and *Paphnutius* precede *Sapientia* in the manuscript, but as the last belongs by reason of its subject to the same group as *Gallicanus* and *Dulcitius*, it is more convenient to discuss it next. It is the best constructed of the “martyrdom” plays, and is singled out for special praise by most of the Roswitha commentators. The final scene in which Sapientia, having buried the bodies of her martyred children outside Rome, lifts up her soul in an ecstatic prayer for death is described by Magnin as “a ray of Sophocles shining through a Christian mind.” Many, however, may find the repetition in the long-drawn-out “torture” scenes monotonous, and the impertinence of Sapientia’s daughters to their imperial persecutor as trying as the real thing must have been. These slips of girls defy “law and order” in the person of the Emperor Hadrian much as in our own day youthful suffragettes used to defy British magistrates. Probably this is in accordance with truth. Roswitha was separated from the days of the first Christians by a shorter space of time than that which separates us from her, and she based her narrative poem about the martyrdom of Saint Pelagius on an account given her by an eyewitness. While modern authors (with the exception of Mr. Bernard Shaw, whose Christian martyrs in *Androcles and the Lion* bear a resemblance to Roswitha’s) love to dwell on the dignity of the early converts to Christianity, Roswitha conveys the impression that the dignity was mingled with impudence.

===Chinese===
在抄本中，《卡利马科斯》（Callimachus）、《亚伯拉罕》（Abraham）与《帕夫努提乌斯》位列《萨皮恩提亚》之前，但因后者就题材而言与《加利卡努斯》和《杜尔基提乌斯》同属一组，紧接着谈它更为便当。它是“殉道”剧中结构最佳的一部，大多数罗斯维塔评论者都对它独加称许。终场一幕，萨皮恩提亚在罗马城外安葬了殉道儿女的遗体之后，在求死的狂喜祷告中将灵魂高高举起——马尼南称此为“一道透过基督徒心灵闪耀的索福克勒斯（Sophocles）之光”。然而许多人也许会觉得冗长的“酷刑”场面重复单调，会觉得萨皮恩提亚的女儿们对帝王迫害者的放肆无礼，同当年实情想必一样令人难堪。这些黄毛少女在哈德良皇帝一人身上挑战“法律与秩序”，恰似近今的青年妇女参政论者挑衅英国的治安法官。这或许正合实情。罗斯维塔距初代基督徒的时代，比我们距她的时代更近，她那部咏圣佩拉焦（Pelagius）殉道的叙事诗，便是依据一位目击者向她提供的记述写成的。现代作家（萧伯纳（Bernard Shaw）先生除外——他的《安德罗克勒斯与狮子》（Androcles and the Lion）中的基督徒殉道者与罗斯维塔笔下的颇为神似）喜欢渲染早期基督徒皈依者的尊严，罗斯维塔给人的印象却是：那尊严之中掺杂着冒失无礼。

===Original===
In *Callimachus*, *Abraham*, and *Paphnutius*, Roswitha sets out to describe the war between the flesh and the spirit, and the long penance which must be done by those who have allowed the flesh to triumph. It is not enough for them to be converted and to realise their crime against the infinite beauty and goodness of God. They are called on to take practical measures to cleanse themselves. *Callimachus* is the first of these plays, and by no means the best, although it timidly sounds a note of passion, rare, if it exists at all, in medieval literature. Some commentators have laboured to establish a resemblance between *Callimachus* and *Romeo and Juliet*, and there are curious parallels. In both you see a sepulchre, a woman’s open grave, and the shroud lifted by the desperate hand of a lover. In both two men come to this tragic scene, bowed down by grief, yet able to control it—in *Romeo and Juliet*, Capulet and Friar Lawrence, in *Callimachus*, the husband of the dead woman and the Apostle John. It would be idle to strain the parallels too far. They might not strike the attention at all if *Callimachus* did not possess a touch of the spirit of *Romeo and Juliet*. It is this which makes the play seem to belong to a later period than the others, and gives it a different character. The passionate language employed, the romance of the story, the colour of the earlier scenes are extraordinary when we remember that the play was written in the 10th century. Haltingly, and apparently without any conscious intention, Roswitha describes the kind of love of which Terence her model knew nothing—that feverish desire absorbing the senses and the soul, which leads to sin or madness or self-slaughter. As if frightened by her own daring (or did the Abbess intervene, as we guess she intervened in *Dulcitius*!), Roswitha spoils the play as a play by a lengthy and tedious final scene in which St. John appears to more advantage as a theologian than as a man.

===Chinese===
在《卡利马科斯》、《亚伯拉罕》与《帕夫努提乌斯》中，罗斯维塔着手描写肉身与灵魂的战争，以及纵容肉身得胜者必须完成的漫长补赎。对他们而言，仅仅皈依、仅仅认识到自己冒犯了上帝无限的美与善，还不够；他们还蒙召采取实际的措施洁净自身。《卡利马科斯》是这几部中的第一部，也绝非最好的一部，尽管它怯生生地奏出一缕激情的音调——这激情在中世纪文学中即便有也属罕见。有些评论者苦心论证《卡利马科斯》与《罗密欧与朱丽叶》（Romeo and Juliet）的相似，两者确有奇特的平行之处。两剧中你都见到一座墓茔、一个敞开的女子坟穴，以及被恋人绝望之手掀开的尸衣。两剧中都有两个男子来到这悲剧现场，悲恸压顶却仍能自持——《罗密欧与朱丽叶》中是凯普莱特（Capulet）与劳伦斯神父（Friar Lawrence），《卡利马科斯》中是亡者的丈夫与使徒约翰（John）。把这些平行处硬扯太远殊为无谓。倘若《卡利马科斯》不具一丝《罗密欧与朱丽叶》的精神，它们也许根本不会惹人注意。正是这一点使此剧看似比其余几部属于更晚的时代，并赋予它别样的品格。想到此剧写于 10 世纪，其中运用的炽烈语言、故事的传奇色彩、前几场的浓淡色调，都属非凡。罗斯维塔踉踉跄跄、而且显然全无自觉意图地，写出了她的典范泰伦提乌斯（Terence）一无所知的那种爱——那种吞没感官与灵魂的炽热欲望，它通向罪愆、疯狂或自戕。仿佛被自己的大胆吓着了（抑或是院长修女插了手，正如我们猜她在《杜尔基提乌斯》中插手一般！），罗斯维塔用一个冗长乏味的终场毁了这出剧之为剧，在那一幕里，圣约翰作为神学家比作为一个人显得更见长。

===Original===
*Abraham* and *Paphnutius* show Roswitha at her best as a dramatist. In both plays the scenes are well knit, the characterization deft and sure, and the dialogue admirably expressive. The opening scenes of *Abraham* reveal that power to suggest character and situation without wordy explanations which is essential in drama. We know at once, although we are not told, that Mary, mere child as she is, is not made of stern stuff, and that her vocation is doubtful. Her replies to the two holy hermits are all that they should be superficially, but through them penetrates a materialism antagonistic to their mystical exaltation. Equally rich in the quality of suggestion is the scene in the house of ill-fame which Abraham visits to rescue his niece from her evil life. She does not recognize him at first, but melancholy seizes her at the supper which it is her duty to enliven by her gaiety. There is the beauty which never ages and appeals to all nations in all times in the following scene, when the hermit, throwing off his worldly disguise, shows his hair grown white through vigils and fasts, and his tonsure, the badge of his thorn-crowned Master, and in words more compassionate than upbraiding moves his lost child to contrition. It is indeed amazing that so true and touching a scene, dealing with a subject which has led later dramatists into false sentiment, coarseness, or mere preaching, should have been written nearly a thousand years ago by an obscure nun in a convent in Lower Saxony.

===Chinese===
《亚伯拉罕》与《帕夫努提乌斯》展现了罗斯维塔作为剧作家的巅峰状态。两剧场次绵密，人物刻画娴熟笃定，对白的表现力令人叹服。《亚伯拉罕》的开场几场显出一种本领：不靠絮絮讲解便能暗示人物与处境——这本是戏剧的命脉所系。虽无人明言，我们立刻便知道，玛丽（Mary）虽还是个孩子，却非刚贞之质，她的圣召令人存疑。她回答两位圣隐修士的话，表面上无可挑剔，字里行间却渗出一种与二老神秘热忱相抵牾的重实之气。同样富于暗示之妙的，是亚伯拉罕为把侄女救出堕落生涯而亲入妓馆的那一场。她起初没有认出他，但在那顿本该由她的欢快来助兴的晚餐上，忧郁攫住了她。接下来的一场自有一种永不衰老、能动一切时代一切民族之心的美：隐修士卸下尘世的伪装，露出因守夜禁食而熬白的头发，露出他的剃发圈——他那荆冠加顶之主的印记——以怜悯多于责备的言语，感得他迷失的孩子痛悔前非。这样真实而动人的一场戏，所处理的题材曾令后世剧作家堕入虚假的感伤、粗鄙或流于说教，而它竟写于近一千年前，出自下萨克森（Lower Saxony）一座修院中一位无名修女之手，实在令人惊叹。

===Original===
Perhaps nothing in *Paphnutius* is on quite the same level of achievement, but a play is not made by a single scene, and *Paphnutius* as a whole is better than *Abraham* as a whole. Few will question that it is Roswitha’s masterpiece. It is very creditable to her that, although the stones of the two plays are similar, she should have shown such variety in the treatment of them. When we compare them we find hardly any repetition. It is interesting to notice that it is not Mary, brought up to the religious life from which she lapses and to which she turns again, who becomes a saint, but Thais, whose life from childhood has been spent in “dangerous delights.” There is a spice of irony in the fact that the penitence of Thais, who had not had Mary’s opportunities, is represented by the dramatist as being on a much higher spiritual plane. With true insight Roswitha makes Paphnutius treat his penitent with far more severity than the hermit Abraham treats Mary. Yet the angelic love of Paphnutius for Thais, thanks to the dramatist’s power of suggestion, penetrates through his austerity, although he never manifests it until the moment when he is assured through the vision of Paul, St. Anthony’s disciple, that the repentance of the sinner has caused that joy in heaven which exceeds all the joy that can be given by the righteous. *Paphnutius* alone among Roswitha’s plays has stood the test of stage representation in modern times,5 and come through it triumphantly, although the miraculous swiftness of Thais’s conversion was considered most “unnatural” by the critics who witnessed the performance.

===Chinese===
《帕夫努提乌斯》中也许没有哪一场达到同等成就，但一出剧并非由单独一场构成，而《帕夫努提乌斯》整体胜过《亚伯拉罕》整体。很少有人会质疑它是罗斯维塔的杰作。两剧情节相仿，她却能处理得如此变化多端，这一点极为她增光。两剧对读，几乎不见重复。耐人寻味的是：成圣的并非玛丽——她自幼受宗教教养，一度堕落，终又回头——而是泰绮丝（Thais），她的一生自童年起便消磨在“危险的欢愉”之中。其间自有一丝反讽：泰绮丝不曾有玛丽那样的机缘，剧作家却将她的补赎表现为居于远高的灵性境界。罗斯维塔以真正的洞见，让帕夫努提乌斯（Paphnutius）对待补赎者的严厉，远甚于隐修士亚伯拉罕对待玛丽。然而，托剧作家暗示之力的福，帕夫努提乌斯对泰绮丝那天使般的爱，仍从他的一派严苛中透射出来，尽管他从不流露，直到那一刻——他借着圣安东尼（St. Anthony）的门徒保罗的异象确知：罪人的悔改已在天上引起了喜乐，这喜乐超过义人所能给出的一切喜乐。在罗斯维塔的剧作中，唯独《帕夫努提乌斯》在现代经受了舞台上演的考验，5并凯旋通过，尽管亲睹演出的评论家们认为泰绮丝皈依得奇迹般迅速，极“不自然”。

===Original===
Roswitha, it must be remembered, believed in miracles. The average Englishman is sceptical. As Mr. Chesterton has pointed out, he will not swear to the possibility of a thing he has not seen, although he is quite ready to swear to the impossibility of a thing he has seen. In the foreword which Mr. Chesterton wrote for the programme of the first performance of *Paphnutius* he compared Roswitha’s treatment of the story of Thais’s conversion with Anatole France’s in his well-known novel *Thais*. “This very strong and moving play (*Paphnutius*) was written by a person about as different from the author of ‘Thais’ as could be capable of wearing the human form, a devout woman, vowed to a restricted life, and writing in the light of a Latin that was gradually going out like a shortening candle. … It is inevitable that such darkness should breed dangerous and even savage things, and that even religion should become almost as fierce as its enemies. … This nun of the Dark Ages wrote without any of that modern comfort and culture which ought, at the very least, to make men kind. When M. Anatole France was the author of ‘Silvestre Bonnard’ it did make him kind. But about Paphnutius and Thais, the harsh ascetic of the hardest times of the 10th century is far kinder than he. In the ‘Thais’ of the great French romancer the whole point is that Thais repents but that Paphnutius relapses. The nun saves both souls. Anatole France loses one of them. That is modern universalism.”

===Chinese===
须记住，罗斯维塔相信奇迹。寻常的英国人则多疑。正如切斯特顿（Chesterton）先生所指出的：凡他没有见过的事，他不肯担保其可能；凡他见过的事，他却随时乐于担保其不可能。切斯特顿先生为《帕夫努提乌斯》首演节目单所撰的前言里，将罗斯维塔对泰绮丝皈依故事的处理，与阿纳托尔·法朗士（Anatole France）名作《泰绮丝》（Thais）中的处理作了比较：“这部极有力量、极动人的剧（《帕夫努提乌斯》）出自这样一个人手笔：她与《泰绮丝》的作者之间差异之大，几乎到了仍能保有人形的地步。一位虔敬的女子，誓度拘限的生活，在一门拉丁文的光照下写作，而那拉丁文正如一支渐短的蜡烛，慢慢熄灭。……这样的黑暗必然孕育危险乃至凶蛮之物，甚至宗教也会变得几乎同它的敌人一样酷烈。……这位黑暗时代的修女，写作时全然没有那种至少本该使人仁厚的现代安逸与教养。阿纳托尔·法朗士先生写《西尔维斯特·博纳尔》（Silvestre Bonnard）时，那教养确曾使他仁厚。但在帕夫努提乌斯与泰绮丝这件事上，这位 10 世纪最严酷时代的苦修者远比他仁厚。在那位伟大的法国传奇小说家的《泰绮丝》里，全部要旨在于泰绮丝悔改了，帕夫努提乌斯却堕落了。修女救回了两个灵魂。阿纳托尔·法朗士丢掉了其中一个。这便是现代的普世主义。”

===Original===
I hope that the publication of these plays in the English language will confirm Roswitha’s right to a high place in medieval literature, and a place also among the few writers of plays which have more than a transitory interest. Perhaps a certain predilection for medieval art is necessary before we can love her wholeheartedly. I do not imagine that those who see no beauty in the primitive art of Cimabue, Giotto, Sana di Pietro, or Lorenzetti will admire the work of a primitive dramatist. But others who find sincere simplicity, as opposed to affected simplicity, a charm in itself, will take Roswitha to their hearts and will have no difficulty in recognizing her merits. In addition to the six plays I have translated the five prefaces printed in Roswitha’s complete works, in the hope that the “strong voice of Gandersheim,” speaking directly to the reader, may win a fresh interest for the plays, and give some idea of the character and attainments of the remarkable woman who wrote them.

===Chinese===
我期望这些剧作以英语问世，将确认罗斯维塔在中世纪文学中据有高位之权，也确认她跻身于那寥寥数位剧作家之列——他们的剧作，其意义不止于一时。或许，须先对中世纪艺术有一定偏爱，方能全心全意地爱她。我不指望那些在契马布埃（Cimabue）、乔托（Giotto）、萨纳·迪·彼得罗（Sana di Pietro）或洛伦泽蒂（Lorenzetti）的原始艺术中见不出美的人，会激赏一位原始派戏剧家的作品。但另一些人，觉得真诚的质朴（有别于矫作的质朴）本身即是一种魅力，他们将把罗斯维塔揽入心怀，也将毫无困难地认识她的长处。除这六部剧外，我还翻译了罗斯维塔全集所刊的五篇序言，希望“甘德斯海姆的强音”直接向读者说话，能为这些剧作赢得一番新的关注，并让人对执笔写出它们的那位杰出女性的品格与造诣，得一概观。

## A Note on the Acting of the Plays / 关于剧本演出的一点说明

===Original===
The evidence that Roswitha’s plays were intended for representation has already been discussed. If they were ever acted in her own time at Gandersheim by members of the community, we need not assume that the performances were ludicrously artless. We have only to read contemporary descriptions of the celebrations of great feasts in monasteries in the so-called “dark ages,” or to observe how strong is the element of significant and controlled “action” in the ceremonial of the Catholic Church as it exists today, to imagine that people accustomed to take part in these dramatic services would have little difficulty in giving an impressive performance of a religious play. Even if we discard the theory that such performances took place, an imaginative conception of what they might have been like will save us, if we desire to act these plays now, from adopting an exaggeratedly primitive method. It is our duty to do our best for them, neglecting no means of emphasizing their dramatic strength and helping their dramatic weakness. As we have no authority in a known “convention” to guide us, the least we can do is to refrain from inventing a comically crude one based on an arrogant condescension to past ignorance of what in any century is dramatically effective.

When *Callimachus* was brought on to the modern stage a misleading impression of Roswitha’s ability as a dramatist was created by a calculated childishness in the interpretation. All the characters were kept in view of the audience whether they were concerned in a scene or not, and the end of each scene was marked, as the end of an over is marked in cricket, by a general change in positions. Roswitha’s piety was held up to ridicule, and her glorification of chastity burlesqued to the satisfaction of those to whom jokes at the expense of old-fashioned virtues never fail to appeal. Drusiana’s prayer that she might die rather than yield to Callimachus was greeted with shouts of laughter. And it was said that the mirth was natural and inevitable because Roswitha’s manner is so naive! Yet if she is treated on her merits, not as an archaic freak, she can be impressive enough on the stage as Edith Craig’s production of *Paphnutius* proved. In this production the abrupt transition from scene to scene was bridged by the singing of plainsong melodies, derived from MSS. of the ninth century. The suggestions for action in the lines were examined with sympathetic insight, and developed with imagination. The actors and actresses took their task seriously and used all their skill in making the characters live. The old story of the conversion of Thais became new, and although many found Roswitha’s treatment of it unpalatable, none found it ludicrous. A comparison of the divergent impressions made by the Roswitha of *Callimachus* and the Roswitha of *Paphnutius* is a lesson in the difficulty of sifting what the dramatist has done from what the interpreter has done, a difficulty all the greater when the text of a play is not available. Now that *Callimachus* can be read it will be easier for those who saw its solitary performance to recognize that it was travestied on the stage.

Imagination, sympathy with Roswitha’s uncompromising religious faith, a few sets of curtains, or an interchangeable scene, actors capable either by nature or training of extracting a pound of effect out of an ounce of dialogue, are the foundations on which performances of these plays can be built. *Paphnutius*, *Abraham*, and *Callimachus* are obviously more actable than the others, but I feel that a great deal might be done with *Sapientia*. Perhaps one day it will be possible to arrange a Roswitha “cycle” for the edification of a few enthusiasts. Meanwhile those who share my belief that plays are not plays until they are acted, can amuse themselves by thinking over different methods of representation.

===Chinese===
罗斯维塔的剧本本为演出而作，相关证据前文已经讨论过。倘若这些剧本在她那个时代确曾在甘德斯海姆由修会成员搬演过，我们也不必假定那些演出拙劣得可笑。我们只需读一读所谓「黑暗时代」修道院盛大节庆的同代记述，或者看一看时至今日天主教会礼仪中那种意味深长而又收放有度的「动作」成分是何等浓厚，便可想见：惯于参与这类富含戏剧性的礼仪的人们，要把一出宗教剧演得动人心魄，并无多大困难。即便我们摈弃这种演出确曾发生过的假说，只要我们今日还想搬演这些剧本，对当年演出可能是什么样子抱一份富于想象的构想，也能使我们免于采用一种夸张到原始程度的手法。为这些剧本尽我们所能，是我们的本分：凡能彰显其戏剧之长、扶助其戏剧之短的手段，皆不可忽略。既然没有一种公认的「程式」可作我们的权威指引，那么我们至少可以做到：不要出于对前人不识戏剧效力之常理的傲慢俯就，去发明一套滑稽粗陋的新程式。

当《卡利马科斯》被搬上现代舞台时，诠释中一种刻意为之的幼稚化，给罗斯维塔作为剧作家的才能造成了错误的印象。所有角色无论是否参与某一场戏，都被一直留在观众眼前；每一场的结束，都以全体演员变换位置的队形来标示，就像板球赛中一轮投球的结束那样。罗斯维塔的虔敬被拿来取笑，她对贞洁的颂扬被滑稽戏仿，以满足那些人——拿老式美德开玩笑的噱头，对他们是永不失灵的妙品。德鲁西娅娜宁死不肯屈从卡利马科斯的祷词，竟迎来哄堂大笑。还有人辩解说，这阵阵笑闹是自然而不可避免的，因为罗斯维塔的笔法如此天真！然而，只要按其实际价值来对待她，而不是把她当成一件古怪的出土旧物，她在舞台上完全可能动人心魄——伊迪丝·克雷格执导的《帕夫努提乌斯》便是明证。在那次演出中，场次之间的突兀转换，用素歌旋律的吟唱来弥缝过渡，那些曲调采自 9 世纪的手抄本。台词中蕴含的动作提示，得到富有同情心的洞察审视，并以想象力加以发挥。男女演员都郑重对待自己的任务，使出全部本领让角色活起来。泰绮丝皈依的古老故事焕然一新；尽管许多人觉得罗斯维塔的处理难以下咽，却没有人觉得它可笑。把《卡利马科斯的罗斯维塔》与《帕夫努提乌斯的罗斯维塔》留下的两种截然相反的印象加以对照，便是一课生动的教训：要从诠释者之所为中筛出剧作家之所为，何其之难；当剧本文本无从得见时，这难处更倍增。如今《卡利马科斯》已经可以读到，当年看过它那唯一一场演出的人，将更容易认清：它在舞台上是被糟蹋了。

想象力，对罗斯维塔那种绝无妥协的宗教信仰的同情体认，几组幕布或一套可换置的景片，再加上一批凭天性或训练便能从一盎司对白中榨出一磅效果的演员——这些便是搬演这些剧本可以倚为根基的东西。《帕夫努提乌斯》、《亚伯拉罕》与《卡利马科斯》显然比其余几部更宜于演出，但我觉得，《萨皮恩提亚》也大有可为。或许有朝一日，能够为少数热心之士编排一套罗斯维塔「连本剧」，使之从中获得教益。与此同时，凡与我同信「剧本不经搬演便不成其为剧本」的人，尽可以在心里细细琢磨各种不同的演出方法，以自娱其间。

## Abraham / 亚伯拉罕

### Argument / 剧情

===Original===
The fall and repentance of Mary, the niece of the hermit Abraham, who, after she has spent twenty years in the religious life as a solitary, abandons it in despair, and, returning to the world, does not shrink from becoming a harlot. But two years later Abraham, in the disguise of a lover, seeks her out and reclaims her. For twenty years she does penance for her sins with many tears, fastings, vigils, and prayers.

===Chinese===
隐修士亚伯拉罕（Abraham）的侄女玛利亚（Mary）的堕落与悔改：她在独修的修道生活中度过二十年后，因绝望而弃之不顾，重返尘世，不惜沦为娼妓。但两年之后，亚伯拉罕乔装成寻欢客寻访到她，将她挽回。此后二十年，她以无数眼泪、斋戒、守夜与祈祷，为自己的罪做补赎。

### Characters / 人物

===Original===
- Abraham
- Ephrem
- Mary
- A friend to Abraham
- An Innkeeper

===Chinese===
- 亚伯拉罕
- 厄弗冷
- 玛利亚
- 亚伯拉罕的一位友人
- 一位客店主人

### Abraham / 亚伯拉罕

#### Scene I / 第一场

===Original===
Abraham
Brother Ephrem, my dear comrade in the hermit life, may I speak to you now, or shall I wait until you have finished your divine praises?

Ephrem
And what can you have to say to me which is not praise of Him Who said: “Where two or three are gathered together in My Name, I am with them”?

Abraham
I have not come to speak of anything which He would not like to hear.

Ephrem
I am sure of it. So speak at once.

Abraham
It concerns a decision I have to make. I long for your approval.

Ephrem
We have one heart and one soul. We ought to agree.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕（Abraham）
厄弗冷（Ephrem）弟兄，我隐修生活中的亲爱同伴，我现在可以同你说话吗，还是该等你颂完神圣的赞歌？

厄弗冷
你所能对我说的，除了赞美那位曾言「哪里有两三个人奉我的名聚集，我就在他们中间」的主之外，还能是什么呢？

亚伯拉罕
我此来要说的话，绝无一句是祂不愿听的。

厄弗冷
我确信如此。那就请即刻说吧。

亚伯拉罕
事关我须作的一个决定。我渴望得到你的赞同。

厄弗冷
我们同心同魂，本该彼此相合。

===Original===
Abraham
I have a little niece of tender years. She has lost both her parents, and my affection for her has been deepened by compassion for her lonely state. I am in constant anxiety on her account.

Ephrem
Ought you who have triumphed over the world to be vexed by its cares!

Abraham
My only care is her radiant beauty! What if it should one day be dimmed by sin.

Ephrem
No one can blame you for being anxious.

Abraham
I hope not.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
我有一个年幼的小侄女。她双亲俱亡，我对她的怜惜之情，又因她孤苦无依而愈发深笃。我为她日夜悬心。

厄弗冷
你既已战胜了世界，岂该再被世界的牵挂所烦扰！

亚伯拉罕
我唯一的牵挂便是她那焕发的美貌！倘有一日它被罪玷污而失色，那可如何是好。

厄弗冷
你为此忧心，无人能责怪你。

亚伯拉罕
但愿如此。

===Original===
Ephrem
How old is she?

Abraham
At the end of this year she will be eight.

Ephrem
She is very young.

Abraham
That does not lessen my anxiety.

Ephrem
Where does she live?

===Chinese===
厄弗冷
她多大了？

亚伯拉罕
到今年年底她就八岁了。

厄弗冷
她还很小。

亚伯拉罕
这并不能减轻我的忧虑。

厄弗冷
她住在哪里？

===Original===
Abraham
At my hermitage now; for at the request of her other kinsfolk I have undertaken to bring her up. The fortune left her ought, I think, to be given to the poor.

Ephrem
A mind taught so early to despise temporal things should be fixed on heaven.

Abraham
I desire with all my heart to see her the spouse of Christ and devoted entirely to His service.

Ephrem
A praiseworthy wish.

Abraham
I was inspired by her name.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
如今住在我的隐修庐；因她其他亲眷之请，我承担了抚养她之责。我想，她名下遗留的财产应当分给穷人。

厄弗冷
一颗这么早便学会轻看尘世之物的心，理当系于上天。

亚伯拉罕
我全心渴望见她成为基督的净配，全然献身于事奉祂。

厄弗冷
可嘉的心愿。

亚伯拉罕
我是受了她的名字的启发。

===Original===
Ephrem
What is she called?

Abraham
Mary.

Ephrem
Mary! Such a name ought to be adorned with the crown of virginity.

Abraham
I have no fear that she will be unwilling, but we must be gentle.

Ephrem
Come, let us go, and impress on her that no life is so sweet and secure as the religious one.

===Chinese===
厄弗冷
她叫什么名字？

亚伯拉罕
玛利亚。

厄弗冷
玛利亚！这样的名字理当戴上童贞的冠冕。

亚伯拉罕
我不怕她不愿意，只是我们须以温柔相待。

厄弗冷
来，我们同去，让她谨记：没有任何生活比修道生活更甘美、更安稳。

#### Scene II / 第二场

===Original===
Abraham
Mary, my child by adoption, whom I love as my own soul! Listen to my advice as to a father’s, and to Brother Ephrem’s as that of a very wise man. Strive to imitate the chastity of the holy Virgin whose name you bear.

Ephrem
Child, would it not be a shame if you, who through the mystery of your name are called to mount to the stars where Mary the mother of God reigns, chose instead the low pleasures of the earth?

Mary
I know nothing about the mystery of my name, so how can I tell what you mean?

Ephrem
Mary, my child, means “star of the sea”—that star which rules the world and all the peoples in the world.

Mary
Why is it called the star of the sea?

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
玛利亚（Mary），我收养的孩子，我爱你就如爱自己的灵魂！请听我的劝告，如同听父亲的；也请听厄弗冷弟兄的劝告，如同听一位极有智慧之人的。要努力效法你所承其名的神圣童贞女的纯洁。

厄弗冷
孩子，你既借你名字的奥秘蒙召，升往群星——上帝之母玛利亚在那里为王——你若转而拣选尘世低贱的逸乐，岂不是奇耻大辱？

玛利亚
我对我名字的奥秘一无所知，又怎能明白您的意思呢？

厄弗冷
玛利亚，我的孩子，意即“海上之星”——那是统辖世界与天下万民的一颗星。

玛利亚
为什么称它为海上之星？

===Original===
Ephrem
Because it never sets, but shines always in the heavens to show mariners their right course.

Mary
And how can such a poor thing as I am—made out of slime, as my uncle says—shine like my name?

Ephrem
By keeping your body unspotted, and your mind pure and holy.

Mary
It would be too great an honour for any human being to become like the stars.

===Chinese===
厄弗冷
因为它永不落下，常在天上照耀，为水手指明正路。

玛利亚
可像我这样卑贱之物——如我叔父所说，是泥污里造出来的——怎能像我名字那样发光呢？

厄弗冷
只要保持你身体无瑕，你心思纯洁神圣。

玛利亚
对任何人来说，变得像星辰一样，都是太大的尊荣。

===Original===
Ephrem
If you choose you can be as the angels of God, and when at last you cast off the burden of this mortal body they will be near you. With them you will pass through the air, and walk on the sky. With them you will sweep round the zodiac, and never slacken your steps until the Virgin’s Son takes you in His arms in His mother’s dazzling bridal room!

Mary
Who but an ass would think little of such happiness! So I choose to despise the things of earth, and deny myself now that I may enjoy it!

Ephrem
Out of the mouths of babes and sucklings! A childish heart, but a mature mind!

===Chinese===
厄弗冷
只要你愿意，你可以像上帝的天使一样；待你最终卸下这必死身体的重担时，他们必在你身旁。你将与他们一同穿越空气，行走于天空；与他们一同环游黄道诸宫，脚步不停，直到童贞女之子在祂母亲那光华夺目的洞房里，将你拥入怀中！

玛利亚
除了蠢驴，谁会小看这样的福分！所以我选择轻看世上之物，现在就克制自己，好叫我得享那福分！

厄弗冷
真是“从婴孩和吃奶的口中”发出了赞美！一颗孩童的心，却有一个成熟的心智！

===Original===
Abraham
God be thanked for it!

Ephrem
Amen to that.

Abraham
But though by God’s grace she has been given the light, at her tender age she must be taught how to use it.

Ephrem
You are right.

Abraham
I will build her a little cell with a narrow entrance near my hermitage. I can visit her there often, and through the window instruct her in the psalter and other pages of the divine law.

Ephrem
That is a good plan.

Mary
I put myself under your direction, Father Ephrem.

Ephrem
My daughter! May the Heavenly Bridegroom to Whom you have given yourself in the tender bud of your youth shield you from the wiles of the devil!

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
为此感谢上帝！

厄弗冷
诚心所愿。

亚伯拉罕
然而她虽蒙上帝恩典得了光照，在这稚龄，还须教她如何运用。

厄弗冷
你说得对。

亚伯拉罕
我要在我的隐修庐旁，为她造一间入口窄小的小室。我可以常去探望她，隔着窗子教她诵读圣咏集（psalter）与神圣律法的其他篇章。

厄弗冷
这是个好主意。

玛利亚
厄弗冷神父，我把自己置于您的指导之下。

厄弗冷
我的女儿！你在青春的嫩芽之际将自己献给的天上新郎，愿祂保守你免遭魔鬼的诡计！

#### Scene III / 第三场

===Original===
Abraham
Brother Ephrem, Brother Ephrem! When anything happens, good or bad, it is to you I turn. It is your counsel I seek. Do not turn your face away, brother—do not be impatient, but help me.

Ephrem
Abraham, Abraham, what has come to you? What is the cause of this immoderate grief? Ought a hermit to weep and groan after the manner of the world?

Abraham
Was any hermit ever so stricken? I cannot bear my sorrow.

Ephrem
Brother, no more of this. To the point; what has happened?

Abraham
Mary! Mary! my adopted child! Mary, whom I cared for so lovingly and taught with all my skill for ten years! Mary—

Ephrem
Well, what is it?

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
厄弗冷弟兄，厄弗冷弟兄！无论何事，或福或祸，我总转向你。我所求的正是你的忠告。弟兄，不要转脸不顾我——不要不耐烦，请帮帮我。

厄弗冷
亚伯拉罕，亚伯拉罕，你怎么了？这般无节制的哀恸，缘何而起？隐修士岂可效法世人的样式哭泣哀号？

亚伯拉罕
何曾有隐修士遭此大难？我的忧伤承当不起。

厄弗冷
弟兄，不要再这样了。说正题；出了什么事？

亚伯拉罕
玛利亚！玛利亚！我收养的孩子！十年来我如此疼爱她、倾尽所能教导她的玛利亚！玛利亚——

厄弗冷
到底怎么了？

===Original===
Abraham
Oh God! She is lost!

Ephrem
Lost? What do you mean?

Abraham
Most miserably. Afterwards she ran away.

Ephrem
But by what wiles did the ancient enemy bring about her undoing?

Abraham
By the wiles of false love. Dressed in a monk’s habit, the hypocrite went to see her often. He succeeded in making the poor ignorant child love him. She leapt from the window of her cell for an evil deed.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
哦上帝！她完了！

厄弗冷
完了？你是什么意思？

亚伯拉罕
惨极了。后来她逃走了。

厄弗冷
可那亘古的仇敌是用什么诡计害她堕落的？

亚伯拉罕
用假爱情的诡计。那伪君子身披修士会衣，频频前去见她。他终于使那可怜无知的孩子爱上了他。她为着一桩罪恶的勾当，从小室的窗口跳了出去。

===Original===
Ephrem
I shudder as I listen to you.

Abraham
When the unhappy girl knew that she was ruined, she beat her breast and dug her nails into her face. She tore her garments, pulled out her hair. Her despairing cries were terrible to hear.

Ephrem
I am not surprised. For such a fall a whole fountain of tears should rise.

Abraham
She moaned out that she could never be the same—

Ephrem
Poor, miserable girl!

===Chinese===
厄弗冷
我听着便不寒而栗。

亚伯拉罕
那不幸的姑娘知道自己毁了之后，捶打胸膛，指甲抠进脸庞。她撕裂衣裳，扯下头发。她绝望的哀号惨不忍闻。

厄弗冷
我不意外。遭此堕落，当涌出一整泉眼泪。

亚伯拉罕
她呻吟着说，她再也不能像从前一样了——

厄弗冷
可怜、悲惨的姑娘！

===Original===
Abraham
And reproached herself for having forgotten our warning.

Ephrem
She might well do so.

Abraham
She cried that all her vigils, prayers, and fasts had been thrown away.

Ephrem
If she perseveres in this penitence she will be saved.

Abraham
She has not persevered. She has added worse to her evil deed.

Ephrem
Oh, this moves me to the depths of my heart!

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
又责备自己忘了我们的训诫。

厄弗冷
她理当如此。

亚伯拉罕
她哭喊说，她一切的守夜、祈祷与斋戒都付诸东流了。

厄弗冷
她若持守这份悔改，必能得救。

亚伯拉罕
她没有持守。她在恶行之上又添了更糟的。

厄弗冷
哦，这叫我心深深震动！

===Original===
Abraham
After all these tears and lamentations she was overcome by remorse, and fell headlong into the abyss of despair.

Ephrem
A bitter business!

Abraham
She despaired of being able to win pardon, and resolved to go back to the world and its vanities.

Ephrem
I cannot remember when the devil could boast of such a triumph over the hermits.

Abraham
Now we are at the mercy of the demons.

Ephrem
I marvel that she could have escaped without your knowledge.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
在这一切眼泪与哀叹之后，她被懊悔压倒，一头栽进了绝望的深渊。

厄弗冷
苦事啊！

亚伯拉罕
她绝望于能得到宽赦，便决意回到世界和它的虚浮中去。

厄弗冷
我不记得魔鬼何曾能在隐修士身上夸耀这样的胜利。

亚伯拉罕
如今我们任凭魔鬼摆布了。

厄弗冷
她竟能在你毫不知情中逃走，令我称奇。

===Original===
Abraham
If I had not been so blind! I ought to have paid more heed to that terrible vision. Yes, I see now that it was sent to warn me.

Ephrem
What vision?

Abraham
I dreamed I was standing at the door of my cell, and that a huge dragon with a loathsome stench rushed violently towards me. I saw that the creature was attracted by a little white dove at my side. It pounced on the dove, devoured it, and vanished.

Ephrem
There is no doubt what this vision meant.

Abraham
When I woke I turned over in my mind what I had seen, and took it as a sign of some persecution threatening the Church, through which many of the faithful would be drawn into error. I prostrated myself in prayer, and implored Him Who knows the future to enlighten me.

Ephrem
You did right.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
我若不是那样瞎眼就好了！我本该更留心那可怕的异象。是的，我如今明白，那是派来警诫我的。

厄弗冷
什么异象？

亚伯拉罕
我梦见自己站在小室门口，一条巨龙带着令人作呕的恶臭向我猛扑过来。我看见那怪兽是被我身旁一只小白鸽引来的。它扑向鸽子，将它吞吃，随即消失。

厄弗冷
这异象的含义毋庸置疑。

亚伯拉罕
醒来后我反复思量所见之景，把它当作某种迫害将临教会的征兆，许多信众将由此被引入歧途。我俯伏祈祷，恳求那知道未来的主光照我。

厄弗冷
你做得对。

===Original===
Abraham
On the third night after the vision, when for weariness I had fallen asleep, I saw the beast again, but now it was lying dead at my feet, and the dove was flying heavenwards safe and unhurt.

Ephrem
I am rejoiced to hear this, for to my thinking it means that some day Mary will return to you.

Abraham
I was trying to get rid of the uneasiness with which the first vision had filled me by thinking of the second, when my little pupil in her cell came to my mind. I remembered, although at the time I was not alarmed, that for two days I had not heard her chanting the divine praises.

Ephrem
You were too tardy in noticing this.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
异象之后的第三个夜晚，我因疲惫沉睡过去，又见了那兽，只是这一次它已横死在我脚前，那鸽子正向天上飞去，安然无伤。

厄弗冷
听闻此讯我甚欣慰，因为依我之见，这意味着玛利亚终有一日会回到你身边。

亚伯拉罕
我正想借思想第二个异象，排遣第一个异象带给我的不安，忽然想起小室里我那小小的学生。我记起——虽然当时并未惊觉——我已有两天没听见她诵唱神圣的赞歌了。

厄弗冷
你察觉得太迟了。

===Original===
Abraham
I admit it. I went at once to her cell, and, knocking at the window, I called her again and again, “Mary! My child! Mary!”

Ephrem
You called in vain?

Abraham
“Mary,” I said. “Mary, my child, what is wrong? Why are you not saying your office?” It was only when I did not hear the faintest sound that I suspected.

Ephrem
What did you do then?

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
我承认。我立刻去到她的小室，敲着窗，一遍又一遍地唤她：“玛利亚！我的孩子！玛利亚！”

厄弗冷
你唤了也是徒然？

亚伯拉罕
“玛利亚，”我说，“玛利亚，我的孩子，怎么了？你为何不诵你的日课？”直到连最微弱的声息也听不见，我才起了疑心。

厄弗冷
那你怎么办？

===Original===
Abraham
When I could no longer doubt that she had gone, I was struck with fear to my very bowels. I trembled in every limb.

Ephrem
I do not wonder, since I, hearing of it, find myself trembling all over.

Abraham
Then I wept and cried out to the empty air, “What wolf has seized my lamb? What thief has stolen my little daughter?”

Ephrem
You had good cause to weep! To lose her whom you had cherished so tenderly!

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
当我再不能怀疑她已经离去时，恐惧直透我的脏腑。我四肢战栗。

厄弗冷
这我并不奇怪，因为我仅是听你说起，便已浑身发抖。

亚伯拉罕
于是我哭泣，向着空茫呼喊：“是什么豺狼叼走了我的羔羊？是什么盗贼偷走了我的小女儿？”

厄弗冷
你大有理由哭泣！失去你如此温柔珍爱的她！

===Original===
Abraham
At last some people came up who knew what had happened. From them I learned that she had gone back to the world.

Ephrem
Where is she now?

Abraham
No one knows.

Ephrem
What is to be done?

Abraham
I have a faithful friend, who is searching all the cities and towns in the country. He says he will never give up until he finds her.

Ephrem
And if he finds her—what then?

Abraham
Then I shall change these clothes, and in the guise of a worldling seek her out. It may be that she will heed what I say, and even after this shipwreck turn again to the harbour of her innocence and peace.

Ephrem
And suppose that in the world they offer you flesh meat and wine?

Abraham
If they do, I shall not refuse; otherwise I might be recognized.

Ephrem
No one will blame you, brother. It will be but praiseworthy discretion on your part to loosen the bridle of strict observance for the sake of bringing back a soul.

Abraham
I am the more eager to try now I know you approve.

Ephrem
He Who knows the secret places of the heart can tell with what motive every action is done. That scrupulous and fair Judge will not condemn a man for relaxing our strict rule for a time and descending to the level of weaker mortals if by so doing he can make more sure of rescuing an errant soul.

Abraham
Help me with your prayers. Pray that I may not be caught in the snares of the devil.

Ephrem
May He Who is supreme good itself, without Whom no good thing can be done, bless your enterprise and bring it to a happy end!

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
终于有些人走上前来，知道发生了何事。我才从他们口中得知，她已回到世上去了。

厄弗冷
她如今在哪里？

亚伯拉罕
无人知晓。

厄弗冷
如今该怎么办？

亚伯拉罕
我有一位忠实的友人，正寻遍全国的城镇。他说找不到她决不罢休。

厄弗冷
若他找到了她——然后呢？

亚伯拉罕
那时我便换下这身衣裳，以俗人的装束去寻访她。或许她会听我的话，纵然遭此船难，仍能回转到她清白与安宁的港湾。

厄弗冷
倘若在世上，人家以肉食和酒款待你呢？

亚伯拉罕
若真如此，我也不推辞；否则我怕被人认出来。

厄弗冷
弟兄，无人会责怪你。为挽回一个灵魂而稍松严规的缰绳，在你反而是可嘉的审慎。

亚伯拉罕
既知你赞同，我愈发急欲一试。

厄弗冷
那洞悉心灵隐密处的主，知晓每个行为背后的动机。那位明察而公义的审判者，必不定罪一个为更稳妥地抢救迷途灵魂，而暂时放宽我们严规、屈就软弱凡人之地步的人。

亚伯拉罕
请以祈祷助我。求你祈祷，愿我不致落入魔鬼的网罗。

厄弗冷
愿那自身即是至善、离了祂便无善可行的主，祝福你的事业，赐它圆满结局！

#### Scene IV / 第四场

===Original===
Abraham
Can that be my friend who two years ago went to search for Mary? Yes, it is he!

Friend
Good day, venerable father.

Abraham
Good day, dear friend. I have waited so long for you. Of late I had begun to despair.

Friend
Forgive me, father. I delayed my return because I did not wish to mock you with doubtful and unreliable news. As soon as I had discovered the truth I lost no time.

Abraham
You have seen Mary?

Friend
I have seen her.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
那会是我那位两年前去寻找玛利亚的友人吗？正是他！

友人（Friend）
日安，可敬的神父。

亚伯拉罕
日安，亲爱的朋友。我等你等了这么久。近来我已开始绝望了。

友人
神父，请宽恕我。我迟迟未归，是因为不愿拿可疑而不可靠的消息戏弄您。一查明真相，我便一刻不误地赶回来了。

亚伯拉罕
你见到玛利亚了？

友人
见到了。

===Original===
Abraham
Where is she? Come, sir, speak! Tell me where.

Friend
It goes to my heart to tell you.

Abraham
Speak—I implore you.

Friend
She lives in the house of a man who trades in the love of young girls like her. A profitable business, for every day he makes a large sum of money out of her lovers.

Abraham
Her lovers? Mary’s lovers?

Friend
Yes.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
她在哪里？说吧，先生，快说！告诉我她在哪里。

友人
说出来实在令我心痛。

亚伯拉罕
说吧——我求你。

友人
她住在一个靠做年轻姑娘爱情买卖营利的男人家里。这可是桩好生意，他每天从她的恩客身上赚得一大笔钱。

亚伯拉罕
她的恩客？玛利亚的恩客？

友人
是的。

===Original===
Abraham
Who are they?

Friend
There are plenty of them.

Abraham
Good Jesu, what is this monstrous thing I hear? Do they say that she, whom I brought up to be Thy bride, gives herself to strange lovers?

Friend
It comes naturally to harlots.

Abraham
If you are my friend, get me a saddle-horse somewhere and a soldier’s dress. I am going to get into that place as a lover.

Friend
Father, mine are at your service.

Abraham
And I must borrow a felt hat to cover my tonsure.

Friend
That is most necessary, if you do not want to be recognized.

Abraham
I have one gold piece. Should I take it to give this man?

Friend
You should, for otherwise he will never let you see Mary.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
都是些什么人？

友人
多得是。

亚伯拉罕
仁善的耶稣啊，我听闻的是何等骇人之事？人们说，这个我养育为祢净配的女子，竟把自己交给陌生的恩客？

友人
娼妓本性如此。

亚伯拉罕
你若真是我的朋友，就替我设法弄一匹鞍马和一身士兵衣裳。我要装作一个寻欢客混进那地方。

友人
神父，我的马和衣裳任凭您用。

亚伯拉罕
我还须借一顶毡帽，遮住我剃度的头顶。

友人
那确是万不可少，倘若您不想被人认出。

亚伯拉罕
我有一枚金币。要不要带上，送给那男人？

友人
要带上，否则他绝不让您见玛利亚。

#### Scene V / 第五场

===Original===
Abraham
Good day, friend.

Innkeeper
Who’s there? Good day, Sir. Come in!

Abraham
Have you a bed for a traveller who wants to spend a night here?

Innkeeper
Why certainly! I never turn anyone away.

Abraham
I am glad of it.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
日安，朋友。

客店主人（Innkeeper）
是哪位？日安，先生。请进！

亚伯拉罕
可有床位，给一位想在此过夜的旅客？

客店主人
那自然有！我从不拒客。

亚伯拉罕
那就好。

===Original===
Innkeeper
Come in then, and I will order supper for you.

Abraham
I owe you thanks for this kind welcome, but I have a greater favour to ask.

Innkeeper
Ask what you like. I will do my best for you.

Abraham
Accept this small present. May the beautiful girl who, I am told, lives here, have supper with me?

Innkeeper
Why should you wish to see her?

===Chinese===
客店主人
那就请进，我这就吩咐给您备晚膳。

亚伯拉罕
多谢你这般殷勤接待，只是我还有一件更大的事相求。

客店主人
但求无妨。我必尽力为您办到。

亚伯拉罕
请收下这点小礼。听闻住在这里的那位美丽姑娘，可否陪我共进晚膳？

客店主人
您为何想见她？

===Original===
Abraham
It would give me much pleasure. I have heard so much talk of her beauty.

Innkeeper
Whoever has spoken to you of her has told only the truth. It would be hard to find a finer wench.

Abraham
I am in love with her already.

Innkeeper
It’s queer that an old man like you should dangle after a young girl.

Abraham
I swear I came here on purpose to feast my eyes on her.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
那会令我大为欢悦。她的美貌，我已听得太多了。

客店主人
向您说起她的人，说的句句是实话。要找比她更标致的妞儿，可难了。

亚伯拉罕
我已经恋上她了。

客店主人
真稀奇，您这把年纪的老先生，竟追逐起年轻姑娘来了。

亚伯拉罕
我发誓，我此来专为饱一饱眼福。

#### Scene VI / 第六场

===Original===
Innkeeper
Mary, come here! Come along now and show off your charms to this young innocent!

Mary
I am coming.

Abraham
Oh, mind, be constant! Tears, do not fall! Must I look on her whom I brought up in the desert, decked out with a harlot’s face? Yes, I must hide what is in my heart. I must strive not to weep, and smile though my heart is breaking.

Innkeeper
Luck comes your way, Mary! Not only do young gallants of your own age flock to your arms, but even the wise and venerable!

Mary
It is all one to me. It is my business to love those who love me.

===Chinese===
客店主人
玛利亚，过来！来，在这位天真的年轻客人面前显显你的风情！

玛利亚
这就来。

亚伯拉罕
哦，心啊，要撑住！眼泪，切莫落下！我竟要眼看着我在旷野中养大的她，敷着一张娼妓的面容？是的，我必须藏起心中所藏。我必须强忍不哭，纵然心碎，也要面带笑容。

客店主人
玛利亚，好运临头啦！投怀送抱的不仅有你同龄的年轻风流客，连睿智可敬的老先生也来了！

玛利亚
对我来说全都一样。爱那些爱我的人，便是我的营生。

===Original===
Abraham
Come nearer, Mary, and give me a kiss.

Mary
I will give you more than a kiss. I will take your head in my arms and stroke your neck.

Abraham
Yes, like that!

Mary
What does this mean? What is this lovely fragrance. So clean, so sweet. It reminds me of the time when I was good.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
再靠近些，玛利亚，给我一个吻。

玛利亚
我给你的不止一个吻。我要把您的头搂在怀里，抚弄您的脖颈。

亚伯拉罕
对，就是这样！

玛利亚
这是怎么回事？这可爱的芬芳是什么？如此洁净，如此甘美。它让我想起我还是好人的时光。

===Original===
Abraham
On with the mask! Chatter, make lewd jests like an idle boy! She must not recognize me, or for very shame she may fly from me.

Mary
Wretch that I am! To what have I fallen! In what pit am I sunk!

Abraham
You forget where you are! Do men come here to see you cry!

Innkeeper
What’s the matter, Lady Mary? Why are you in the dumps? You have lived here two years, and never before have I seen a tear, never heard a sigh or a word of complaint.

Mary
Oh, that I had died three years ago before I came to this!

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
快戴上面具！闲扯吧，像个浪荡子一样说些狎昵的笑话！千万不能让她认出我，否则她羞愧之下可能会从我面前逃走。

玛利亚
我这可怜虫！我堕落到何等地步！我陷在怎样的泥坑里！

亚伯拉罕
你忘了这是什么地方！男人来这里难道是看你哭的！

客店主人
怎么了，玛利亚姑娘？为何闷闷不乐？你在这里住了两年，我从未见过你一滴泪，从未听过你一声叹息或半句怨言。

玛利亚
唉，我三年前就死了多好，何至于落到这步田地！

===Original===
Abraham
I came here to make love to you, not to weep with you over your sins.

Mary
A little thing moved me, and I spoke foolishly. It is nothing. Come, let us eat and drink and be merry, for, as you say, this is not the place to think of one’s sins.

Abraham
I have eaten and drunk enough, thanks to your good table, Sir. Now by your leave I will go to bed. My tired limbs need a rest.

Innkeeper
As you please.

Mary
Get up my lord. I will take you to bed.

Abraham
I hope so. I would not go at all unless you came with me.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
我来此是为向你求欢，不是为陪你哭诉你的罪。

玛利亚
一点小事触动了我，才说了蠢话。没什么。来，我们吃喝快乐吧，正如您所说，这里不是想念罪过的地方。

亚伯拉罕
承蒙贵店盛馔，先生，我已酒足饭饱。现在请恕我告退就寝。我疲乏的四肢需要歇息了。

客店主人
请便。

玛利亚
起来吧，大人。我陪您上床。

亚伯拉罕
正合我意。你若不陪我去，我根本就不去了。

#### Scene VII / 第七场

===Original===
Mary
Look! How do you like this room? A handsome bed, isn’t it? Those trappings cost a lot of money. Sit down and I will take off your shoes. You seem tired.

Abraham
First bolt the door. Someone may come in.

Mary
Have no fear. I have seen to that.

Abraham
The time has come for me to show my shaven head, and make myself known! Oh, my daughter! Oh, Mary, you who are part of my soul! Look at me. Do you not know me? Do you not know the old man who cherished you with a father’s love, and wedded you to the Son of the King of Heaven?

Mary
God, what shall I do! It is my father and master Abraham!

Abraham
What has come to you, daughter?

===Chinese===
玛利亚
看！这房间您中意吗？一张漂亮的床，不是吗？那些帷帐花了不少钱呢。请坐，我来替您脱鞋。您看来累了。

亚伯拉罕
先闩上门。说不定有人进来。

玛利亚
别怕。我已照管好了。

亚伯拉罕
到了我露出剃发头顶、表明身份的时候了！啊，我的女儿！啊，玛利亚，你是我灵魂的一部分！看着我。你不认得我了吗？你不认得这位以父亲之爱珍爱你、将你许配给天上君王之子的老人了吗？

玛利亚
上帝啊，我该怎么办！是我的父亲、我的师尊亚伯拉罕！

亚伯拉罕
你怎么了，女儿？

===Original===
Mary
Oh, misery!

Abraham
Who deceived you? Who led you astray?

Mary
Who deceived our first parents?

Abraham
Have you forgotten that once you lived like an angel on earth!

Mary
All that is over.

Abraham
What has become of your virginal modesty? Your beautiful purity?

Mary
Lost. Gone!

===Chinese===
玛利亚
哦，苦啊！

亚伯拉罕
谁欺哄了你？谁引你入了歧途？

玛利亚
谁欺哄了我们的始祖呢？

亚伯拉罕
你忘了你曾像世上的天使一样生活吗！

玛利亚
一切都过去了。

亚伯拉罕
你童贞的羞怯到哪里去了？你美好的纯洁呢？

玛利亚
丢了。没了！

===Original===
Abraham
Oh, Mary, think what you have thrown away! Think what a reward you had earned by your fasting, and prayers, and vigils. What can they avail you now! You have hurled yourself from heavenly heights into the depths of hell!

Mary
Oh God, I know it!

Abraham
Could you not trust me? Why did you desert me? Why did you not tell me of your fall? Then dear brother Ephrem and I could have done a worthy penance.

Mary
Once I had committed that sin, and was defiled, how could I dare come near you who are so holy?

Abraham
Oh, Mary, has anyone ever lived on earth without sin except the Virgin’s Son?

Mary
No one, I know.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
啊，玛利亚，想想你抛弃了什么！想想你以斋戒、祈祷与守夜赚得了怎样的赏报。它们如今于你还有何用！你已把自己从天庭之巅掷进了地狱的深渊！

玛利亚
哦上帝，我知道！

亚伯拉罕
你竟不能信靠我吗？你为何离弃我？你为何不把你的堕落告诉我？那样，亲爱的厄弗冷弟兄与我便能做一场相称的补赎。

玛利亚
我既犯了那罪、已被玷污，又怎敢亲近您这样圣洁的人？

亚伯拉罕
啊，玛利亚，除了童贞女之子，世上可有人活着不犯罪？

玛利亚
据我所知，没有。

===Original===
Abraham
It is human to sin, but it is devilish to remain in sin. Who can be justly condemned? Not those who fall suddenly, but those who refuse to rise quickly.

Mary
Wretched, miserable creature that I am!

Abraham
Why have you thrown yourself down there? Why do you lie on the ground without moving or speaking? Get up, Mary! Get up, my child, and listen to me!

Mary
No! no! I am afraid. I cannot bear your reproaches.

Abraham
Remember how I love you, and you will not be afraid.

Mary
It is useless. I cannot.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
犯罪是人，滞留罪中却是魔。谁当受公义的定罪？不是那些骤然跌倒的，而是那些拒不起身的。

玛利亚
我这可悲、可怜的受造之物！

亚伯拉罕
你为何扑倒在那里？为何一动不动、一言不发地伏在地上？起来，玛利亚！起来，我的孩子，听我说！

玛利亚
不！不！我怕。我受不住您的责备。

亚伯拉罕
想想我是何等爱你，你就不会害怕了。

玛利亚
没有用的。我不能。

===Original===
Abraham
What but love for you could have made me leave the desert and relax the strict observance of our rule? What but love could have made me, a true hermit, come into the city and mix with the lascivious crowd? It is for your sake that these lips have learned to utter light, foolish words, so that I might not be known! Oh, Mary, why do you turn away your face from me and gaze upon the ground? Why do you scorn to answer and tell me what is in your mind.

Mary
It is the thought of my sins which crushes me. I dare not look at you; I am not fit to speak to you.

Abraham
My little one, have no fear. Oh, do not despair! Rise from this abyss of desperation and grapple God to your soul!

Mary
No, no! My sins are too great. They weigh me down.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
若不是对你的爱，还有什么能使我离开旷野、放宽我们严规的持守？若不是爱，还有什么能使我这真正的隐修士进城混迹于淫荡的人群？为了你，这双嘴唇才学会了说轻佻蠢笨的话，好叫人不认出我！啊，玛利亚，你为何把脸从我面前转开，双眼只盯着地面？你为何不屑回答，把心里的话告诉我？

玛利亚
是对我罪孽的思念压垮了我。我不敢看您；我不配同您说话。

亚伯拉罕
我的小人儿，不要害怕。啊，不要绝望！从这绝望的深渊里站起来，把上帝紧紧抱在你的灵魂上！

玛利亚
不，不！我的罪太重了。它们压得我沉下去。

===Original===
Abraham
The mercy of heaven is greater than you or your sins. Let your sadness be dispersed by its glorious beams. Oh, Mary, do not let apathy prevent your seizing the moment for repentance. It matters not how wickedness has flourished. Divine grace can flourish still more abundantly!

Mary
If there were the smallest hope of forgiveness, surely I should not shrink from doing penance.

Abraham
Have you no pity for me? I have sought you out with so much pain and weariness! Oh shake off this despair which we are taught is the most terrible of all sins. Despair of God’s mercy—for that alone there is no forgiveness. Sin can no more embitter His sweet mercy than a spark from a flint can set the ocean on fire.

Mary
I know that God’s mercy is great, but when I think how greatly I have sinned, I cannot believe any penance can make amends.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
上天的仁慈大过你，也大过你的罪。让你的悲伤被它荣耀的光芒驱散吧。啊，玛利亚，莫让麻木拦阻你抓住悔改的时机。恶行如何猖獗都不要紧。神圣的恩宠能更加丰盛地茂长！

玛利亚
哪怕有一丝宽赦的希望，我也必不逃避补赎。

亚伯拉罕
你就不怜恤我吗？我寻你寻得何等辛劳疲惫！啊，甩掉这绝望吧——我们受教得知，它是一切罪中最可怕的。绝望于上帝的仁慈——唯独这一样不得宽赦。罪不能使祂甘甜的仁慈变苦，正如燧石上迸出的火星点不燃大海。

玛利亚
我知道上帝的仁慈浩大，可我一想到自己罪孽何等深重，便不能相信任何补赎能够弥补。

===Original===
Abraham
I will take your sins on me. Only come back and take up your life again as if you had never left it.

Mary
I do not want to oppose you. What you tell me to do I will do with all my heart.

Abraham
My daughter lives again! I have found my lost lamb and she is dearer to me than ever.

Mary
I have a few possessions here—a little gold and some clothes. What ought I to do with them?

Abraham
What came to you through sin, with sin must be left behind.

Mary
Could it not be given to the poor, or sold for an offering at the holy altar?

Abraham
The price of sin is not an acceptable offering to God.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
我愿担当你的罪。你只管回来，重拾你的生活，仿佛从未离开过一样。

玛利亚
我不愿违背您。您吩咐我做什么，我都全心去做。

亚伯拉罕
我的女儿又活了！我寻回了迷失的羔羊，她比从前更亲了。

玛利亚
我在这里还有些财物——一点金子和几件衣裳。该怎么处置它们？

亚伯拉罕
从罪而来的，须随罪一同撇下。

玛利亚
不能分给穷人，或变卖了在神圣的祭坛前献作供品吗？

亚伯拉罕
罪的代价，不是上帝悦纳的祭品。

===Original===
Mary
Then I will not trouble any more about my possessions.

Abraham
Look! The dawn! It is growing light. Let us go.

Mary
You go first, dearest father, like the good shepherd leading the lost lamb that has been found. The lamb will follow in your steps.

Abraham
Not so! I am going on foot, but you—you shall have a horse so that the stony road shall not hurt your delicate feet.

Mary
Oh, let me never forget this tenderness! Let me try all my life to thank you! I was not worth pity, yet you have shown me no harshness; you have led me to repent not by threats but by gentleness and love.

===Chinese===
玛利亚
那我再也不为这些财物挂心了。

亚伯拉罕
看！天亮了！晨光渐明。我们走吧。

玛利亚
您先行，最亲爱的父亲，如好牧人引领寻回的迷途羔羊。羔羊必跟着您的脚步行走。

亚伯拉罕
不可这样！我步行便是，你——你要骑马，免得碎石硌伤你娇嫩的双足。

玛利亚
啊，愿我永不忘记这份慈柔！愿我毕生竭力报答您！我本不值得怜恤，您却不曾对我疾言厉色；您引导我悔改，不是靠威吓，而是靠温柔与爱。

===Original===
Abraham
I ask only one thing, Mary. Be faithful to God for the rest of your life.

Mary
With all my strength I will persevere, and though my flesh may fail, my spirit never will.

Abraham
You must serve God with as much energy as you have served the world.

Mary
If His will is made perfect in me it will be because of your merits.

Abraham
Come, let us hasten on our way.

Mary
Yes, let us set out at once. I would not stay here another moment.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
我只求一件事，玛利亚。此生余下的岁月，对上帝忠贞不渝。

玛利亚
我必倾尽全力坚持到底，纵然肉体衰败，我的灵决不退缩。

亚伯拉罕
你事奉上帝，须拿出你事奉世界时的那股劲来。

玛利亚
祂的旨意若在我身上成全，那是靠您的功行。

亚伯拉罕
来，我们快上路吧。

玛利亚
好，我们即刻启程。这地方我一刻也不想多留。

#### Scene VIII / 第八场

===Original===
Abraham
Courage, Mary! You see how swiftly we have made the difficult and toilsome journey.

Mary
Everything is easy when we put our hearts into it.

Abraham
There is your deserted little cell.

Mary
God help me! It was the witness of my sin. I dare not go there.

Abraham
It is natural you should dread the place where the enemy triumphed.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
鼓起勇气，玛利亚！你看，这段艰辛劳苦的路，我们走得多快。

玛利亚
只要全心去做，什么事都容易。

亚伯拉罕
那就是你荒废的小室。

玛利亚
上帝助我！它是我的罪的见证。我不敢进去。

亚伯拉罕
你惧怕仇敌得胜之地，这是人之常情。

===Original===
Mary
Where, then, am I to do penance?

Abraham
Go into the inner cell. There you will be safe from the wiles of the serpent.

Mary
Most gladly as it is your wish.

Abraham
Now I must go to my good friend Ephrem. He alone mourned with me when you were lost, and he must rejoice with me now that you have been found.

Mary
Of course.

===Chinese===
玛利亚
那我该在哪里做补赎呢？

亚伯拉罕
进里面的小室去。在那里你可免受古蛇的诡计。

玛利亚
既是您的意愿，我甘愿之至。

亚伯拉罕
如今我须去见我的好友厄弗冷。你迷失时，唯他与我同哀；你寻回了，他也必与我同乐。

玛利亚
那是自然。

#### Scene IX / 第九场

===Original===
Ephrem
Well, brother! If I am not mistaken, you bring good news.

Abraham
The best in the world.

Ephrem
You have found your lost lamb?

Abraham
I have, and, rejoicing, have brought her back to the fold.

Ephrem
Truly this is the work of divine grace.

Abraham
That is certain.

Ephrem
How is she spending her days? I should like to know how you have ordered her life. What does she do?

===Chinese===
厄弗冷
哎呀，弟兄！若我没有看错，你带来了好消息。

亚伯拉罕
天下最好的消息。

厄弗冷
你寻回迷失的羔羊了？

亚伯拉罕
寻回了，并且欢欢喜喜把她带回了羊栈。

厄弗冷
这实在是神圣恩宠的作为。

亚伯拉罕
确然无疑。

厄弗冷
她这些日子怎么过？我想知道你如何安排她的生活。她做些什么？

===Original===
Abraham
All that I tell her.

Ephrem
That is well.

Abraham
Nothing is too difficult for her—nothing too hard. She is ready to endure anything.

Ephrem
That is better.

Abraham
She wears a hair shirt, and subdues her flesh with continual vigils and fasts. She is making the poor frail body obey the spirit by the most rigorous discipline.

Ephrem
Only through such a severe penance can the stains left by the pleasures of the flesh be washed away.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
凡我吩咐的，她都照行。

厄弗冷
这就好了。

亚伯拉罕
没有什么对她太难——没有什么太苦。任何事她都甘愿承受。

厄弗冷
那就更好了。

亚伯拉罕
她身穿苦衣，以不断的守夜和斋戒克制肉体。她以最严苛的苦修，使这可怜脆弱的身体顺服于灵魂。

厄弗冷
唯有借着这般严厉的补赎，肉身逸乐留下的污痕才能洗净。

===Original===
Abraham
Those who hear her sobs are cut to the heart, and the tale of her repentance has turned many from their sins.

Ephrem
It is often so.

Abraham
She prays continually for the men who through her were tempted to sin, and begs that she who was their ruin may be their salvation.

Ephrem
It is right that she should do this.

Abraham
She strives to make her life as beautiful as for a time it was hideous.

Ephrem
I rejoice at what you tell me. To the depths of my heart.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
听见她呜咽的人无不痛彻心肺，她悔改的传闻已使许多人转离罪恶。

厄弗冷
事情往往如此。

亚伯拉罕
她不断为那些因她而受诱犯罪的人祈祷，恳求那曾毁灭他们的她，能成为他们的救恩。

厄弗冷
她这样做是理所应当的。

亚伯拉罕
她正竭力使自己的生活，变得如它一度那般丑陋一样美丽。

厄弗冷
你所讲的使我欢喜。欢喜到心底。

===Original===
Abraham
And with us rejoice phalanxes of angels, praising the Lord for the conversion of a sinner.

Ephrem
Over whom, we are told, there is more joy in heaven than over the just man who needs no penance.

Abraham
The more glory to Him, because there seemed no hope on earth that she could be saved.

Ephrem
Let us sing a song of thanksgiving—let us glorify the only begotten Son of God, Who of His love and mercy will not let them perish whom He redeemed with His holy blood.

Abraham
To Him be honour, glory, and praise through infinite ages. Amen.

===Chinese===
亚伯拉罕
与我们同乐的，还有列队的天使，他们为一个罪人的归正赞美主。

厄弗冷
我们受教得知，天上为一个罪人欢喜，比为那无需补赎的义人欢喜更大。

亚伯拉罕
愿祂更得荣耀，因为地上本已无她可得救的指望。

厄弗冷
让我们同唱感恩之歌——让我们荣耀上帝的独生子，祂以祂的仁爱与慈悲，绝不容祂用圣血所救赎的人灭亡。

亚伯拉罕
愿尊贵、荣耀与赞颂都归与祂，直到无穷无尽的时代。阿们。

## Callimachus / 卡利马科斯

### Argument / 剧情梗概

===Original===
The resurrection of Drusiana and Callimachus.
Callimachus cherishes a guilty passion for Drusiana, not only while she is alive but after she has died in the Lord. He dies from the bite of a serpent, but, thanks to the prayers of Saint John the Apostle, he is restored to life, together with Drusiana, and is born again in Christ.

===Chinese===
德鲁西娅娜（Drusiana）与卡利马科斯（Callimachus）的复活。
卡利马科斯对德鲁西娅娜怀着有罪的情欲，不仅在她生前，甚至在她安逝于主之后。他死于毒蛇之咬，但多亏使徒圣约翰的祈祷，他与德鲁西娅娜一同复生，在基督里重获新生。

### Characters / 人物

===Original===
- Callimachus
- Friends to Callimachus
- Drusiana
- Andronicus
- Fortunatus
- The Apostole John

===Chinese===
- 卡利马科斯
- 卡利马科斯的朋友们
- 德鲁西娅娜
- 安德罗尼库斯（Andronicus）
- 福尔图纳图斯（Fortunatus）
- 使徒约翰（John）

### Callimachus / 卡利马科斯

#### Scene I / 第一场

===Original===
Callimachus
My friends, a word with you.

Friends
We are at your service as long as you please.

Callimachus
I should prefer to speak with you apart from the crowd.

Friends
What pleases you, pleases us.

Callimachus
Then we will go to some quieter place where no one will interrupt us.

Friends
Just as you like.

===Chinese===
卡利马科斯
朋友们，跟你们说句话。

朋友们
但凭吩咐，我们随时候命。

卡利马科斯
我想避开人群，同你们单独谈。

朋友们
你乐意的，我们也乐意。

卡利马科斯
那我们去个清静些的地方，没人打扰。

朋友们
悉听尊便。

#### Scene II / 第二场

===Original===
Callimachus
For a long time now I have been in great trouble. I hope that by confiding in you I shall find relief.

Friends
When a man tells his friends of his sufferings it is only fair that they should try to share them.

Callimachus
I would to heaven that you could lighten this load upon my heart!

Friends
Well, tell us precisely what is wrong. We will grieve with you, if we must. If not, we can do our best to distract your mind.

Callimachus
I love—

Friends
What do you love?

Callimachus
A thing of beauty, a thing of grace!

===Chinese===
卡利马科斯
许久以来，我深陷苦恼。希望向你们倾吐之后，我能得到解脱。

朋友们
一个人向朋友诉说苦楚，朋友理当设法分担。

卡利马科斯
但愿你们真能卸下我心头的重负！

朋友们
那就把实情原原本本告诉我们。若是难免，我们与你同悲；若还来得及，我们尽力为你解忧。

卡利马科斯
我爱——

朋友们
你爱什么？

卡利马科斯
一件美的东西，一件优雅的东西！

===Original===
Friends
That is too vague! How can we tell from this what is the object of your love?

Callimachus
Woman.

Friends
Ah, now you say “woman” we all understand!

Callimachus
By woman, I mean a woman.

Friends
Clearer still! But it is impossible to give an opinion on a subject until the subject is defined. So name the woman.

Callimachus
Drusiana.

===Chinese===
朋友们
这太含糊了！这样我们怎么知道你爱的对象是什么？

卡利马科斯
女人。

朋友们
啊，你一说“女人”，我们全明白了！

卡利马科斯
我说女人，意思是某个女人。

朋友们
更清楚了！可事情不点明，就没法发表意见。说出她的名字吧。

卡利马科斯
德鲁西娅娜。

===Original===
Friends
What? The wife of Prince Andronicus?

Callimachus
Yes.

Friends
Nothing can come of that. She has been baptized.

Callimachus
What do I care, if I can win her love?

Friends
You cannot.

Callimachus
What makes you say so?

Friends
You are crying for the moon.

===Chinese===
朋友们
什么？亲王安德罗尼库斯的妻子？

卡利马科斯
是。

朋友们
那不会有结果的。她已受洗。

卡利马科斯
只要能赢得她的爱，我管那些做什么？

朋友们
你赢不了。

卡利马科斯
何出此言？

朋友们
你是在痴人说梦。

===Original===
Callimachus
Am I the first to do so? Have I not the example of many others to encourage me?

Friends
Now listen. This woman you sigh for is a follower of the holy Apostle John, and has devoted herself entirely to God. They say she will not even go to the bed of Andronicus although he is a devout Christian. Is it likely that she will listen to you?

Callimachus
I came to you for consolation, and instead you drive me to despair!

Friends
We should be poor friends if we consoled and flattered you at the expense of the truth.

===Chinese===
卡利马科斯
难道我是头一个？不是有许多先例给我打气吗？

朋友们
听着。你为之叹息的这个女人，是圣使徒约翰的追随者，已把自己全然献给上帝。据说连安德罗尼库斯的床她都不肯上，尽管他是虔诚的基督徒。她可能听你的吗？

卡利马科斯
我来求安慰，你们却把我往绝望里逼！

朋友们
若为了安慰奉承你而牺牲真相，那我们就不配为友了。

===Original===
Callimachus
Since you refuse to advise me, I will go to her and pour out my soul in words that would melt a heart of stone!

Friends
Fool! It is hopeless!

Callimachus
I defy the stars!

Friends
We shall see.

===Chinese===
卡利马科斯
既然你们不肯为我出主意，我就去找她，用能熔化顽石的话语倾吐我的灵魂！

朋友们
蠢货！没指望的！

卡利马科斯
我偏要逆着星命干！

朋友们
走着瞧。

#### Scene III / 第三场

===Original===
Callimachus
Drusiana, listen to me! Drusiana, my deepest heart’s love!

Drusiana
Your words amaze me, Callimachus. What can you want of me?

Callimachus
You are amazed?

Drusiana
I am astounded.

Callimachus
First I want to speak of love!

Drusiana
Love! What love?

===Chinese===
卡利马科斯
德鲁西娅娜，听我说！德鲁西娅娜，我心底最深的爱！

德鲁西娅娜
你的话让我惊愕，卡利马科斯。你能要我什么呢？

卡利马科斯
你惊愕？

德鲁西娅娜
我骇然。

卡利马科斯
我要先谈谈爱！

德鲁西娅娜
爱！什么爱？

===Original===
Callimachus
That love with which I love you above all created things.

Drusiana
Why should you love me? You are not of my kin. There is no legal bond between us.

Callimachus
It is your beauty.

Drusiana
My beauty?

Callimachus
Yes.

Drusiana
What is my beauty to you?

===Chinese===
卡利马科斯
我以此爱爱你，胜过爱一切受造之物。

德鲁西娅娜
你为什么爱我？你非我亲族，我们之间没有法定姻缘。

卡利马科斯
是你的美貌。

德鲁西娅娜
我的美貌？

卡利马科斯
是。

德鲁西娅娜
我的美貌与你何干？

===Original===
Callimachus
But little now—it is that which tortures me—but I hope that it may be much before long.

Drusiana
Not a word more. Leave me at once, for it is a sin to listen to you now that I understand your devilish meaning.

Callimachus
My Drusiana, do not kill me with your looks. Do not drive away one who worships you, but give back love for love.

Drusiana
Wicked, insidious words! They fall on deaf ears. Your love disgusts me. Understand I despise you!

Callimachus
You cannot make me angry, because I know that you would own my passion moves you if you were not ashamed.

===Chinese===
卡利马科斯
眼下还只是些许——正是它折磨着我——但我希望不久它便会增多。

德鲁西娅娜
不许再说一个字。立刻离开我，如今我明白了你魔鬼般的用意，再听下去便是罪。

卡利马科斯
我的德鲁西娅娜，别用你的眼色杀了我。别赶走一个崇拜你的人，请以爱还爱。

德鲁西娅娜
邪恶、阴险的话！它们落进聋子的耳朵。你的爱令我憎恶。明白告诉你，我鄙夷你！

卡利马科斯
你激不起我的怒气，因为我知道，若不是羞怯作祟，你会承认我的激情打动了你。

===Original===
Drusiana
It moves me to indignation, nothing else.

Callimachus
That feeling will not last.

Drusiana
I shall not change, be sure of that.

Callimachus
I would not be too sure.

Drusiana
You frantic, foolish man! Do not deceive yourself! Why delude yourself with vain hopes? What madness leads you to think that I shall yield? I have renounced even what is lawful—my husband’s bed!

Callimachus
I call heaven and earth to witness that if you do not yield I will never rest from the fight for you. I will be as cunning as the serpent. I will use all my skill and strength to trap you.

===Chinese===
德鲁西娅娜
它打动我的是义愤，仅此而已。

卡利马科斯
那种感觉长不了。

德鲁西娅娜
我不会变，你放明白些。

卡利马科斯
我看未必。

德鲁西娅娜
你这狂乱愚蠢的人！别自欺了！何必用虚妄的希望迷惑自己？什么疯狂让你以为我会屈服？连合法的东西——我丈夫的床——我都弃绝了！

卡利马科斯
我请天地作证：你若不屈服，我为你而战绝不停歇。我要像蛇一样狡猾，用尽全部技巧与力量来诱捕你。

#### Scene IV / 第四场

===Original===
Drusiana
O Lord Jesus, what use is my vow of chastity? My beauty has all the same made this man love me. Pity my fears, O Lord. Pity the grief which has seized me. I know not what to do. If I tell anyone what has happened, there will be disorder in the city on my account; if I keep silence, only Thy grace can protect me from falling into the net spread for me. O Christ, take me to Thyself. Let me die swiftly. Save me from being the ruin of a soul!

Andronicus
Drusiana, Drusiana! Christ, what blow has fallen on me! Drusiana is dead. Run one of you and fetch the holy man John.

===Chinese===
德鲁西娅娜
主耶稣啊，我的守贞之誓有什么用？我的美貌终究还是让这个男人爱上了我。主啊，怜悯我的恐惧，怜悯攫住我的忧苦。我不知所措。若把我遇到的事告诉旁人，城里将因我而起骚乱；若我保持沉默，唯有你的恩典能护佑我不落入为我张开的网。基督啊，接我到你那里去吧。让我速死。救我免于毁灭一个灵魂！

安德罗尼库斯
德鲁西娅娜，德鲁西娅娜！基督啊，何等打击落在我身上！德鲁西娅娜死了。你们谁快跑去把圣者约翰请来。

#### Scene V / 第五场

===Original===
John
Why do you weep, my son?

Andronicus
Oh, horrible! O Lord, that life should suddenly become so hateful!

John
What troubles you?

Andronicus
Drusiana, your disciple, Drusiana—

John
She has passed from the sight of men?

Andronicus
Yes. And I am desolate.

John
It is not right to mourn so bitterly for those whose souls we know rejoice in peace.

===Chinese===
约翰
我儿，你为何哭泣？

安德罗尼库斯
啊，可怕！主啊，生命竟突然变得如此可憎！

约翰
什么事困扰你？

安德罗尼库斯
德鲁西娅娜，你的门徒，德鲁西娅娜——

约翰
她已从人眼前逝去了？

安德罗尼库斯
是。我孤苦无依了。

约翰
明知逝者的灵魂正安享平安，如此恸哭是不应当的。

===Original===
Andronicus
God knows I do not doubt that her soul is in eternal joy, and that her incorrupt body will rise again. What grieves me so sorely is that in my presence just now she prayed for death. She begged she might die.

John
You know her reason?

Andronicus
I know it, and will tell you when I am less sick with grief.

John
Come. We must celebrate the funeral rites with proper ceremony.

Andronicus
There is a marble tomb near here in which the body shall be laid, and our steward Fortunatus shall guard her grave.

John
It is right that she should be interred with honour. God rest her soul in peace.

===Chinese===
安德罗尼库斯
上帝明鉴，我不怀疑她的灵魂在永恒喜乐之中，她那不朽的身体必将复活。令我如此锥心的，是方才她当着我的面祈求死亡。她恳求让她死去。

约翰
你知道缘故吗？

安德罗尼库斯
我知道，等我悲痛稍缓再告诉你。

约翰
来吧。我们须以应有的仪轨举行葬礼。

安德罗尼库斯
附近有一座大理石墓，可将遗体安放其中，我们的管家福尔图纳图斯会看守她的坟墓。

约翰
她理应体面下葬。愿上帝使她的灵魂安息。

#### Scene VI / 第六场

===Original===
Callimachus
Fortunatus, Fortunatus, what is to become of me? Death itself cannot quench my love for Drusiana!

Fortunatus
Poor wretch!

Callimachus
I shall die if you do not help me.

Fortunatus
How can I help you?

===Chinese===
卡利马科斯
福尔图纳图斯，福尔图纳图斯，我会落得什么下场？连死亡也无法熄灭我对德鲁西娅娜的爱！

福尔图纳图斯
可怜虫！

卡利马科斯
你不帮我，我就活不成了。

福尔图纳图斯
我怎么帮你？

===Original===
Callimachus
In this. You can let me look on her, dead.

Fortunatus
Up to now the body is sound and whole, I reckon because it was not wasted with disease. As you know she was taken in a moment by a fever.

Callimachus
Oh, how happy I should be if I might see for myself.

Fortunatus
If you are willing to pay me well, you can do what you like.

===Chinese===
卡利马科斯
就这样：你让我看她一眼，死了的她。

福尔图纳图斯
到现在遗体还完好无损，我想是因为没有疾病的损耗。你知道，她是一场热病瞬间夺去的。

卡利马科斯
啊，若能亲眼见到，我该多么幸福。

福尔图纳图斯
只要你肯出大价钱，想干什么都行。

===Original===
Callimachus
Here, take all I have with me, and be sure that I will give you more, much more, later.

Fortunatus
Quick, then! We’ll go to the tomb.

Callimachus
You cannot go quickly enough for me.

===Chinese===
卡利马科斯
拿着，我身上带的都给你，放心，日后我还会给你更多，多得多。

福尔图纳图斯
那就快点！我们去墓室。

卡利马科斯
再快也不嫌快。

#### Scene VII / 第七场

===Original===
Fortunatus
There lies the body. The face is not like the face of a corpse. The limbs show no sign of decay. You can take her to your heart.

Callimachus
O Drusiana, Drusiana, I worshipped you with my whole soul! I yearned from my very bowels to embrace you! And you repulsed me, and thwarted my desire. Now you are in my power, now I can wound you with my kisses, and pour out my love on you.

Fortunatus
Take care! A monstrous serpent! It is coming towards us!

Callimachus
A curse on me! And on you, Fortunatus, who led me on and urged me to this infamy. Wretch, may you die from the serpent’s bite! Terror and remorse are killing me.

===Chinese===
福尔图纳图斯
遗体在那儿躺着。那脸不像死人的脸，四肢没有腐烂的迹象。你可以把她搂进怀里。

卡利马科斯
啊，德鲁西娅娜，德鲁西娅娜，我以整个灵魂崇拜你！我五内如焚地渴望拥抱你！你却拒绝我，挫败我的欲望。如今你在我掌中，如今我可以用亲吻刺伤你，把我的爱倾倒在你身上。

福尔图纳图斯
小心！一条巨蛇！它朝我们来了！

卡利马科斯
我遭了天谴！也咒你，福尔图纳图斯，是你引诱我、怂恿我干这桩丑事。恶棍，愿你死于蛇咬！恐惧和悔恨正在杀死我。

#### Scene VIII / 第八场

===Original===
John
Come, Andronicus, let us go to Drusiana’s tomb, and commend her soul to Christ in prayer.

Andronicus
It is like your holiness not to forget one who trusted in you.

John
Behold! The invisible God appears to us, made visible in the form of a beautiful youth.

Andronicus
*To the Spectators.* Tremble.8

===Chinese===
约翰
来，安德罗尼库斯，我们去德鲁西娅娜的墓，以祈祷把她的灵魂托付给基督。

安德罗尼库斯
不忘怀信赖您的人，正见您的圣德。

约翰
看哪！那不可见的上帝向我们显现，取了一位俊美青年的可见形貌。

安德罗尼库斯
（对观众）战栗吧。8

===Original===
John
Lord Jesus, why hast Thou deigned to manifest Thyself to Thy servants in this place?

God
To raise Drusiana from the dead, and with her him who lies outside her tomb, have I come, that in them My Name may be glorified.

Andronicus
How swiftly He was caught up again into heaven!

John
I cannot altogether understand what this means.

Andronicus
Let us go on to the tomb. It may be that there what is now obscure will become clear.

===Chinese===
约翰
主耶稣，你为何屈尊在此地向你的仆人显现？

上帝
我来，是要使德鲁西娅娜从死里复活，并使躺在她墓外的那人一同复活，好让我的名在他们身上得荣耀。

安德罗尼库斯
他何等迅捷地又被接回天上！

约翰
我不能全然明白这是什么意思。

安德罗尼库斯
我们去墓那边吧。也许在那里，如今隐晦的事会变明朗。

#### Scene IX / 第九场

===Original===
John
In Christ’s name, what miracle is this? The sepulchre is open, and Drusiana’s body has been cast forth. And near it lie two other corpses enlaced in a serpent’s coils.

Andronicus
I begin to understand. This is Callimachus, who while he lived was consumed with an unholy passion for Drusiana. It troubled her greatly and her distress brought on a fever. She prayed that she might die.

John
Such was her love of chastity.

Andronicus
After her death the wretched man, crazed with love, and stung by the defeat of his wicked plan, was still more inflamed by desire.

John
Pitiable creature!

Andronicus
I have no doubt that he bribed this unworthy servant to give him the opportunity for committing a detestable crime.

===Chinese===
约翰
凭基督之名，这是何等奇迹？墓穴敞开着，德鲁西娅娜的遗体被抛在外头。近旁还躺着两具尸体，缠在蛇的盘绕之中。

安德罗尼库斯
我开始明白了。这是卡利马科斯，生前对德鲁西娅娜怀着不圣洁的情欲，备受煎熬。这令她极为不安，忧苦引发了热病，她才祈求一死。

约翰
她对贞洁的爱竟至于此。

安德罗尼库斯
她死后，这可怜人爱得发狂，又因邪恶计划受挫而心痛如刺，欲火反而烧得更旺。

约翰
可悲的人！

安德罗尼库斯
我毫不怀疑，他买通了这不配称仆人的仆人，为他制造机会去犯那可憎的罪行。

===Original===
John
It is not to be believed!

Andronicus
But death struck both of them down before the deed was accomplished.

John
They met their deserts.

Andronicus
What astonishes me most is that the Divine Voice should have promised the resurrection of him who planned the crime, and not of him who was only an accomplice. Maybe it is because the one, blinded by the passion of the flesh, knew not what he did, while the other sinned of deliberate malice.

John
With what wonderful exactness the Supreme Judge examines the deeds of men! How even the scales in which He weighs the merits of each individual man! None can understand, none explain. Human wisdom cannot grasp the subtlety of the divine judgment.

Andronicus
So we should be content to marvel at it, as it is not in our power to attain a precise knowledge of the causes of things.

===Chinese===
约翰
令人难以置信！

安德罗尼库斯
但死亡在罪行得逞之前，就把两人双双击倒了。

约翰
他们罪有应得。

安德罗尼库斯
最让我惊异的是，那神圣之声应许复活的是主谋，而非仅是协从。也许是因为前者被肉欲蒙蔽，不知自己所为；后者却出于蓄意的恶意而犯罪。

约翰
至高的审判者查究人的行为，何等精审！他称量各人功过的天平，何等公平！无人能懂，无人能解。人的智慧测不透神圣审判的精微。

安德罗尼库斯
所以我们只应惊叹而已，因为究明事物的缘由，非我们力所能及。

===Original===
John
Often the sequel teaches us to understand better.

Andronicus
Then, blessed John, do now what you were told to do. Raise Callimachus to life, and the knot of our perplexity may be untied.

John
First I must invoke the name of Christ to drive away the serpent. Then Callimachus shall be raised.

Andronicus
You are right; else the venom of the creature might do him fresh injury.

John
Hence, savage monster! Away from this man, for now he is to serve Christ.

Andronicus
Although the beast has no reason, it heeds your command.

===Chinese===
约翰
后文常会教我们看得更明白。

安德罗尼库斯
那么，蒙福的约翰，请行那吩咐你行的事吧。叫卡利马科斯复活，我们困惑的死结或许就解开了。

约翰
我先须祈求基督之名驱走那蛇，然后卡利马科斯才能复活。

安德罗尼库斯
您说得对；否则那毒虫的毒液可能再伤着他。

约翰
去吧，凶残的怪物！离开这人，因为他如今要事奉基督了。

安德罗尼库斯
那野兽虽无理性，却听从了你的命令。

===Original===
John
Not through my power, but through Christ’s, it obeys me.

Andronicus
Look! As swift as thought it has vanished!

John
O God, the world cannot contain nor the mind of man comprehend the wonders of Thy incalculable unity, Thou Who alone art what Thou art! O Thou Who by mingling different elements canst create man, and by separating those elements again canst dissolve him, grant that the spirit and the body of this Callimachus may be joined once more, and that he may rise again wholly as he was, so that all looking on him may praise Thee, Who alone canst work miracles!

Andronicus
Look! The breath of life stirs in him again, but he does not move.

John
Calhmachus! In the name of Christ, arise, and confess your sin! Do not keep back the smallest grain of the truth.

===Chinese===
约翰
它服从我，不是凭我的能力，而是凭基督的。

安德罗尼库斯
看！它快如意念般消失了！

约翰
上帝啊，你那不可测度的独一体，其奇妙世界不能容纳，人心不能领悟，唯你是你所是！你混合诸元素而能造人，再分开诸元素而能消解人；求你使这卡利马科斯的灵魂与身体重新结合，使他完好如初地复活，叫一切看见他的人都赞美你，因唯你能行奇迹！

安德罗尼库斯
看！生命的气息又在他体内涌动，只是他还不动。

约翰
卡利马科斯！奉基督之名，起来，认你的罪！真理之事，一丝一毫也不许隐瞒。

===Original===
Callimachus
I cannot deny that I came here for an evil purpose, but the pangs of love consumed me. I was beside myself.

John
What mad folly possessed you? That you should dare think of such a shameful outrage to the chaste dead!

Callimachus
Yes, I was mad; but this knave Fortunatus led me on.

John
And now, most miserable man, confess! Were you so vile as to do what you desired?

Callimachus
No! I could think of it, but I could not do it.

John
What prevented you?

Callimachus
I had hardly touched the lifeless body—I had hardly drawn aside the shroud, when that fellow there, who has been the spark to my fire, died from the serpent’s poison.

Andronicus
A good riddance!

===Chinese===
卡利马科斯
我不能否认，我来此是为邪恶的目的，但爱的苦痛煎熬着我。我方寸大乱。

约翰
什么疯狂的愚妄迷住了你？你竟敢对贞洁的死者起这样可耻的恶念！

卡利马科斯
是，我是疯了；是这个恶棍福尔图纳图斯引诱的我。

约翰
如今，最不幸的人，招认吧！你竟卑劣到做出你想做的事了吗？

卡利马科斯
没有！我能想到，却做不出来。

约翰
是什么拦住了你？

卡利马科斯
我还没碰着那无生气的身体——还没掀开尸衣，那边那个家伙，我这场火的火星，就死于蛇毒了。

安德罗尼库斯
死得好！

===Original===
Callimachus
At the same moment there appeared to me a young man, beautiful yet terrible, who reverently covered the corpse again. From his flaming face and breast burning coals flew out, and one of them, falling on me, touched my face. I heard a voice say, “Callimachus, die to live!” It was then I breathed my last.

John
Oh, heavenly grace! God delights not in the damnation of the wicked.

Callimachus
You have heard the dreadful tale of my temptation. I beg you not to delay the merciful remedy.

John
I will not delay it.

Callimachus
I am overwhelmed by the thought of my abominable crime. I repent with my whole heart, and bewail my sin.

John
That is but right, for a great fault must be atoned for by a great repentance.

===Chinese===
卡利马科斯
同一瞬间，一个青年向我显现，俊美却可畏，他恭谨地重新遮盖了遗体。燃烧的炭火从他炽烈的脸和胸膛飞出，一块落在我身上，碰到我的脸。我听见有声音说：“卡利马科斯，死而后生！”我就在那时断了气。

约翰
啊，天恩浩荡！上帝不愿恶人灭亡。

卡利马科斯
您已听见我受试探的可怕经过。求您不要迟延那仁慈的救药。

约翰
我不迟延。

卡利马科斯
一想到我可憎的罪，我就无地自容。我全心悔改，哀恸我的罪。

约翰
这是应当的，因为大过须以大悔来补赎。

===Original===
Callimachus
Oh, if I could lay bare my heart and show you the bitter anguish I suffer, you would pity me!

John
Not so. Rather does your suffering fill me with joy, for I know that it will be your salvation.

Callimachus
I loathe the delights of the flesh, and all the sins of my past life.

John
That is well.

Callimachus
I truly repent my foul deed.

John
Again that is well.

Callimachus
I am filled with such remorse that I have no desire to live unless I can be born again in Christ and changed.

===Chinese===
卡利马科斯
啊，若能剖开我的心，让您看看我受的苦痛，您会怜悯我的！

约翰
不然。你的受苦反倒使我充满喜乐，因为我知道它将成就你的救恩。

卡利马科斯
我憎恶肉体的欢愉，憎恶我过往一生的所有罪。

约翰
这就好。

卡利马科斯
我真心悔悟我的污行。

约翰
这也很好。

卡利马科斯
我悔恨交加，除非在基督里重生、脱胎换骨，我已无意苟活。

===Original===
John
I do not doubt that heavenly grace is at work in you.

Callimachus
Oh, hasten then to help a man in dire need! Give me some comfort! Help me to throw off the grief which crushes me! Show me how a Pagan may change into a Christian, a fornicator into a chaste man! Oh, set my feet on the way of truth! Teach me to live mindful of the divine promises!

John
Now blessed be the only Son of God, Who made Himself partaker of our frailty, and showed you mercy, my son Callimachus, by striking you down with the death which has brought you to the true life. So has He saved the creature He made in His own image from the death of the soul.

Andronicus
Most strange, most wonderful miracle!

===Chinese===
约翰
我不怀疑，天上的恩典正在你心里动工。

卡利马科斯
啊，那就快救助这危困中的人吧！给我些安慰！帮我卸下压碎我的忧伤！指教我，一个异教徒如何变为基督徒，一个淫荡之人如何变为贞洁之人！啊，把我的脚引上真理之路！教我怀着对神圣应许的记念而活！

约翰
愿上帝的独生子受赞美，他自甘分担我们的软弱，并以那引你进入真生命的死亡击倒你，向你施了怜悯，我儿卡利马科斯。他就这样救了他按自己形象所造的造物，免于灵魂之死。

安德罗尼库斯
何等奇异、何等奇妙的奇迹！

===Original===
John
O Christ, redemption of the world, and sinners’ atonement, I have no words to praise Thee! The sweetness of Thy compassion amazes me. Now Thou dost win the sinner with gentleness, now Thou dost chastise him with just severity, and callest on him to do penance.

Andronicus
Glory to His divine goodness!

John
Who would have presumed to hope that a man like this, intent on a wicked deed when death overtook him, would be raised to life again, and given the chance of making reparation! Blessed be Thy name forever and ever, O Thou Who alone canst do these wondrous things!

Andronicus
Holy John, give me some comfort too. The love I bear my dead wife will not let me rest until I have seen her also called back from the dead.

===Chinese===
约翰
基督啊，世界的救赎，罪人的挽回祭，我没有言语足以赞美你！你怜悯的甘甜令我惊叹。你时而以温柔赢获罪人，时而以公义的严厉惩戒他，召他行补赎。

安德罗尼库斯
愿荣耀归于他的神圣美善！

约翰
谁敢奢望这样的人——死亡追上他时，他正一心作恶——竟能复活，还能得到补过的机会！愿你尊名受颂赞，世世无穷，唯独你能行这等奇事！

安德罗尼库斯
圣约翰，也给我些安慰吧。对我亡妻的爱让我不得安息，除非我也看见她从死里被唤回。

===Original===
John
Drusiana, our Lord Jesus Christ calls you back to life!

Drusiana
Glory and praise to Thee, O Lord, Who hast made me live again!

Callimachus
Thanks be to that merciful power, my Drusiana, through which you, who left this life in such sorrow, rise again in joy!

Drusiana
Venerable father John, you have restored to life Callimachus, who loved me sinfully. Should you not also raise from the dead the man who betrayed my buried body?

Callimachus
Apostle of Christ, do not believe it! Will you release from the fetters of death this evil creature, this traitor, who led me away and persuaded me to venture on that horrible deed?

===Chinese===
约翰
德鲁西娅娜，我们的主耶稣基督唤你回生！

德鲁西娅娜
荣耀颂赞归于你，主啊，你使我重获生命！

卡利马科斯
感谢那仁慈的大能，我的德鲁西娅娜，你含着那样的愁苦离开此世，如今又在喜乐中复生！

德鲁西娅娜
