可敬的约翰神父，您使罪爱我的卡利马科斯复生了。您不也该把那出卖我埋葬遗体的人从死里救起吗？

卡利马科斯
基督的使徒，不要信她！您要把这邪恶的东西、这叛徒从死亡的锁链里放出来吗？是他引开我，劝我去做那桩可怕的事！

===Original===
John
You should not wish to deprive him of divine mercy, my son.

Callimachus
He tried to ruin me! He is not worthy of resurrection!

John
We are taught by our faith that man must forgive his fellow-man if he would be forgiven by God.

Andronicus
That is true.

John
Remember that when the only Son of God, the Virgin’s firstborn, the one man born without a stain, came into this world, He found us all bowed under the heavy weight of sin.

Andronicus
True again.

===Chinese===
约翰
我儿，你不该想剥夺他领受神圣怜悯的机会。

卡利马科斯
他要毁掉我！他不配复活！

约翰
我们的信仰教导我们：人若要上帝赦免自己，必须赦免自己的同伴。

安德罗尼库斯
这是真的。

约翰
要记得，上帝的独生子、童贞女的首生子、唯一无瑕而生的人来到这世界时，发现我们全都伏在罪的重担之下。

安德罗尼库斯
的确。

===Original===
John
And though not one of us was guiltless, He deprived no one of His mercy, but offered Himself for all, and for all laid down His life in love.

Andronicus
Had the Innocent One not been slain, none of us would have been saved.

John
He cannot rejoice in the damnation of those whom He bought with His blood.

Andronicus
To Him be praise!

John
This is why we must not grudge the grace of God to anyone. It is no merit of ours if it abounds in ourselves.

Callimachus
Your rebuke makes me ashamed.

===Chinese===
约翰
我们中间虽无一人无罪，他却不曾把怜悯从任何人身上夺去，而是为众人献上自己，为众人在爱中舍命。

安德罗尼库斯
若非那无辜者被杀，我们无一能得救。

约翰
他不能因他用血买来之人灭亡而喜乐。

安德罗尼库斯
愿颂赞归于他！

约翰
所以我们不可把上帝的恩典吝于给任何人。恩典若丰丰盛盛住在我们身上，也非我们的功德。

卡利马科斯
您的责备使我羞愧。

===Original===
John
Yet it is not for me to oppose you. Drusiana, inspired by God Himself shall raise this man.

Drusiana
Divine Essence without material form, Who hast made man in Thine own image and breathed into this clay the spirit of life, bring back the vital heat to the body of Fortunatus, that our triple resurrection may glorify the adorable Trinity.

John
Amen.

Drusiana
Fortunatus, awake, and in the name of Christ burst the bonds of death.

===Chinese===
约翰
然而我也不便违逆你。德鲁西娅娜，你将受上帝亲自感动，使这人复活。

德鲁西娅娜
无形无质的神圣本体，你按自己的形象造人，将生命之气吹入这泥土，求你使福尔图纳图斯的身体重获生命的热力，好让我们三人的复活荣耀那可钦崇的三位一体。

约翰
阿们。

德鲁西娅娜
福尔图纳图斯，醒来，奉基督之名挣断死亡的锁链。

===Original===
Fortunatus
Who wakes me? Who takes my hand? Who calls me back to life?

John
Drusiana.

Fortunatus
How can that be? Only a few days since she died.

John
Yes, but now, through the power of Christ, she lives again.

Fortunatus
And is that Callimachus who stands there? By his sober and pious look one would think he is no longer dying of love for his Drusiana!

John
All that is changed. Now he loves and serves Christ.

===Chinese===
福尔图纳图斯
谁唤醒了我？谁拉着我的手？谁唤我回生？

约翰
德鲁西娅娜。

福尔图纳图斯
那怎么可能？她死了才几天。

约翰
是，可如今，凭着基督的大能，她又活了。

福尔图纳图斯
站在那儿的是卡利马科斯吗？瞧他清醒虔敬的神色，谁能想到他不久前还在为他的德鲁西娅娜害相思害得要死！

约翰
一切都变了。如今他爱慕基督、事奉基督。

===Original===
Fortunatus
No!

John
It is true.

Fortunatus
If it is as you say, if Drusiana has restored me to life and Callimachus believes in Christ, I reject life and choose death. I would rather not exist than see them swelling with grace and virtue!

John
Oh, incredible envy of the devil! Oh, malice of the old serpent, who since he made our first parents taste death has never ceased to writhe at the glory of the righteous! Oh, Fortunatus, brimful of Satan’s bitter gall, how much do you resemble the rotten tree that, bearing only bad fruit, must be cut down and cast into the fire! To the fire you must go, where, deprived of the society of those who fear God, you will be tormented without respite forever.

Andronicus
Look! Oh, look! His wounds have opened again. He has been taken at his word. He is dying.

===Chinese===
福尔图纳图斯
不会吧！

约翰
是真的。

福尔图纳图斯
若真如你所说，若德鲁西娅娜使我复生、卡利马科斯信了基督，那我拒绝生命，选择死亡。我宁可不存在，也不愿看他们恩典美德充盈的样子！

约翰
啊，魔鬼那难以置信的嫉妒！啊，古蛇的恶意，自从它使我们始祖尝到死味，它见到义人的荣耀便从未停止翻搅！啊，满盛撒但苦胆的福尔图纳图斯，你多么像那棵只结坏果子的朽树，必被砍倒扔进火里！你必归入那火，在那里，与一切敬畏上帝之人隔绝，受无尽的煎熬，永无休止。

安德罗尼库斯
看！啊，快看！他的伤口又裂开了。他求仁得仁。他要死了。

===Original===
John
Let him die and go down to hell, who through envious spite rejected the gift of life.

Andronicus
A terrible fate.

John
Nothing is more terrible than envy, nothing more evil than pride.

Andronicus
Both are vile.

John
The man who is the victim of one is the victim of the other, for they have no separate existence.

Andronicus
Please explain.

===Chinese===
约翰
让他死去，堕入地狱吧，他因嫉妒怨恨而拒绝了生命的恩赐。

安德罗尼库斯
可怕的命运。

约翰
无物比嫉妒更可怕，无物比骄傲更邪恶。

安德罗尼库斯
两者皆卑劣。

约翰
为其一所害的人，必为另一所害，因为它们本不可分。

安德罗尼库斯
请解释。

===Original===
John
The proud are envious, and the envious are proud. A jealous man cannot endure to hear others praised, and seeks to belittle those who are more perfect. He disdains to take a lower place, and arrogantly seeks to be put above his equals.

Andronicus
That is clear.

John
This wretched man’s pride was wounded. He could not endure the humiliation of recognizing his inferiority to these two in whom he could not deny God had made more grace to shine.

Andronicus
I understand now why his resurrection was not spoken of. It was known he would die again.

John
He deserved to die twice, for to his crime of profaning the sacred grave entrusted to him, he added hatred and envy of those who had been restored to life.

Andronicus
The wretched creature is dead now.

===Chinese===
约翰
骄傲者嫉妒，嫉妒者骄傲。善妒的人听不得别人受称赞，便设法贬低更完全的人。他不甘居人下，傲慢地谋求凌驾同侪。

安德罗尼库斯
这就清楚了。

约翰
这可怜人的骄傲受了伤。他不能否认上帝使这两人身上闪耀更多恩典，因此，承认自己不如他们的那份屈辱，他忍受不了。

安德罗尼库斯
我如今明白，为何无人提起他的复活。原来早知道他还会再死。

约翰
他配死两次，因为在他亵渎所托墓穴的罪上，又添上了对复生之人的仇恨与嫉妒。

安德罗尼库斯
这可怜的东西现在是死了。

===Original===
John
Come, let us go—Satan must have his own. This day shall be kept as a festival in thanksgiving for the wonderful conversion of Callimachus. Men shall long speak of it, and of his resurrection from the dead, and of Drusiana, on whom his love brought misery. Let us give thanks to God, that just and penetrating Judge Who alone can search the heart and reins and reward or punish fairly. To Him alone be honour, strength, glory, praise, and blessing, world without end. Amen.

===Chinese===
约翰
来，我们走吧——撒但必得他自己的份。今日当守为节日，以感谢卡利马科斯奇妙的归正。人们将久久传讲这事，传讲他从死里复活，传讲德鲁西娅娜——他的爱给她带来了苦难。让我们感谢上帝，那公义而明察的审判者，唯独他能洞察人的心肠肺腑，公平地赏罚。唯愿尊贵、权能、荣耀、颂赞、祝福都归于他，世世无穷。阿们。

## Dedication / 献词

===Original===
To Gerberg

Illustrious Abbess, venerated no less for uprightness and honesty than for the high distinction of a royal and noble race, Roswitha of Gandersheim, the last of the least of those fighting under your ladyship’s rule, desires to give you all that a servant owes her mistress.

O my Lady, bright with the varied jewels of spiritual wisdom, your maternal kindness will not let you hesitate to read what, as you know, was written at your command! It was you who gave me the task of chronicling in verse the deeds of the Emperor, and you know that it was impossible to collect them together from hearsay. You can imagine the difficulties which my ignorance put in my way while I was toiling over this work. There were things of which I could not find any written record, nor could I elicit information by word of mouth which seemed sufficiently reliable. I was like a person in a strange land wandering without a guide through a forest where the path is concealed by dense snow. In vain he tries to follow the directions of those who have shown the way. He wanders from the path, now by chance strikes it again, until at last, penetrating the thickness of the wood, he reaches a place where he may take a long-desired rest, and sitting down there, does not proceed further until someone overtakes him, or he discovers the footprints of one who has gone before. Even so have I, obeying the command to undertake a complete chronicle of great deeds, gone on my way, trembling, hesitating, and vacillating, so great was the difficulty of finding a path in the forest of these royal achievements.

And now, worn out by the journey, I am holding my peace and resting in a suitable place. I do not propose to go further without better guidance. If I could be inspired by the eloquent words of learned folk (either already set down or to be set down in the future) I might perhaps find a means of glozing my uncouth workmanship. At present I am defenceless at every point, because I am not supported by any authority. I also fear I shall be accused of temerity in presuming to describe in my humble uncultured way matters which ought to be set forth with all the ceremony of great learning. Yet if my work is examined by those who know how to weigh things fairly, I shall be more easily pardoned on account of my sex and my inferior knowledge, especially as I did not undertake it of my own will but at your command. Why should I fear the judgment of others, since if there are mistakes I should fall only under your censure, and why should I not escape reproof seeing that I was anxious to keep silence? I should deserve blame if I sought to withhold my work. In any case I leave the decision to you and your friend, Archbishop William, to whom you have thought fit to show these unpolished lines.

===Chinese===
致格贝格

尊贵的院长修女：您之受人尊崇，既因王室贵胄之显赫门第，亦不稍减于正直与诚笃之德。甘德斯海姆的罗斯维塔——在您治下效命的众人中最微末者中的最末一人——愿将仆役对女主君所应尽的一切，悉数奉献于您。

哦，我的女主人，您焕发着灵性智慧的多彩珠光；您慈母般的仁厚，必不会使您踌躇于展读这些您明知是奉您之命而写下的文字！正是您将编写皇帝功业纪事诗的任务交付于我，而您也知道，仅凭道听途说，根本无从将其搜罗齐全。您可以想见，我在这件工作上艰难跋涉时，自身的愚陋给我设置了多少障碍。有些事，我找不到任何文字记载；有些事，我虽能从口头探问，却得不到足够可靠的讯息。我就像一个身在异乡的旅人，没有向导，在积雪掩埋路径的森林中彷徨。他循着先行者指引的方向前行，却是徒劳；他偏离了路径，又偶然重新踏上，直到最后穿过密林深处，抵达一处可以安享久盼之憩息的地方，坐下歇息，不再前行，直到有人赶上他，或他发现了先行者的足迹。我奉命撰写一部完备的丰功伟绩编年史，也正是这样一路走来——战栗着、踌躇着、摇摆着，因为在这片帝王功业的森林里寻路，实在难乎其难。

如今，旅程已使我疲惫不堪，我便住口不言，在一处相宜的地方歇息。没有更好的指引，我不打算再往前走。倘若博学之士的锦言妙句（无论已经写下，还是日后将要写下的）能够给我以启迪，我或许还能找到一点办法，为我粗陋的笔工稍加文饰。眼下我处处无以自辩，因为没有任何权威足以倚仗。我还担心会被人指斥胆大妄为：竟敢以如此卑陋无文的方式，去描叙本应以渊博学问的全部隆仪来铺陈的事体。然而，倘若我的作品得到公允持衡之士的审阅，那么念及我的女性身份与浅薄的学识，尤其是念及我并非出于自愿、而是奉您之命才承担此事，我当更容易获得宽宥。我又何必畏惧他人的评判呢？倘若真有差误，我理应只受您一人的裁断；而我既然一心只想保持缄默，又怎会逃不过责备呢？倘若我试图将作品秘不示人，那才真正当受咎责。无论如何，我把裁夺之权交托于您和您的友人威廉大主教——您认为宜于将这些未经润色的诗行出示于他。

## Dulcitius / 杜尔基提乌斯

### Argument / 剧情梗概

===Original===
The martyrdom of the holy virgins Agape, Chionia, and Irena. The Governor Dulcitius seeks them out in the silence of the night with criminal intent, but hardly has he entered their dwelling than he becomes the victim of a delusion, under which he mistakes for the objects of his passion the saucepans and frying-pans in the kitchen. These he embraces and covers with kisses until his face and clothes are black with soot and dirt. Later, by order of Diocletian, he hands the maidens over to the care of Sisinnius, who is charged with their punishment. Sisinnius in his turn is made the sport of the most strange delusions, but at length succeeds in getting Agape and Chionia burnt, and Irena shot to death with arrows.

===Chinese===
圣洁童女阿加佩（Agape）、希奥尼娅（Chionia）与伊雷娜（Irena）的殉道。总督杜尔基提乌斯（Dulcitius）趁夜深人静、心怀邪念前来寻访她们，不料刚一踏进她们的居所，便成了错觉的牺牲品——在错觉之中，他把厨房里的炖锅与平底锅当成了泄欲的对象。他对它们又搂又亲，直到脸和衣服全被煤烟污垢染黑。其后，他奉戴克里先（Diocletian）之命，把少女们移交给受命惩处她们的西西尼乌斯（Sisinnius）。西西尼乌斯又轮到自己被种种怪异的错觉所戏弄，但终究还是把阿加佩与希奥尼娅烧死，把伊雷娜乱箭射杀。

### Characters / 人物

===Original===
- The Emperor Diocletian
- Agape
- Chionia
- Irena
- Dulcitius, governor of Thessalonica.
- Soldiers
- Sisinnius
- Wife to Dulcitius
- Ushers of the Imperial Palace
- Ladies-in-Waiting on the Wife of Dulcitius

===Chinese===
- 皇帝戴克里先
- 阿加佩
- 希奥尼娅
- 伊雷娜
- 杜尔基提乌斯，塞萨洛尼基（Thessalonica）总督
- 士兵们
- 西西尼乌斯
- 杜尔基提乌斯之妻
- 皇宫司阍
- 杜尔基提乌斯夫人的宫廷女侍

### Dulcitius / 杜尔基提乌斯

#### Scene I / 第一场

===Original===
Diocletian
The pure and famous race to which you belong and your own rare beauty make it fitting that you should be wedded to the highest in our court. Thus we decree, making the condition that you first promise to deny your Christ and sacrifice to the gods.

Agape
We beg you not to concern yourself about us, and it is useless to make preparations for our marriage. Nothing can make us deny that Name which all should confess, or let our purity be stained.

Diocletian
What does this madness mean?

Agape
What sign of madness do you see in us?

Diocletian
It is clear enough.

Agape
In what way are we mad?

===Chinese===
戴克里先
你们出身高贵显赫的门第，加上自身罕见的姿容，理应婚配我朝中最尊贵之人。朕如此裁断，但有一个条件：你们须先允诺背弃你们的基督，向诸神献祭。

阿加佩
恳请陛下不必为我们费心，为我们的婚事做准备也是徒劳。没有什么能叫我们否认那万人皆当承认的圣名，也没有什么能容我们的贞洁蒙受玷污。

戴克里先
这疯狂是何意思？

阿加佩
陛下在我们身上看出什么疯狂的迹象？

戴克里先
再清楚不过。

阿加佩
我们如何疯狂了？

===Original===
Diocletian
Is it not madness to give up practising an ancient religion and run after this silly new Christian superstition?

Agape
You are bold to slander the majesty of Almighty God. It is dangerous.

Diocletian
Dangerous? To whom?

Agape
To you, and to the state you rule.

Diocletian
The girl raves. Take her away.

Chionia
My sister does not rave. She is right.

===Chinese===
戴克里先
抛弃世代奉行的古老宗教，去追逐这愚妄的新兴基督徒迷信，难道不是疯狂吗？

阿加佩
你胆敢诽谤全能上帝的威严，这是危险的。

戴克里先
危险？对谁？

阿加佩
对你，对你统治的国家。

戴克里先
这女子在说呓语。把她带下去。

希奥尼娅
我姐姐没有说呓语。她说得对。

===Original===
Diocletian
This maenad seems even more violent than the other! Remove her also from our presence, and we will question the third.

Irena
You will find her as rebellious and as determined to resist.

Diocletian
Irena, you are the youngest in years. Show yourself the oldest in dignity.

Irena
Pray tell me how.

Diocletian
Bow your head to the gods, and set an example to your sisters. It may rebuke and save them.

Irena
Let those who wish to provoke the wrath of the Most High prostrate themselves before idols! I will not dishonour this head which has been anointed with heavenly oil by abasing it at the feet of images.

===Chinese===
戴克里先
这个酒神狂女似乎比刚才那个还要凶悍！把她也带下去，朕来盘问第三个。

伊雷娜
你们会发现她同样桀骜，同样决意抗拒。

戴克里先
伊雷娜，你年纪最幼，却要在尊严上显出最长。

伊雷娜
请教如何显法。

戴克里先
向诸神低头，为你的姐姐们做个榜样，或可惩戒她们，拯救她们。

伊雷娜
谁愿触怒至高者，谁就去向偶像俯伏！这头已蒙天上的膏油涂沐，我绝不容它在雕像脚下受辱。

===Original===
Diocletian
The worship of the gods does not bring dishonour to those who practise it, but, on the contrary, the greatest honour.

Irena
What could be more shameful baseness, what baser shame, than to venerate slaves as if they were lords?

Diocletian
I do not ask you to worship slaves, but the gods of princes and the rulers of the earth.

Irena
A god who can be bought cheap in the marketplace, what is he but a slave?

Diocletian
Enough of this presumptuous chatter. The rack shall put an end to it!

Irena
That is what we desire. We ask nothing better than to suffer the most cruel tortures for the love of Christ.

Diocletian
Let these obstinate women who dare to defy our authority be laden with chains and thrown into a dungeon. Let them be examined by Governor Dulcitius.

===Chinese===
戴克里先
敬拜诸神不会使敬拜者蒙羞，恰恰相反，会带来至高的荣耀。

伊雷娜
还有比把奴隶当作主子来敬奉更可耻的下贱、更下贱的可耻吗？

戴克里先
朕并非要你敬拜奴隶，而是要你敬拜君王之神、大地统治者之神。

伊雷娜
一个在市场上花贱价便能买到的神，不是奴隶又是什么？

戴克里先
住口，休得放肆饶舌。让拉肢刑架来终结这一切！

伊雷娜
那正是我们所愿。为了对基督的爱，纵受最残酷的酷刑，我们也别无所求。

戴克里先
将这些胆敢抗拒朕之威权的顽固女子戴上镣铐，投入地牢，交由总督杜尔基提乌斯审问。

#### Scene II / 第二场

===Original===
Dulcitius
Soldiers, produce your prisoners.

Soldiers
The ones you wanted to see are in there.

Dulcitius
Ye Gods, but these girls are beautiful! What grace, what charm!

Soldiers
Perfect!

Dulcitius
I am enraptured!

Soldiers
No wonder!

===Chinese===
杜尔基提乌斯
士兵们，把囚犯带上来。

士兵们
您要见的人就在里面。

杜尔基提乌斯
诸位神明，这些姑娘真美啊！何等的风姿，何等的妩媚！

士兵们
美极了！

杜尔基提乌斯
我心醉神迷了！

士兵们
难怪！

===Original===
Dulcitius
I’m in love! Do you think they will fall in love with me?

Soldiers
From what we know, you will have little success.

Dulcitius
Why?

Soldiers
Their faith is too strong.

Dulcitius
A few sweet words will work wonders!

Soldiers
They despise flattery.

===Chinese===
杜尔基提乌斯
我恋爱了！你们说，她们会不会也爱上我？

士兵们
照我们所知，您难有胜算。

杜尔基提乌斯
为什么？

士兵们
她们的信仰太坚定了。

杜尔基提乌斯
几句甜言蜜语就会创造奇迹！

士兵们
她们瞧不上奉承。

===Original===
Dulcitius
Then I shall woo in another fashion—with torture!

Soldiers
They would not care.

Dulcitius
What’s to be done, then?

Soldiers
That is for you to find out.

Dulcitius
Lock them in the inner room—the one leading out of the passage where the pots and pans are kept.

Soldiers
Why there?

===Chinese===
杜尔基提乌斯
那我就换个方式求爱——用刑！

士兵们
她们不会在乎的。

杜尔基提乌斯
那可怎么办？

士兵们
那就得您自己去想法子了。

杜尔基提乌斯
把她们锁进里面的房间——就是那间由存放锅罐的过道进去的屋子。

士兵们
为什么锁那儿？

===Original===
Dulcitius
I can visit them oftener.

Soldiers
It shall be done.

===Chinese===
杜尔基提乌斯
我可以更常去探望她们。

士兵们
遵命。

#### Scene III / 第三场

===Original===
Dulcitius
What can the prisoners be doing at this hour of night?

Soldiers
They pass the time singing hymns.

Dulcitius
Let us approach.

Soldiers
Now you can hear their silver-sweet voices in the distance.

Dulcitius
Take your torches, and guard the doors. I will go in and enjoy myself in those lovely arms!

Soldiers
Enter. We will wait for you here.

===Chinese===
杜尔基提乌斯
深夜此时，囚犯们在做什么呢？

士兵们
她们唱圣歌打发时光。

杜尔基提乌斯
我们走近些。

士兵们
现在您能听见她们银铃般的歌声从远处传来了。

杜尔基提乌斯
拿好火把，守住门。我要进去，在那可爱的臂弯里痛快一番！

士兵们
请进。我们在这里等您。

#### Scene IV / 第四场

===Original===
Agape
What noise is that outside the door?

Irena
It is that wretch Dulcitius.

Chionia
Now may God protect us!

Agape
Amen.

Chionia
There is more noise! It sounds like the clashing of pots and pans and fire-irons.

===Chinese===
阿加佩
门外是什么声音？

伊雷娜
是那个恶棍杜尔基提乌斯。

希奥尼娅
愿上帝此刻保护我们！

阿加佩
阿们。

希奥尼娅
又有响动了！听着像是锅盆和火钳火棍相撞的声音。

===Original===
Irena
I will go and look. Come quick and peep through the crack of the door!

Agape
What is it?

Irena
Oh, look! He must be out of his senses! I believe he thinks that he is kissing us.

Agape
What is he doing?

Irena
Now he presses the saucepans tenderly to his breast, now the kettles and frying-pans! He is kissing them hard!

===Chinese===
伊雷娜
我去看看。快来，从门缝里偷看！

阿加佩
怎么回事？

伊雷娜
啊，快看！他准是失心疯了！我看他以为自己在亲吻我们呢。

阿加佩
他在做什么？

伊雷娜
这会儿他把炖锅温柔地搂在怀里，一会儿又是水壶和平底锅！他亲得可起劲了！

===Original===
Chionia
How absurd!

Irena
His face, his hands, his clothes! They are all as black as soot. He looks like an Ethiope.

Agape
I am glad. His body should turn black—to match his soul, which is possessed of a devil.

Irena
Look! He is going now. Let us watch the soldiers and see what they do when he goes out.

===Chinese===
希奥尼娅
太荒唐了！

伊雷娜
他的脸、他的手、他的衣裳！全都黑得像烟灰。他看上去活像个埃塞俄比亚人。

阿加佩
我很高兴。他的身体就该变黑——正配他那被魔鬼盘踞的灵魂。

伊雷娜
看！他要走了。咱们瞧瞧士兵们见他出来会怎样。

#### Scene V / 第五场

===Original===
Soldiers
What’s this? Either one possessed by the devil, or the devil himself. Let’s be off!

Dulcitius
Soldiers, soldiers! Why do you hurry away? Stay, wait! Light me to my house with your torches.

Soldiers
The voice is our master’s voice, but the face is a devil’s. Come, let’s take to our heels! This devil means us no good.

Dulcitius
I will hasten to the palace. I will tell the whole court how I have been insulted.

===Chinese===
士兵们
这是什么？不是着魔的人，就是魔鬼本人。快跑吧！

杜尔基提乌斯
士兵们，士兵们！你们跑什么？站住，等等！替我打着火把，照我回家。

士兵们
声音是我们大人的声音，脸却是魔鬼的脸。快，咱们逃命吧！这魔鬼对我们不怀好意。

杜尔基提乌斯
我要赶紧到宫里去，向满朝上下诉说我是怎样受辱的。

#### Scene VI / 第六场

===Original===
Dulcitius
Ushers, admit me at once. I have important business with the Emperor.

Ushers
Who is this fearsome, horrid monster? Coming here in these filthy rags! Come, let us beat him and throw him down the steps. Stop him from coming further.

Dulcitius
Ye gods, what has happened to me? Am I not dressed in my best? Am I not clean and fine in my person? And yet everyone who meets me expresses disgust at the sight of me and treats me as if I were some foul monster! I will go to my wife. She will tell me the truth. But here she comes. Her looks are wild, her hair unbound, and all her household follow her weeping.

===Chinese===
杜尔基提乌斯
司阍们，立刻放我进去。我有要事面奏皇帝。

司阍们
这骇人的怪物是谁？穿着这身破烂肮脏衣服就来了！来，咱们揍他一顿，把他扔下台阶，别让他再往前走。

杜尔基提乌斯
诸位神明啊，我这是怎么了？我难道不是盛装打扮？我难道不是仪表整洁光鲜？可每个遇见我的人都一脸厌恶，把我当成什么污秽的怪物！我去找我的妻子，她会告诉我真相。可她来了——她神色狂乱，头发散乱，全家人跟在她身后哭泣。

#### Scene VII / 第七场

===Original===
Wife of Dulcitius
My lord, my lord, what evil has come on you? Have you lost your reason, Dulcitius? Have the Christ-worshippers put a spell on you?

Dulcitius
Now at last I know! Those artful women have made an ass of me!

Wife of Dulcitius
What troubled me most, and made my heart ache, was that you should not know there was anything amiss with you.

Dulcitius
Those impudent wenches shall be stripped and exposed naked in public. They shall have a taste of the outrage to which I have been subjected!

===Chinese===
杜尔基提乌斯之妻
老爷，老爷，您遭了什么邪了？您失了心智吗，杜尔基提乌斯？是那些拜基督的人对您施了咒吗？

杜尔基提乌斯
我总算明白了！那些刁滑的女人把我耍成了蠢驴！

杜尔基提乌斯之妻
最让我揪心、最让我心痛的，是你竟然不知道自己出了什么岔子。

杜尔基提乌斯
那些无耻的丫头要被剥光衣服，当众示众。让她们也尝尝我所受的这场羞辱！

#### Scene VIII / 第八场

===Original===
Soldiers
Here we are sweating like pigs and what’s the use? Their clothes cling to their bodies like their own skin. What’s more, our chief, who ordered us to strip them, sits there snoring, and there’s no way of waking him. We will go to the Emperor and tell him all that has passed.

===Chinese===
士兵们
咱们累得像猪一样汗如雨下，可有什么用？她们的衣服紧贴在身上，就像她们自己的皮肤。再说，命令我们剥她们衣服的头儿，却坐在那儿打鼾，怎么也叫不醒。咱们去见皇帝，把经过的一切都禀告他。

#### Scene IX / 第九场

===Original===
Diocletian
I grieve to hear of the outrageous way in which the Governor Dulcitius has been insulted and hoaxed! But these girls shall not boast of having blasphemed our gods with impunity, or of having made a mock of those who worship them. I will entrust the execution of my vengeance to Count Sisinnius.

===Chinese===
戴克里先
听闻总督杜尔基提乌斯遭受如此骇人的羞辱与戏弄，朕深感痛心！但这些女子亵渎我们的神明而妄图逍遥，又嘲弄敬拜诸神之人，绝不容她们夸口。朕将把复仇之事交由西西尼乌斯伯爵执行。

#### Scene X / 第十场

===Original===
Sisinnius
Soldiers, where are these impudent hussies who are to be put to the torture?

Soldiers
In there.

Sisinnius
Keep Irena back, and bring the others here.

Soldiers
Why is one to be treated differently?

Sisinnius
She is young, and besides she may be more easily influenced when not intimidated by her sisters.

Soldiers
That may be so.

===Chinese===
西西尼乌斯
士兵们，那些要受刑的无耻丫头在哪儿？

士兵们
在里面。

西西尼乌斯
把伊雷娜留下，把另外两个带来。

士兵们
为什么一个要区别对待？

西西尼乌斯
她年纪小，再说，没有姐姐们给她撑胆，她更容易被说动。

士兵们
或许是吧。

#### Scene XI / 第十一场

===Original===
Soldiers
We have brought the girls you asked for.

Sisinnius
Agape, and you, Chionia, take my advice.

Agape
And if we do, what then?

Sisinnius
You will sacrifice to the gods.

Agape
We offer a perpetual sacrifice of praise to the true God, the eternal Father, to His Son, co-eternal, and to the Holy Ghost.

===Chinese===
士兵们
您要的姑娘们带来了。

西西尼乌斯
阿加佩，还有你，希奥尼娅，听我一句劝。

阿加佩
听了又怎样？

西西尼乌斯
你们就向诸神献祭。

阿加佩
我们向真神、永恒的圣父、与他同恒的圣子、并圣灵，献上永不止息的赞美之祭。

===Original===
Sisinnius
I do not speak of that sacrifice. That is prohibited on pain of the most severe penalties.

Agape
You have no power over us, and can never compel us to sacrifice to demons.

Sisinnius
Do not be obstinate. Sacrifice to the gods, or by order of the Emperor Diocletian I must put you to death.

Chionia
Your Emperor has ordered you to put us to death, and you must obey, as we scorn his decree. If you were to spare us out of pity, you also would die.

===Chinese===
西西尼乌斯
我说的不是那种祭。那种祭已被严禁，违者严惩不贷。

阿加佩
你们辖制不了我们，永远不能强迫我们向魔鬼献祭。

西西尼乌斯
别顽固不化。向诸神献祭，否则我奉戴克里先皇帝之命，必须处死你们。

希奥尼娅
你的皇帝命你处死我们，你只得服从，正如我们蔑视他的敕令。你若因怜悯饶了我们，你自己也得死。

===Original===
Sisinnius
Come, soldiers! Seize these blasphemers and fling them alive into the flames.

Soldiers
We will build a pyre at once. The fierceness of the fire will soon put an end to their insolence.

Agape
O Lord, we know Thy power! It would not be anything strange or new if the fire forgot its nature and obeyed Thee. But we are weary of this world, and we implore Thee to break the bonds that chain our souls, and to let our bodies be consumed that we may rejoice with Thee in heaven.

Soldiers
O wonderful, most wonderful! Their spirits have left their bodies, but there is no sign of any hurt. Neither their hair, nor their garments, much less their bodies, have been touched by the flames!

Sisinnius
Bring Irena here.

Soldiers
There she is.

===Chinese===
西西尼乌斯
来人，士兵们！抓住这些亵渎者，把她们活生生扔进火里。

士兵们
我们马上架起柴堆。烈火很快就会终结她们的狂妄。

阿加佩
主啊，我们深知你的大能！火焰若忘记自己的本性而服从你，那并非什么奇事或新事。但我们已厌倦这尘世，恳求你斩断锁住我们灵魂的枷锁，让我们的身体被烧尽，使我们得以在天上与你同乐。

士兵们
啊，奇妙，太奇妙了！她们的灵魂已离开身体，却看不出丝毫损伤。她们的头发、衣裳无一被火焰所伤，更不必说身体了！

西西尼乌斯
把伊雷娜带来。

士兵们
她来了。

#### Scene XII / 第十二场

===Original===
Sisinnius
Irena, take warning from the fate of your sisters, and tremble, for if you follow their example you will perish.

Irena
I long to follow their example, and to die, that I may share their eternal joy.

Sisinnius
Yield, yield!

Irena
I will yield to no man who persuades me to sin.

Sisinnius
If you persist in your refusal, I shall not grant you a swift death. I shall eke it out and every day I shall increase and renew your torments.

Irena
The greater my pain, the greater my glory!

===Chinese===
西西尼乌斯
伊雷娜，以你姐姐们的下场为戒，发抖吧，你若步她们的后尘，必将灭亡。

伊雷娜
我渴望步她们的后尘，渴望死去，好分享她们永恒的喜乐。

西西尼乌斯
屈服吧，屈服吧！

伊雷娜
谁劝我犯罪，我绝不向谁屈服。

西西尼乌斯
你若执意拒绝，我就不赐你速死。我要把死拉长，天天加重、翻新你的痛苦。

伊雷娜
我的痛苦越大，我的荣耀越大！

===Original===
Sisinnius
You are not afraid of being tortured, I know, but I can use another means that will be abhorrent to you.

Irena
By Christ’s help I shall escape from all you can devise against me.

Sisinnius
I can send you to a house of ill-fame, where your body will be abominably defiled.

Irena
Better far that my body should suffer outrage than my soul.

Sisinnius
When you are dishonoured and forced to live among harlots, you can no longer be numbered among the virgins.

Irena
The wage of sin is death; the wage of suffering a crown. If the soul does not consent, there is not guilt.

===Chinese===
西西尼乌斯
我知道你不怕受刑，但我还有别的手段，那是你所憎恶的。

伊雷娜
靠基督的帮助，你所能谋算的一切都害不了我。

西西尼乌斯
我可以把你送进妓院，让你的身体蒙受可憎的玷污。

伊雷娜
我的身体受辱，远胜于我的灵魂受辱。

西西尼乌斯
你一旦受了玷污，被迫生活在娼妓中间，就再不能算在童贞女之列了。

伊雷娜
罪的工价乃是死，受苦的工价乃是冠冕。灵魂若不同意，便没有罪过。

===Original===
Sisinnius
In vain I try to spare her, and show pity to her youth!

Soldiers
We could have told you as much. She is not to be frightened, and nothing can make her worship the gods.

Sisinnius
I will show her no more mercy.

Soldiers
That is the only way to deal with her.

Sisinnius
Have no pity. Be rough with her, and drag her to the lowest brothel you can find.

Irena
They will never take me there.

===Chinese===
西西尼乌斯
我想饶过她、怜惜她的青春，全是白费！

士兵们
这话我们本可早就告诉您。吓不倒她，也没有什么能让她敬拜诸神。

西西尼乌斯
我再不对她留情。

士兵们
对付她就该这样。

西西尼乌斯
不必留情。对她下手狠些，把她拖到你们能找到的最下等妓院去。

伊雷娜
他们永远别想把我带到那里。

===Original===
Sisinnius
Indeed! What can prevent them?

Irena
The power that rules the world.

Sisinnius
We shall see.

Irena
Yes! Sooner than you will like!

Sisinnius
Soldiers, do not let the absurd prophecies of this woman interfere with your duty.

Soldiers
We are not likely to be frightened by a slip of a girl! We will carry out your orders at once.

===Chinese===
西西尼乌斯
是吗！什么能拦住他们？

伊雷娜
那统管万有的权能。

西西尼乌斯
走着瞧。

伊雷娜
好！比你乐意的要快！

西西尼乌斯
士兵们，别让这女子的荒唐预言妨碍了你们的差事。

士兵们
咱们可不会被一个黄毛丫头吓住！我们立刻执行您的命令。

#### Scene XIII / 第十三场

===Original===
Sisinnius
Who are these men hurrying towards us? They cannot be the soldiers who took away Irena. Yet they resemble them. Yes, these are the men! Why have you returned so suddenly? Why are you panting for breath?

Soldiers
We ran back to find you.

Sisinnius
Where is the girl?

Soldiers
On the crest of the mountain.

Sisinnius
What mountain?

Soldiers
The mountain yonder, nearest this place.

Sisinnius
O fools, madmen! Have you lost your senses?

Soldiers
What’s the matter? Why do you look at us so threateningly, and speak with such anger?

===Chinese===
西西尼乌斯
朝咱们跑来的这些人是谁？总不会是带走伊雷娜的那些士兵吧。可他们又很像。没错，正是他们！你们为什么突然回来了？为什么气喘吁吁？

士兵们
我们是跑回来找您的。

西西尼乌斯
那姑娘在哪儿？

士兵们
在山顶上。

西西尼乌斯
哪座山？

士兵们
就是那边那座，离这儿最近的山。

西西尼乌斯
啊，蠢货，疯子！你们失了魂吗？

士兵们
怎么了？您为什么这样凶地瞪着我们，这样怒气冲冲地说话？

===Original===
Sisinnius
May the gods crush you with their thunder!

Soldiers
What have we done? How have we offended? We have only obeyed your orders.

Sisinnius
Fools! Did I not tell you to take this rebellious girl to a brothel?

Soldiers
That is so, but while we were on the way up came two young strangers and told us you had sent them to take Irena to the summit of the mountain.

Sisinnius
I learn this for the first time from you.

Soldiers
So we see.

Sisinnius
What were these strangers like?

Soldiers
They were gorgeously dressed and looked like people of rank.

===Chinese===
西西尼乌斯
愿诸神用雷霆砸死你们！

士兵们
我们做错了什么？怎么冒犯了？我们不过是奉您的命令行事。

西西尼乌斯
蠢货！我不是吩咐你们把这忤逆的丫头送进妓院吗？

士兵们
是这样，可我们正走在路上，来了两个陌生的年轻人，说您派他们把伊雷娜带上山顶去。

西西尼乌斯
这事我还是头一回从你们嘴里听说。

士兵们
这我们看出来了。

西西尼乌斯
那两个生人什么样？

士兵们
他们衣着华贵，看着像有身份的人。

===Original===
Sisinnius
Did you not follow them?

Soldiers
Yes, we followed them.

Sisinnius
What did they do?

Soldiers
They placed themselves one on each side of Irena, and told us to hasten and tell you what we had seen.

Sisinnius
Then there is nothing to do but for me to mount my horse and ride to the mountain to discover who has dared to play us this trick.

Soldiers
We will come too.

===Chinese===
西西尼乌斯
你们没跟上他们？

士兵们
跟了，我们跟着他们。

西西尼乌斯
他们做了什么？

士兵们
他们一左一右站在伊雷娜身旁，叫我们赶紧回来把所见的一切告诉您。

西西尼乌斯
那我别无他法，只好上马赶往山去，看看到底是谁胆敢这样耍我们。

士兵们
我们也去。

#### Scene XIV / 第十四场

===Original===
Sisinnius
What has happened to me? These Christians have bewitched me. I wander blindly round this hill, and when I stumble on a path I can neither follow it nor return upon my steps.

Soldiers
We are all the sport of some strange enchantment. We are exhausted. If you let this madwoman live an hour longer it will be the death of us all.

Sisinnius
Take a bow one of you, bend it as far as you can, and loose a shaft that shall pierce this devilish witch.

Soldiers
That’s the way!

===Chinese===
西西尼乌斯
我这是怎么了？这些基督徒对我施了魔法。我围着这山丘盲目打转，偶然撞上一条小径，却既走不下去，也退不回来。

士兵们
咱们全都被某种怪异的魔法捉弄着。咱们都累垮了。您若让这疯女人再多活一个时辰，咱们全都没命了。

西西尼乌斯
你们谁取一张弓来，尽力拉满，放一箭射穿这魔鬼般的巫女。

士兵们
这才对！

===Original===
Irena
You wretched Sisinnius! Do you not blush for your shameful defeat? Are you not ashamed that you could not overcome the resolution of a little child without resorting to force of arms?

Sisinnius
I accept the shame gladly, since now I am sure of your death.

Irena
To me my death means joy, but to you calamity. For your cruelty you will be damned in Tartarus. But I shall receive the martyr’s palm, and, adorned with the crown of virginity, I shall enter the azure palace of the Eternal King, to Whom be glory and honour forever and ever!

===Chinese===
伊雷娜
你这可悲的西西尼乌斯！你那可耻的败绩不叫你脸红吗？你竟无法制服一个小孩子的决心，非得诉诸兵刃，不觉得羞愧吗？

西西尼乌斯
这羞辱我乐于领受，因为如今你的死是确凿的了。

伊雷娜
我的死对我意味着喜乐，对你却是灾殃。你将因你的残忍被打入塔耳塔洛斯（Tartarus）受罚。而我将领受殉道者的棕榈枝，戴着童贞的冠冕，进入永恒之王的碧霄宫殿。愿荣耀与尊贵归于他，世世无穷！

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. Since this was written, an English translation of one of the plays, *Abraham*, has been issued by a private press. ↩︎

===Chinese===
1. 自本文写成以来，其中一部剧作《亚伯拉罕》（*Abraham*）的英译本已由一家私人出版社印行。 ↩︎

===Original===
2. I have adopted this form of the name in preference to “Hrotsuitha,” “Hrotswitha,” or “Hrosvitha,” as being more easily pronounced and more pleasant to the eye. The name is said to be derived from the old Saxon word “Hrodsuind” (strong voice), a derivation accepted by Roswitha herself in her preface to her plays, when she writes “*ego, clamor validus Gandeishermensis*,” and approved by Grimm. ↩︎

===Chinese===
2. 我采用这个名字的此种拼法，而舍弃「Hrotsuitha」「Hrotswitha」或「Hrosvitha」，因其更易上口、更悦目。据说此名源自古萨克森语词「Hrodsuind」（强音）；罗斯维塔本人在其剧作序言中写下「*ego, clamor validus Gandeishermensis*」（我，甘德斯海姆的强音）时，便已认可这一词源，格林对此亦表赞同。 ↩︎

===Original===
3. Believing that the representation of the plays is possible, even desirable, I have also aimed at making the dialogue *speakable*. ↩︎

===Chinese===
3. 我相信这些剧本可以搬演，甚至值得搬演，因此我还力求使对白便于上口（*speakable*）。 ↩︎

===Original===
4. The manuscript is now in the Munich City Library. Recently another manuscript, containing four of the six dramas, is reported to have been discovered among the state archives of Cologne. (*Times* Berlin Correspondent, May 9, 1922.) ↩︎

===Chinese===
4. 该手稿今存慕尼黑市立图书馆。据报，最近在科隆的国家档案中又发现了一部手稿，载有六部剧作中的四部。（《泰晤士报》柏林通讯记者，1922 年 5 月 9 日。） ↩︎

===Original===
5. Since this was written *Callimachus* (translation by Arthur Waley) has been produced by the Art Theatre. *Paphnutius*, in my translation, was produced by Miss Edith Craig for the Pioneer Players at the Savoy Theatre on June 4, 1914, Miss Ellen Terry appearing in the part of the Abbess. ↩︎

===Chinese===
5. 自本文写成以来，《卡利马科斯》（*Callimachus*，亚瑟·韦利译本）已由艺术剧院搬演。拙译《帕夫努提乌斯》（*Paphnutius*）则于 1914 年 6 月 4 日由伊迪丝·克雷格小姐为先驱剧社（Pioneer Players）搬上萨伏伊剧院，埃伦·特里小姐饰演院长修女一角。 ↩︎

===Original===
6. Celtes prints this as part of the text; Magnin as a direction, on the ground that it is *introducuntur*, not *introducautur* in the MS. ↩︎

===Chinese===
6. 策尔蒂斯将此语排作正文的一部分；马尼南则排作舞台提示，理由是手稿中作 *introducuntur*（他们被引上场），而非 *introducautur*。 ↩︎

===Original===
7. Another “stage direction” omitted by Celtes. ↩︎

===Chinese===
7. 另一条被策尔蒂斯略去的「舞台提示」。 ↩︎

===Original===
8. This admonition to “spectators” is in the MS. and seems inexplicable if Roswitha wrote her plays to be read, not performed. ↩︎

===Chinese===
8. 这则对「观众」的训示见于手稿；倘若罗斯维塔写剧只为供人阅读而非演出，此语便似乎无从解释。 ↩︎

===Original===
9. When *Paphnutius* was acted, the dialogue of the “disciples” was allotted to several different actors, with the interesting result that some definite characters emerged. ↩︎

===Chinese===
9. 《帕夫努提乌斯》上演时，「众门徒」的对白分派给了数位不同的演员，结果颇有意味：几个轮廓分明的人物竟由此浮现出来。 ↩︎

===Original===
10. It has been my duty to preserve this rather tiresome numerical discourse, which no doubt Roswitha introduced to impress the “learned men” to whom she submitted her work, because it throws an interesting light on the studies pursued in such a monastery as Gandersheim in the 10th century. Equivalent modern English terms have been employed where the original, by change of usage, has become misleading. For example, “divisor” and “quotient” have been substituted for “denomination” and “quantity.” ↩︎

===Chinese===
10. 保留这段相当累赘的数理议论，是我的职责所在；罗斯维塔写入它，无疑是为了折服她呈送作品的那些「博学之士」，而这段文字恰好生动地映照出 10 世纪甘德斯海姆这类修道院中所研习的学问。凡因用法变迁而已致误解的原始用语，均已改用意义相当的现代英语术语；例如以「divisor」（除数）与「quotient」（商）替换了「denomination」与「quantity」。 ↩︎

## Gallicanus / 加利卡努斯

### Argument / 剧情提要

===Original===
The conversion of Gallicanus, Commander-in-Chief. On the eve of his departure for a campaign against the Scythians, Gallicanus is betrothed to the Emperor Constantine's daughter, Constance, a consecrated virgin.

When threatened with defeat in battle, Gallicanus is converted by John and Paul, Grand Almoners to Constance. He is immediately baptized and takes a vow of celibacy.

Later he is exiled by order of Julian the Apostate, and receives the crown of martyrdom. John and Paul are put to death by the same prince and buried secretly in their own house. Not long after, the son of their executioner becomes possessed by a devil. He is cured after confessing the crime committed by his father. He bears witness to the merits of the martyrs, and is baptized, together with his father.

===Chinese===
全军统帅加利卡努斯（Gallicanus）的皈依。加利卡努斯出征斯基泰人（Scythians）前夕，与皇帝君士坦丁（Constantine）之女、已誓守贞洁的康斯坦丝（Constance）订下婚约。

当战事危急、败局将临之时，加利卡努斯经康斯坦丝的两位大施赈官约翰（John）与保罗（Paul）劝化而皈依。他随即受洗，并立誓终身守贞。

后来他奉叛教者尤利安（Julian the Apostate）之命遭流放，终获殉道者的冠冕。约翰与保罗亦死于同一位君主之手，被秘密葬于自己家中。不久之后，行刑者之子被魔鬼附身。他在认明父亲所犯的罪行之后得以痊愈。他见证两位殉道者的功德，并与父亲一同受洗。

### Characters / 人物表

#### Characters in Part I / 第一部人物

===Original===
- The Emperor Constantine
- Gallicanus
- Constance, daughter of Constantine.
- Artemia, daughter of Gallicanus.
- Attica, daughter of Gallicanus.
- John, Grand Almoner to Constance.
- Paul, Grand Almoner to Constance.
- Lords of the Court
- Bradan, King of the Scythians.
- Tribunes
- Roman soldiers
- Scythian soldiers
- Helena, Mother of Constantine.

===Chinese===
- 皇帝君士坦丁
- 加利卡努斯
- 康斯坦丝，君士坦丁之女
- 阿尔忒弥娅（Artemia），加利卡努斯之女
- 阿提卡（Attica），加利卡努斯之女
- 约翰，康斯坦丝的大施赈官
- 保罗，康斯坦丝的大施赈官
- 廷臣
- 布拉丹（Bradan），斯基泰人之王
- 护民官
- 罗马士兵
- 斯基泰士兵
- 海伦娜（Helena），君士坦丁之母

#### Characters in Part II / 第二部人物

===Original===
- Julian the Apostate
- Gallicanus
- Terentianus
- John
- Paul
- Consuls
- Christians
- Soliders

===Chinese===
- 叛教者尤利安
- 加利卡努斯
- 泰伦提亚努斯（Terentianus）
- 约翰
- 保罗
- 执政官
- 基督徒
- 士兵

### Part I / 第一部

#### Scene I / 第一场

===Original===
Constantine
Gallicanus, this tries my patience. You have delayed the offensive against the Scythians too long. The only nation which boldly resists our power and refuses to make peace with Rome! You know well enough that you were chosen because of your energy in your country's service.

Gallicanus
Most noble Constantine, I have served you hand and foot, ungrudgingly, devotedly, and have always striven to repay your trust in me with deeds. I have never shirked any task.

Constantine
Is there any need to remind me? As if your great services were not always in mind! I spoke, not to reproach you, but to urge you to act quickly.

Gallicanus
I will set out at once.

Constantine
I am rejoiced to hear it.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
加利卡努斯，你这是在考验我的耐心。你对斯基泰人的攻势拖延得太久了。唯独这个邦国，胆敢顽抗我们的威权，拒绝与罗马缔和！你心里清楚，选中你，正是看重你为国效力的魄力。

加利卡努斯
最尊贵的君士坦丁，我为您尽心竭力，毫无怨言，一片赤诚，始终力求以实绩报答您对我的信任。我从未推诿过任何使命。

君士坦丁
何须你来提醒我？莫非你的赫赫功勋不曾时刻挂在我心上！我那番话并非责备，只是催你从速行动。

加利卡努斯
我即刻启程。

君士坦丁
闻此言，我心甚慰。

===Original===
Gallicanus
I am ready to obey your orders if it costs me my life.

Constantine
Your zeal pleases me. I appreciate your devotion.

Gallicanus
As both are immense should they not be rewarded on the same scale?

Constantine
That is only fair.

Gallicanus
It is easier for a man to undertake a difficult enterprise when he is sustained by the knowledge that his reward is sure.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
纵然赔上性命，我也甘愿遵奉您的号令。

君士坦丁
你的热忱令我欣悦。你的忠心我深为赞赏。

加利卡努斯
既然二者皆如此深重，难道不该以同等的尺度酬报吗？

君士坦丁
那是理所当然。

加利卡努斯
人若确知必有酬报，便更容易承担艰难的事业。

===Original===
Constantine
Naturally.

Gallicanus
I beg you then to promise me now my prize for this dangerous undertaking. In hard and strenuous fighting, when it seems as if I must be defeated, the thought of this reward will give me new strength.

Constantine
The reward deemed by the Senate the most glorious a man can desire has never been withheld from you, and never shall be. You enjoy the freedom of my court, and the highest honour among those who surround me.

Gallicanus
I know, but I am not thinking of that.

Constantine
If you have other ambitions, you must tell me.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
这是自然。

加利卡努斯
那么我恳求您现在就许诺我此番冒险的酬赏。在艰苦鏖战、眼看败局将成之际，对这份酬报的念想会赋予我新的力量。

君士坦丁
元老院公认为人生所能企望的最光荣的酬赏，从不曾亏待于你，将来也决不亏待。你得以自由出入我的宫廷，在我左右之人中享有至高的尊荣。

加利卡努斯
我知道，但我心中所想并非这些。

君士坦丁
你若有别的抱负，必须告诉我。

===Original===
Gallicanus
I have.

Constantine
What are they?

Gallicanus
Dare I tell you?

Constantine
Of course!

Gallicanus
You will be angry.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
确有。

君士坦丁
是什么？

加利卡努斯
我敢说吗？

君士坦丁
尽管说！

加利卡努斯
您会动怒的。

===Original===
Constantine
Not at all!

Gallicanus
You are sure?

Constantine
Quite sure.

Gallicanus
We shall see. I say you will be indignant.

Constantine
Your fears are groundless. Come! Speak!

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
绝不会！

加利卡努斯
您确定？

君士坦丁
十分确定。

加利卡努斯
那我们走着瞧。我说您定会愤然。

君士坦丁
你的顾虑毫无根据。来吧！说吧！

===Original===
Gallicanus
Since you command me, I will. I love Constance. I love your daughter.

Constantine
That is well. You do right to love the daughter of your sovereign. Your love honours her.

Gallicanus
You say this to cut me short.

Constantine
Not so.

Gallicanus
I wish to marry her. Will you give your consent?

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
既然您命我开口，我便说了。我爱康斯坦丝。我爱您的女儿。

君士坦丁
这很好。你爱上君王之女，实为应当。你的爱为她增光。

加利卡努斯
您这样说，不过是想打断我的话。

君士坦丁
并非如此。

加利卡努斯
我要娶她为妻。您肯应允吗？

===Original===
Constantine
He asks no small thing, my lords. This is an honour of which none of you have ever dreamed.

Gallicanus
Alas! I foresaw this. He scorns me. *(To the Lords)* Intercede for me, I implore you.

The Lords
Most illustrious Emperor, we beg you to be generous. Remember his services, and do not turn a deaf ear to his request.

Constantine
I have not done so, but it is my duty first to make sure that my daughter consents.

The Lords
That is only reasonable.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
诸位大人，他所求的可不是小事。这是你们谁都不曾梦见的荣宠。

加利卡努斯
唉！我早料到会如此。他轻蔑我。（向众廷臣）我恳求你们，为我求情。

众廷臣
最显赫的陛下，我们恳请您宽宏大度。念他历年功勋，莫对他的请求置若罔闻。

君士坦丁
我并未置之不理，只是我须先弄清我的女儿是否应允，这是为父的本分。

众廷臣
这也在情理之中。

===Original===
Constantine
I will go to her, and, if such is your wish, Gallicanus, I will lay the project before her.

Gallicanus
It is my wish.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
我这就去见她。加利卡努斯，倘若这是你的意愿，我便将此事向她陈明。

加利卡努斯
正合我愿。

#### Scene II / 第二场

===Original===
Constance
Our Lord the Emperor approaches. He looks strangely grave and sad. What can it mean?

Constantine
Constance, my child, come nearer. I wish to speak to you.

Constance
I am here, my lord. Command me.

Constantine
I am in great distress of mind. My heart is heavy.

Constance
As you came in I saw that you were sad, and without knowing the reason I was troubled.

===Chinese===
康斯坦丝
皇帝陛下驾临了。他神色异常凝重而忧伤。这意味着什么呢？

君士坦丁
康斯坦丝，我的孩子，走近些。我有话对你说。

康斯坦丝
我在这里，陛下。请吩咐。

君士坦丁
我心中愁苦至极。心头沉重。

康斯坦丝
您一进来我便看出您面带忧色，虽然不知缘由，心里也已不安。

===Original===
Constantine
It is on your account.

Constance
On my account?

Constantine
Yes.

Constance
You frighten me. What is it, my lord?

Constantine
The fear of grieving you ties my tongue.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
正是为了你。

康斯坦丝
为了我？

君士坦丁
是的。

康斯坦丝
您吓着我了。究竟何事，陛下？

君士坦丁
惟恐使你伤心，我如鲠在喉。

===Original===
Constance
You will grieve me more by keeping silence.

Constantine
Gallicanus, my General, whose victories have won him the first place among the princes of my realm—Gallicanus, whose sword is necessary for the defence of the Empire—Gallicanus—

Constance
What of him?

Constantine
He wants to make you his wife.

Constance
Me?

Constantine
Yes.

Constance
I would rather die.

===Chinese===
康斯坦丝
您若缄默不言，只会更令我伤心。

君士坦丁
加利卡努斯，我的统帅，他的战功使他在我国诸王公之中位居第一——加利卡努斯，他的剑是保卫帝国所必需——加利卡努斯——

康斯坦丝
他怎么了？

君士坦丁
他想娶你为妻。

康斯坦丝
娶我？

君士坦丁
是的。

康斯坦丝
我宁死不从。

===Original===
Constantine
I knew that would be your answer.

Constance
It cannot surprise you, as it was with your consent and approval that I consecrated myself to God.

Constantine
I have not forgotten.

Constance
I will keep my vow inviolate. Nothing can ever force me to break it.

Constantine
I know you are right, and the greater my difficulty. For if, as is my duty as your father, I permit you to be faithful to your vow, as a sovereign I shall suffer for it. Yet were I to oppose your resolution—which God forbid!—I should deserve eternal punishment.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
我料到你会这样回答。

康斯坦丝
这想必不令您意外，当初正是在您的首肯与嘉许之下，我把自己奉献给了上帝。

君士坦丁
我没有忘记。

康斯坦丝
我必持守我的誓愿，永不违犯。无论什么都不能迫我背弃它。

君士坦丁
我知道你有理，可这更增我的为难。因为倘若我尽为父之责，容你忠于誓愿，作为君王我便要为此付出代价。然而我若违逆你的决心——但愿上帝不许！——我便当受永罚。

===Original===
Constance
If I despaired of divine help I should be more wretched than you.

Constantine
That is true.

Constance
But a heart which trusts in God's goodness is armed against sorrow.

Constantine
You speak well, my Constance.

Constance
My lord, if you will deign to listen to my advice, I can show you how to escape this double danger.

===Chinese===
康斯坦丝
我若对上帝的助佑绝望，就会比您更加悲苦。

君士坦丁
这话不错。

康斯坦丝
但一颗信靠上帝美善的心，自有盔甲抵挡忧伤。

君士坦丁
你说得对，我的康斯坦丝。

康斯坦丝
陛下，您若肯屈尊听我一言，我便可教您如何避开这双重危局。

===Original===
Constantine
Oh, that you could!

Constance
You must pretend that you are willing to grant Gallicanus what he asks when the war has been won. Make him believe that I agree. Persuade him to leave with me during his absence at the war his two daughters, Attica and Anemia, as pledges of the bond of love which is to unite us. Tell him that in return I will send with him on his expedition my two Almoners, John and Paul.

Constantine
And if he should return victorious? What then?

Constance
We must pray the Father of us all that he will change his mind.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
但愿真能如此！

康斯坦丝
您须佯作情愿在战事告捷之后，允准加利卡努斯所求。让他相信我已应允。再劝他出征期间将他的两个女儿阿提卡与阿尔忒弥娅留在我身边，作为缔结我们爱情盟约的信物。并告诉他，作为回报，我的两位施赈官约翰与保罗将随他一同出征。

君士坦丁
倘若他凯旋而归呢？那时又当如何？

康斯坦丝
我们只能祈求众人之父，让他回心转意。

===Original===
Constantine
My daughter, my daughter! Your sweet words have softened the harshness of your father's grief! Henceforth I will not give way to anxiety.

Constance
There is no need.

Constantine
I will return to Gallicanus and satisfy him with this promise.

Constance
Go in peace, my lord.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
我的女儿，我的女儿！你甜美的话语化开了你父亲愁苦的坚冰！从今以后，我不再忧心忡忡。

康斯坦丝
无须忧虑。

君士坦丁
我这就回到加利卡努斯那里，以此承诺使他安心。

康斯坦丝
愿您平安而去，陛下。

#### Scene III / 第三场

===Original===
Gallicanus
O princes, I die of impatience to learn what has come of this long conference between our august sovereign and his daughter.

The Lords
He promised to plead your cause.

Gallicanus
Oh, that his arguments may prevail!

The Lords
Maybe they will.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
诸位大人，我们威严的陛下与他的女儿密谈良久，我心急如焚，只盼知道结果如何。

众廷臣
他答应为你陈情。

加利卡努斯
但愿他的劝说奏效！

众廷臣
或许会的。

===Original===
Gallicanus
Peace! Silence all of you! The Emperor comes. His face is not anxious as when he left us, but serene and glad.

The Lords
A good omen!

Gallicanus
It is said that the face is the mirror of the soul. If this be true, the calm joy in his reflects a kindly mood.

The Lords
We trust so.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
静一静！都别作声！皇帝来了。他面容不像离去时那般忧虑，而是安详而欣悦。

众廷臣
吉兆！

加利卡努斯
人说面容是心灵的镜子。若果真如此，他脸上的恬静喜色，正反映出一片好意。

众廷臣
但愿如此。

#### Scene IV / 第四场

===Original===
Constantine
Gallicanus!

Gallicanus
What did he say?

The Lords
Forward, forward. He is asking for you.

Gallicanus
Now the good gods help me!

Constantine
Gallicanus, set out for the war with an easy mind. On your return you shall receive the reward you covet.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
加利卡努斯！

加利卡努斯
他说了什么？

众廷臣
快去，快去。他正叫你。

加利卡努斯
愿善良的诸神护佑我！

君士坦丁
加利卡努斯，安心出征吧。待你归来，必得你所渴求的酬赏。

===Original===
Gallicanus
This is not a jest?

Constantine
How can you ask?

Gallicanus
I should be happy indeed if I could know one thing.

Constantine
What may that be?

Gallicanus
Her answer.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
这不是戏言？

君士坦丁
你怎么能这样问？

加利卡努斯
倘若能确知一事，我便真正欢喜了。

君士坦丁
何事？

加利卡努斯
她的答复。

===Original===
Constantine
My daughter's answer?

Gallicanus
Yes. What did she say?

Constantine
It is unreasonable to expect a young maid to answer in so many words. Events will prove that she consents.

Gallicanus
If I could be assured of that, I should trouble little about the manner of her answer.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
我女儿的答复？

加利卡努斯
正是。她怎么说？

君士坦丁
要一位少女明言答复，未免不合情理。事态自会证明她是应允的。

加利卡努斯
只要能确知这一点，她以何种方式答复，我并不介意。

===Original===
Constantine
You want proof?

Gallicanus
I hunger for it.

Constantine
Then listen. She has given orders that her Almoners, John and Paul, shall stay with you until the day of your nuptials.

Gallicanus
And her reason?

Constantine
That by constant intercourse with them you may learn to know how she lives—her habits and her tastes.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
你要凭证？

加利卡努斯
我如饥似渴。

君士坦丁
那就听着。她已吩咐，她的两位施赈官约翰与保罗将伴你左右，直到你完婚之日。

加利卡努斯
她的理由是？

君士坦丁
让你与他们朝夕相处，从而得知她的生活——她的习性与好恶。

===Original===
Gallicanus
An excellent plan, and one that pleases me beyond measure.

Constantine
She would like you in return to allow your two young daughters to live with her for the same period. She thinks she can learn from them how to please you.

Gallicanus
Oh, joy, joy! All things are falling out as I wished.

Constantine
Send for your daughters without delay.

Gallicanus
Are my soldiers still there? Come, fellows, hasten! Run to my daughters and bring them to their sovereign's presence.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
妙策，实在令我欣喜万分。

君士坦丁
作为回报，她也愿你让两个年幼的女儿在同一时期住到她那里。她想从她们那里学得如何取悦于你。

加利卡努斯
啊，欢喜，欢喜！诸事正遂我心愿。

君士坦丁
即刻派人接你的女儿们来。

加利卡努斯
我的士兵们还在吗？来，弟兄们，快去！跑到我女儿们那里，领她们前来觐见她们的主上。

#### Scene V / 第五场

===Original===
Soldiers
Most noble Constance, the illustrious daughters of Gallicanus are here. They are beautiful, wise and virtuous, and in every way worthy of your friendship.

Constance
They are welcome. *They are introduced with ceremony.*6 O Christ, lover of virginity and fount of chastity! Thou Who through the intercession of Thy holy martyr Agnes hast preserved my body from stain and my mind from pagan errors! Thou Who hast shown me as an example Thy Mother's virgin bed where Thou didst manifest Thyself true God! Thou Who before time began wast born of God the Father, and in the fullness of time wast born again true man, of a mother's womb—I implore Thee, true Wisdom, co-eternal with the Father, the Creator, Upholder and Governor of the Universe, to grant my prayer! May Gallicanus, who seeks to gain the love which I can give only to Thee, be turned from his unlawful purpose. Take his daughters to Thyself, and pour the sweetness of Thy love into their hearts that they may despise all carnal bonds, and be admitted to the blessed company of virgins who are consecrated to Thee!

Artemia
Hail, most noble Constance! Imperial highness, hail!

Constance
Greeting, my sisters, Artemia and Attica. Stand up, stand up! No, do not kneel. Salute me rather with a loving kiss.

Artemia
We come joyfully to offer you our homage, lady. We are ready to serve you with our whole hearts, and we seek no reward but your love.

===Chinese===
众士兵
最尊贵的康斯坦丝，加利卡努斯两位贤德的女儿到了。她们美丽、聪慧而贞静，方方面面都配得上您的友谊。

康斯坦丝
欢迎她们。（她们在隆重的仪礼中被引上。）6 基督啊，童贞之爱慕者，贞洁之泉源！您借着圣殉道者阿格尼丝（Agnes）的代祷，保全了我的身体不受玷污、我的心灵不受异教谬误！您将您母亲的童贞之榻显示给我作榜样，在那里您显明自己为真神！您在时间肇始之前由上帝父所生，及至时期满足，又由母亲之胎降生为真人——真智慧啊，与父同永恒、宇宙的创造者、扶持者与统御者，我恳求您应允我的祈祷！愿那企图攫取我只能给您之爱的加利卡努斯，从不义的心念中回转。求您将他的女儿们收纳归于您自己，把您之爱的甘甜倾注于她们心中，使她们鄙弃一切肉体的羁绊，得以加入那奉献于您的童贞者的福乐行列！

阿尔忒弥娅
万福，最尊贵的康斯坦丝！帝国殿下，万福！

康斯坦丝
欢迎你们，我的姊妹阿尔忒弥娅与阿提卡。起来，起来！不，不必跪拜。请以亲爱的吻向我致意吧。

阿尔忒弥娅
夫人，我们欢欢喜喜前来向您致敬。我们甘愿全心全意服侍您，所求的报偿惟有您的爱。

===Original===
Constance
We have one Lord Who is in heaven. He alone should be served like that. We owe Him a love and fidelity which must be shown not only with whole hearts but with whole bodies. That is if we would enter His kingdom with the virgin's palm.

Artemia
We do not question this. You will find us eager to obey you in all things, but never so eager as when you exhort us to confess our faith and keep our vow of purity.

Constance
That is a good answer, and one worthy of a noble mind. I see that through divine grace you already have the faith.

Artemia
How could we poor idolators have any good thought if light had not been given us from above?

Constance
The strength of your faith makes me hope that Gallicanus too will believe some day.

Artemia
He has only to be taught. Then he must believe.

Constance
Send for John and Paul.

===Chinese===
康斯坦丝
我们只有一位主在天上。惟独祂配得那样的服侍。我们欠祂的爱与忠贞，不仅当以全心彰显，也当以全身彰显——倘若我们愿手持童贞的棕榈枝进入祂的国度，就当如此。

阿尔忒弥娅
这我们毫不怀疑。您将看到我们乐于在凡事上顺服您，而最乐意的，莫过于您劝勉我们宣认信仰、持守贞洁之愿的时候。

康斯坦丝
这回答很好，配得一颗高贵的心灵。我看出你们已借着神圣的恩典怀有了信仰。

阿尔忒弥娅
若不是上天赐下光来，我们这些可怜的拜偶像者又怎能有半点善念呢？

康斯坦丝
你们信仰的力量使我希望，加利卡努斯有朝一日也会相信。

阿尔忒弥娅
只消有人教导他。那时他必然相信。

康斯坦丝
去请约翰与保罗来。

#### Scene VI / 第六场

===Original===
John
You sent for us, Highness. We are here.

Constance
Go at once to Gallicanus and attach yourselves to his person. Instruct him little by little in the mysteries of our faith. Perhaps God means to make us the instruments of winning him to His service.

Paul
God give us success! We shall do all we can.

===Chinese===
约翰
殿下召见我们，我们在此。

康斯坦丝
即刻到加利卡努斯那里去，随侍在他身旁。将我们信仰的奥义一点一点教导他。或许上帝有意借我们之手，使他归入祂的事奉。

保罗
愿上帝赐我们成功！我们必竭尽全力。

#### Scene VII / 第七场

===Original===
Gallicanus
You are welcome, John—and you, Paul. I have awaited your coming with impatience.

John
As soon as we received our lady's commands we hastened at once to put ourselves at your service.

Gallicanus
Your offer to serve me gives me a pleasure that nothing else could give.

Paul
That is natural, for, as the saying goes, "The friends of our friends are our friends."

Gallicanus
A true saying.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
欢迎你们，约翰——还有你，保罗。我望眼欲穿地等候你们的到来。

约翰
我们一领受夫人的命令，便即刻赶来听候您的差遣。

加利卡努斯
你们愿事奉我，这份心意给我的欢愉，是别的任何事物都不能给的。

保罗
这是自然，正如俗话所说：「朋友的朋友，便是我们的朋友。」

加利卡努斯
确是至理。

===Original===
John
The love our lady bears you assures us of your goodwill.

Gallicanus
You can rely on it. Come, tribunes and centurions, assemble the troops. Soldiers in my command, I present to you John and Paul, for whose arrival our departure has been delayed.

Tribunes
Lead us on. *The tribunes gather round Gallicanus.*7

Gallicanus
We must first go to the Capitol, and visit the temples to propitiate the gods with the customary sacrifices. That is the way to obtain success for our arms.

Tribunes
That is certain.

John
Let us withdraw for a time.

Paul
We cannot do otherwise.

===Chinese===
约翰
我们夫人对您的爱，使我们确信您的好意。

加利卡努斯
尽管放心。来吧，护民官与百夫长们，集合部队。受我节制的士兵们，我向你们引见约翰与保罗，正是为了等候他们到来，我们的启程才一再延误。

众护民官
请带领我们前进。（众护民官围拢到加利卡努斯身旁。）7

加利卡努斯
我们必须先前往卡皮托利诺山（Capitol），到各神庙中循惯例献祭，以求取悦诸神。如此方能保佑我军旗开得胜。

众护民官
这是自然。

约翰
我们且退避一时。

保罗
也只能如此。

#### Scene VIII / 第八场

===Original===
John
The General is leaving the temple. Let us mount our horses and ride to meet him.

Paul
This moment.

Gallicanus
I noticed you were not with us. Where have you been?

John
We were seeing to our baggage. We have sent it on ahead that we may ride with you unencumbered.

Gallicanus
Well planned!

===Chinese===
约翰
统帅正离开神庙。我们上马迎他去吧。

保罗
即刻便去。

加利卡努斯
我留意到你们没与我们同行。你们到哪里去了？

约翰
我们照看行装去了。已将辎重先行送去，这样便可轻装与您同行。

加利卡努斯
安排得好！

#### Scene IX / 第九场

===Original===
Gallicanus
By Jupiter, tribunes, I see the legions of an immense army advancing! The diversity of their arms is enough to make the stoutest heart tremble.

Tribunes
By Hercules, the enemy!

Gallicanus
Let us resist with courage, and show them we are men!

Tribunes
It is useless to attempt resistance to such a host.

Gallicanus
What, then, do you propose?

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
以朱庇特起誓，护民官们，我看见一支大军的无数军团正开进过来！他们兵械之纷繁，足以令最坚毅的心为之战栗。

众护民官
以赫拉克勒斯起誓，是敌人！

加利卡努斯
让我们奋勇抵抗，让他们看看我们是堂堂男儿！

众护民官
面对如此大军，抵抗无益。

加利卡努斯
那么，你们有何良策？

===Original===
Tribunes
Surrender.

Gallicanus
Apollo forbid!

Tribunes
By Pollux, we must surrender! See, we are surrounded on every side—we are being mown down—we perish!

Gallicanus
Ye gods! What will happen if the tribunes refuse to obey me, and surrender?

John
Promise you will become a Christian, and you will conquer.

===Chinese===
众护民官
投降。

加利卡努斯
阿波罗不容！

众护民官
以波吕克斯起誓，我们只能投降了！看，我们四面受敌——将士纷纷倒下——我们要完了！

加利卡努斯
诸神啊！护民官们若抗命投降，局面将何以收拾？

约翰
您许愿皈依基督，就必得胜。

===Original===
Gallicanus
I swear! And I will keep my vow.

One of the Enemy
Woe to us, King Bradan! Fortune, who but now promised us victory, was mocking us. Our men are weakening, their strength is exhausted—they have lost heart and are giving up the struggle.

Bradan
I am uncertain what to do. A strange faintheartedness has seized me also. There is but one course—we must surrender.

The Enemy
There is nothing else to do.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
我起誓！我必守此愿。

敌军一人
吾王布拉丹，我们完了！命运女神方才还许给我们胜利，原来只是在嘲弄我们。我们的将士正在衰弱，气力耗尽——他们已丧失斗志，正在放弃战斗。

布拉丹
我不知所措。一种莫名的怯懦也攫住了我。只有一条路——我们只能投降。

敌军众兵
别无他法。

===Original===
Bradan
Gallicanus, do not destroy us! Be merciful! Spare our lives and do with us what you will.

Gallicanus
Have no fear. There is no need to tremble. Give me hostages, acknowledge yourselves tributaries of the Emperor, and you shall live happy under a Roman peace.

Bradan
You have only to name the number and rank of the hostages, and the tribute to be exacted.

===Chinese===
布拉丹
加利卡努斯，不要毁灭我们！发发慈悲！饶我们性命，任凭你处置。

加利卡努斯
不必惧怕。无须战栗。交出人质，承认你们是皇帝的臣属，你们便可在罗马的和平之下安乐度日。

布拉丹
人质的数目与身份、贡赋的数额，全凭您定夺。

===Original===
Gallicanus
Soldiers, lay down arms. Slay no one, wound no one, but embrace as friends these men whom you had to fight as enemies of the Empire.

John
How much more powerful is one fervent prayer than all the pride of man!

Gallicanus
That is true indeed.

Paul
What mighty succour God in His mercy sends to those who humbly trust in Him!

Gallicanus
I have had good proof of it.

John
But the promise made when the storm was raging must be kept now it is calm.

Gallicanus
I agree. It is my wish to be baptized as soon as possible, and to devote the rest of my life to the service of God.

Paul
You are right.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
士兵们，放下武器。勿杀一人，勿伤一人，要把这些你们不得不当作帝国之敌而与之交战的人，当作朋友去拥抱。

约翰
一次热切的祈祷，远比人的一切骄傲更有力量！

加利卡努斯
千真万确。

保罗
上帝以何等的仁慈，将何等大能的援助赐给谦卑信靠祂的人！

加利卡努斯
我已有切身的印证。

约翰
然而风浪之中所许的愿，如今风平浪静，便当践行。

加利卡努斯
我同意。但愿尽早受洗，将余生奉献于事奉上帝。

保罗
您说得对。

#### Scene X / 第十场

===Original===
Gallicanus
Look! That vast crowd of citizens has gathered to see our entry into Rome! See how they flock to acclaim us, bearing according to custom the symbols of victory!

John
It is only natural.

Gallicanus
Yet the glorious victory was not won by my valour nor by the help of their gods.

John
No, assuredly; the glory belongs to the one true God.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
看啊！那么多公民聚集起来，观瞻我们进入罗马！看他们如何循着习俗、手持胜利的象征，簇拥着向我们欢呼！

约翰
这是自然。

加利卡努斯
然而这光荣的胜利，既非凭我的勇武，也非靠他们的神明之助。

约翰
确实不是；荣耀属于独一的真神。

===Original===
Gallicanus
That being so, we must pass the temples without going in.

John
A wise decision.

Gallicanus
And instead make a humble confession of faith in the Church of the Apostles.

Paul
O happy man! And most happy thought! In this you show yourself a true Christian.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
既然如此，我们路过诸神庙，不可进去。

约翰
明智的决定。

加利卡努斯
而是要到使徒教堂去谦卑地宣认信仰。

保罗
有福的人！更是有福的念头！由此你显明自己是一个真正的基督徒。

#### Scene XI / 第十一场

===Original===
Constantine
I am greatly astonished, soldiers, that Gallicanus should be so long in presenting himself before his sovereign.

Soldiers
The moment he arrived in Rome he went to the Church of Saint Peter, and, prostrating himself on the ground, gave thanks to the Almighty for giving him the victory.

Constantine
Gallicanus?

Soldiers
It is true.

Constantine
Impossible!

Soldiers
Here he comes. You can ask him yourself.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
士兵们，加利卡努斯迟迟不来觐见他的主上，我深感诧异。

众士兵
他一到罗马，便径直前往圣彼得教堂，俯伏在地，感谢全能者赐他胜利。

君士坦丁
加利卡努斯？

众士兵
确是如此。

君士坦丁
难以置信！

众士兵
他来了。您可亲自问他。

#### Scene XII / 第十二场

===Original===
Constantine
Welcome, Gallicanus! I have awaited your arrival with impatience. I long to hear from your own lips how the battle went and how it ended.

Gallicanus
I will tell you the whole story.

Constantine
Wait a moment, for even the battle is of small importance compared with the one thing which I want most to hear.

Gallicanus
What may that be?

Constantine
On your departure for the war you visited the temple of the gods; on your return you went to the Church of the Apostles. Why?

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
欢迎你，加利卡努斯！我望眼欲穿地等候你到来。渴望听你亲口讲述战事经过与结局。

加利卡努斯
我将把全部经过告诉您。

君士坦丁
且慢，因为就连战事，与我最想听的那件事相比，也是小事。

加利卡努斯
那是何事？

君士坦丁
你出征时拜的是诸神之庙；归来时去的却是使徒教堂。这是为何？

===Original===
Gallicanus
You ask that?

Constantine
Have I not told you, man, that I wish to know above all things!

Gallicanus
I will explain.

Constantine
Proceed, I beg you.

Gallicanus
Most Sacred Emperor, I confess I visited the temples on my departure, as you have said, and humbly sought the help of gods and demons.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
您要问这个？

君士坦丁
你这个人啊，我岂不曾告诉过你，此事我比什么都想知道！

加利卡努斯
我这就解释。

君士坦丁
请讲，我求之不得。

加利卡努斯
至圣的陛下，我承认，如您所言，出征时我拜访了诸神庙，谦卑地祈求过诸神与鬼魔的相助。

===Original===
Constantine
According to the old Roman custom.

Gallicanus
To my thinking, a bad custom.

Constantine
I am of the same mind.

Gallicanus
Then the tribunes arrived with their legions and we began our march.

Constantine
You set out from Rome with great pomp.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
这是循罗马的旧例。

加利卡努斯
依我之见，是个恶例。

君士坦丁
我亦作此想。

加利卡努斯
随后护民官们率各军团抵达，我们便开拔了。

君士坦丁
你们离罗马时排场盛大。

===Original===
Gallicanus
We pushed on, met the enemy, engaged them, and were defeated.

Constantine
Romans defeated!

Gallicanus
Routed.

Constantine
When was such a disaster ever known in our history!

Gallicanus
Once again I offered those hideous sacrifices, but what god came to my help? The fury of the enemy redoubled, and great numbers of my men were slain.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
我们挺进，遭遇敌军，与之交战，结果战败。

君士坦丁
罗马人战败了！

加利卡努斯
溃败。

君士坦丁
我们的历史上何曾有过这样的灾难！

加利卡努斯
我又献上那些可憎的祭品，可有什么神来助我？敌军的凶焰倍增，我的部下大批被杀。

===Original===
Constantine
I am amazed.

Gallicanus
It was then that the tribunes, disregarding my orders, began to surrender.

Constantine
To the enemy?

Gallicanus
To the enemy.

Constantine
And what did you do?

Gallicanus
What could I do but take to flight?

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
我大为骇异。

加利卡努斯
就在那时，护民官们无视我的号令，开始投降。

君士坦丁
向敌人？

加利卡努斯
向敌人。

君士坦丁
那你怎么办？

加利卡努斯
我除了逃命还能怎样？

===Original===
Constantine
Impossible!

Gallicanus
It is true.

Constantine
What anguish for a man of your courage!

Gallicanus
The sharpest.

Constantine
And how did you escape?

Gallicanus
My faithful companions, John and Paul, advised me to make a vow to the Creator.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
难以置信！

加利卡努斯
是实情。

君士坦丁
像你这样英勇的人，何等痛楚！

加利卡努斯
痛彻肺腑。

君士坦丁
那你是如何脱身的？

加利卡努斯
我忠实的同伴约翰与保罗，劝我向创造者许愿。

===Original===
Constantine
Good advice.

Gallicanus
I found it so. Hardly had I opened my lips to make the vow than I received help from heaven.

Constantine
How?

Gallicanus
A young man of immense stature appeared before me carrying a cross on his shoulder. He bade me follow him sword in hand.

Constantine
This young man, whoever he was, was sent from heaven.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
良策。

加利卡努斯
果然如此。我开口许愿的话音未落，便得到了上天的援助。

君士坦丁
怎样的援助？

加利卡努斯
一位身形魁伟的青年显现在我面前，肩上扛着十字架。他吩咐我手持剑跟随他。

君士坦丁
这位青年，不论他是谁，必是上天所差。

===Original===
Gallicanus
So it proved. At the same moment I saw at my side some soldiers whose faces were strange to me. They promised me their help.

Constantine
The host of Heaven!

Gallicanus
I am sure of it. Following in the steps of my guide, I advanced fearlessly into the midst of the enemy until I came face to face with their King, by name Bradan. Suddenly overcome by the strangest terror he threw himself at my feet, surrendered with his whole army, and promised to pay tribute in perpetuity to the ruler of the Roman world.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
果真如此。同一时刻，我看见身旁多了一些陌生的士兵。他们应允助我。

君士坦丁
是天军！

加利卡努斯
我确信无疑。我跟随引路者的脚步，无所畏惧地直捣敌阵中心，直到与他们的王——名叫布拉丹的——当面相遇。他蓦地被一种莫可名状的恐怖所慑，扑倒在我脚下，率全军投降，并允诺永远向罗马世界的主宰纳贡。

===Original===
Constantine
Now praise be to Him Who gave us this victory. Those who put their trust in Him will never be confounded.

Gallicanus
My experience witnesses to it.

Constantine
And now I should like to know what became of the treacherous tribunes?

Gallicanus
They hastened to implore my forgiveness.

Constantine
And you showed them mercy?

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
赞美那赐给我们这场胜利的主吧。信靠祂的人必不至于羞愧。

加利卡努斯
我的经历便是见证。

君士坦丁
现在我想知道，那些背信的护民官下场如何？

加利卡努斯
他们急忙恳求我的宽恕。

君士坦丁
你宽恕了他们？

===Original===
Gallicanus
I show mercy to men who had abandoned me in the hour of peril and surrendered to the enemy against my orders! No, assuredly!

Constantine
What did you do?

Gallicanus
I offered to pardon them on one condition.

Constantine
What condition?

Gallicanus
I told them that those who consented to become Christians would be allowed to retain their rank, and might even receive fresh honours, but that those who refused would not be pardoned, and would be degraded.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
要我怜悯那些在危难之际弃我而去、抗命降敌之徒？不，断然不能！

君士坦丁
那你怎么处置的？

加利卡努斯
我提出，满足一个条件便赦免他们。

君士坦丁
什么条件？

加利卡努斯
我告诉他们：凡愿意成为基督徒的，可保留军职，甚至还能获得新的荣升；凡拒绝的，则不予赦免，且遭贬黜。

===Original===
Constantine
A fair proposition, and honourable to the leader who made it.

Gallicanus
For my own part, purified in the waters of baptism, I have surrendered myself completely to the will of God. I am ready to renounce even your daughter, whom I love more than anything in the world. I wish to abstain from marriage that I may devote myself wholly to the service of the Virgin's Son.

Constantine
Come near, nearer yet, and let me fold you in my arms! Now, Gallicanus, the time has come for me to tell you what up to now I have been obliged to keep secret.

Gallicanus
What is it?

Constantine
My daughter, and your own two also, have chosen the same holy path which you yourself wish to follow.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
公正的提议，提出它的统帅亦因此增光。

加利卡努斯
至于我自己，我已在洗礼之水中得洁净，已将自己全然交托于上帝的旨意。我甘愿舍弃哪怕是您的女儿——我爱她胜过世间一切。我愿弃绝婚姻，好使自己全身心事奉童贞女之子。

君士坦丁
过来，再近些，让我将你拥入怀中！加利卡努斯，如今时候到了，我要告诉你一件至今不得不隐瞒的事。

加利卡努斯
什么事？

君士坦丁
我的女儿，还有你的两个女儿，都已选定了你自己也想走的那条圣路。

===Original===
Gallicanus
I rejoice to hear it.

Constantine
Their desire to keep their vow of virginity is so ardent that neither entreaties nor threats can alter their resolution.

Gallicanus
God help them to persevere!

Constantine
Come, let us go to their apartments.

Gallicanus
Lead on. I will follow.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
闻此我心欢喜。

君士坦丁
她们持守童贞之愿的心何等炽热，恳求与威吓都不能改变她们的决心。

加利卡努斯
愿上帝助她们坚定不移！

君士坦丁
来，我们到她们的居所去。

加利卡努斯
请先行。我随后来。

===Original===
Constantine
They are coming here. Look, they hasten to greet us, and my glorious mother, noble Helena, is with them. They all weep for joy.

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
她们到这儿来了。看，她们快步前来迎接我们，我光荣的母亲、高贵的海伦娜也与她们同来。众人喜极而泣。

#### Scene XIII / 第十三场

===Original===
Gallicanus
Be at peace, most holy virgins. Persevere in the fear of God, and preserve untouched the treasure of your virginity. Then you will be worthy of the embraces of the eternal King.

Constance
We shall keep our vows with more joy now we know that you are on our side.

Gallicanus
Have no fear that I shall put any obstacle in your way. Far from it! I consent gladly, and desire nothing better than to see you fulfil your vow, my Constance, you, for whom I was eager to risk life itself.

Constance
I see the hand of the Most High in this change in you.

Gallicanus
If I had not changed, and for the better, I could never have consented to renounce you.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
至圣的童贞女们，愿你们平安。当在敬畏上帝中坚忍持守，保守童贞的宝库完好无损。如此，你们便配得永恒之王的拥抱。

康斯坦丝
如今知道您与我们同心，我们守愿将更加喜乐。

加利卡努斯
不要担心我会在你们的路上设置任何阻碍。恰恰相反！我欣然应允，再无别的期盼，惟愿见你成就誓愿，我的康斯坦丝——为了你，我曾甘愿以身犯险。

康斯坦丝
我在您的这一转变中，看见了至高者的手。

加利卡努斯
倘若我没有转变——变得更好——我绝不会同意舍弃你。

===Original===
Constance
The Lover of virginal purity and the Author of all good resolutions made you renounce me because He had already claimed me for His own. May He Who has separated us in the body on earth unite us in the joys of eternity.

Gallicanus
So be it! So be it!

Constantine
And now, since we are united in the bond of Christ's love, you shall live with us in our palace, and be treated with as much honour as though you were our own son.

Gallicanus
What temptation is to be feared more than the lust of the eyes?

Constantine
None, I know.

===Chinese===
康斯坦丝
那钟爱童贞之纯洁的、一切善愿的创始者，使你舍弃了我，因为祂早已将我据为祂自己所有。愿那在地上使我们在肉身中分离的祂，在永恒的喜乐中使我们重聚。

加利卡努斯
愿它成就！愿它成就！

君士坦丁
如今，我们既已在基督之爱的纽带中联合为一，你就住在我们的宫中，受尊荣相待，一如我们的亲生子。

加利卡努斯
还有什么试探比眼目的情欲更可畏呢？

君士坦丁
据我所知，没有。

===Original===
Gallicanus
Then is it right that I should see her too often? As you know, I love her more than my own kin, more than my life, more than my soul!

Constantine
You must do what you think best.

Gallicanus
Thanks to our Lord Christ and to my labours, your army was never so strong as now. Give me leave, then, to transfer my service to that Emperor through Whose power I have returned victorious, and to Whom I owe any success I have won in life.

Constantine
To Him be praise and glory. All creatures should serve Him.

Gallicanus
Above all those whom He has generously helped in time of need.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
那么，我仍与她频频相见，妥当吗？您知道，我爱她胜过骨肉，胜过生命，胜过灵魂！

君士坦丁
你认为怎样最好，就怎样行吧。

加利卡努斯
托我主基督之恩并我多年劬劳，您的军队从未像如今这样强大。那么，请准许我转而事奉那位皇帝——正是借着祂的大能我凯旋而归，我生平所获的一切成就也都归于祂。

君士坦丁
愿赞美与荣耀归于祂。一切受造之物都当事奉祂。

加利卡努斯
尤其是那些祂在困乏之时慷慨救助过的人。

===Original===
Constantine
That is true.

Gallicanus
I am giving to my daughters the portion of my property which is theirs by right. Another I am devoting to the support of pilgrims. With the remainder I propose to enrich my slaves—whom I have freed—and to relieve the poor.

Constantine
You are disposing of your wealth wisely, and you will be rewarded.

Gallicanus
As for me, I long to go to Ostia and become the disciple of the holy man, Hilarion. In his brotherhood I hope to spend the rest of my life praising God and helping the poor.

Constantine
May the Divine Being to Whom all things are possible bring your plans to a happy issue! May you always do the will of Him Who lives and reigns in the Unity of the Trinity, and at last attain eternal joy!

===Chinese===
君士坦丁
此言不虚。

加利卡努斯
我正在把我财产中依理属于我女儿的那一份分给她们。另一份我用于资助朝圣者。至于余下的，我打算用来厚赐我的奴隶们——我已还他们自由——并周济穷人。

君士坦丁
你处置家财如此明智，必得酬报。

加利卡努斯
至于我自己，我渴望前往奥斯提亚（Ostia），投于圣者希拉里安（Hilarion）门下做弟子。我盼望在他的修道团体中度过余生，赞美上帝，救助穷人。

君士坦丁
愿那位万事皆能的上帝使你的宏图善终！愿你永远遵行那生活且掌权于三位一体合一之中的祂的旨意，并最终得享永恒的喜乐！

===Original===
Gallicanus
Amen.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
阿们。

### Part II / 第二部

#### Scene I / 第一场

===Original===
Julian
The cause of the unrest in our Empire is clear enough. These Christians enjoy too much liberty. Their claim that they obey laws made in the time of Constantine is false.

Consuls
It would be a disgrace to tolerate it.

Julian
I do not intend to tolerate it.

===Chinese===
尤利安
我帝国动荡的缘由再清楚不过。这些基督徒享有太多自由。他们声称遵守君士坦丁时代制定的法律，此言虚妄。

众执政官
容忍此事实为国耻。

尤利安
我也不打算容忍。

===Original===
Consuls
Those words are worthy of you.

Julian
Soldiers, arm yourselves and strip the Christians of all they possess. Remind them of these words of their Christ: "He who does not renounce all that he possesses for my sake cannot be my disciple."

Soldiers
We will carry out your orders instantly.

===Chinese===
众执政官
此言方配得上您。

尤利安
士兵们，拿起武器，剥尽基督徒的所有。要用他们的基督的这番话提醒他们：「人不撇下一切所有的跟从我，就不能作我的门徒。」

众士兵
我们即刻执行您的命令。

#### Scene II / 第二场

===Original===
Consuls
The soldiers have returned.

Julian
Is all well?

Soldiers
Well indeed.

===Chinese===
众执政官
士兵们回来了。

尤利安
诸事顺遂吗？

众士兵
再顺遂不过。

===Original===
Julian
Why have you returned so soon?

Soldiers
We will tell you. We had planned to seize Gallicanus's castle and occupy it in your name. But no sooner did one of us set foot on the threshold than he was straightway stricken with leprosy or madness.

Julian
Return and force Gallicanus to quit the realm or sacrifice to the gods.

===Chinese===
尤利安
那为何这么快就回来？

众士兵
我们这就禀告。我们原计划夺取加利卡努斯的城堡，以您的名义占领它。但我们中的一人刚踏上门槛，便即刻染上麻风或癫狂。

尤利安
回去，强迫加利卡努斯要么离开国境，要么向诸神献祭。

#### Scene III / 第三场

===Original===
Gallicanus
Do not waste your breath, fellows. Your advice is useless. I hold all that exists beneath the sun as nothing compared with eternal life. Banished for Christ's sake, I shall retire to Alexandria, where I hope to win the martyr's crown.

===Chinese===
加利卡努斯
弟兄们，不必白费唇舌了。你们的劝告毫无用处。在我看来，太阳之下的一切，与永恒生命相比皆如尘土。既为基督之故被逐，我将退往亚历山大里亚（Alexandria），盼望在那里赢得殉道者的冠冕。

#### Scene IV / 第四场

===Original===
Soldiers
Gallicanus, exiled by your orders, fled to Alexandria. He was arrested in that city by the Governor, Ratianus, and has perished by the sword.

Julian
That is well.

Soldiers
But John and Paul still defy you.

Julian
What are they doing?

===Chinese===
众士兵
加利卡努斯奉您的敕令被放逐，逃往亚历山大里亚。他在该城被总督拉提亚努斯（Ratianus）逮捕，已死于剑下。

尤利安
很好。

众士兵
但约翰与保罗仍在违抗您。

尤利安
他们在做什么？

===Original===
Soldiers
Travelling up and down the country giving away the fortune Constance left them.

Julian
Bring them before me.

Soldiers
They are here.

===Chinese===
众士兵
奔走各地，散尽康斯坦丝遗赠给他们的财产。

尤利安
把他们带到我面前来。

众士兵
他们已在此。

#### Scene V / 第五场

===Original===
Julian
John and Paul, from the cradle you have been attached to the Emperor's household. You served my predecessor.

John
That is so.

Julian
Then what could be more fitting than that you should serve me also in this palace where you were brought up?

Paul
We will not serve you.

===Chinese===
尤利安
约翰，保罗，你们自幼便依附于皇室。你们服侍过我的先帝。

约翰
正是。

尤利安
那么，你们在这座宫中长大，转而也来服侍我，岂不是顺理成章？

保罗
我们不服侍你。

===Original===
Julian
You refuse?

John
We have said it.

Julian
Do you deny that I am Augustus?

Paul
No, but we say you are Augustus with a difference.

===Chinese===
尤利安
你们敢拒绝？

约翰
我们已言明。

尤利安
你们否认我是奥古斯都吗？

保罗
不否认，但我们说，你是有别于他们的奥古斯都。

===Original===
Julian
How do I differ from my predecessors?

John
In your religion and your virtue.

Julian
What do you mean?

Paul
We mean that those most famous and glorious princes, Constantine, Constantius and Constance, whom we served, were very Christian rulers who were zealous in the service of God.

===Chinese===
尤利安
我与先帝们有何分别？

约翰
分别在于你的宗教与你的德行。

尤利安
此话怎讲？

保罗
我们的意思是：我们所服侍过的那些最负盛名、最光荣的君王——君士坦丁、君士坦提乌斯（Constantius）与康斯坦丝——都是极为虔诚的基督徒君主，热心事奉上帝。

===Original===
Julian
I know, but in this I do not choose to follow their example.

Paul
You follow worse examples. They frequented the churches and, laying their diadems on the ground, adored Jesus Christ on their knees.

Julian
And you think that I should imitate them?

John
You are not made of the same stuff.

===Chinese===
尤利安
我知道，但在这一点上，我不愿追随他们的榜样。

保罗
你所追随的是更坏的榜样。他们常去教堂，将王冠置于地上，跪拜耶稣基督。

尤利安
你以为我该效法他们？

约翰
你与他们并非同一种材料所造。

===Original===
Paul
By doing homage to the Creator they elevated the Imperial dignity—yes, they transfigured it with the splendour of their virtue and their holy lives. So they deserved the success which crowned their enterprises.

Julian
As I do.

John
In a very different way, for the divine grace was with them.

Julian
Absurd! Once I too was fool enough to believe in these meaningless practices. I was a priest of your Church.

John
Do you hear, Paul? How do you like this priest?

===Chinese===
保罗
正因为向创造者致敬，他们提升了帝国的尊严——是的，他们以自己德行与圣洁生命的光辉，使这尊严焕然改观。因此，加冕于他们功业的成功，实至名归。

尤利安
正如我一样。

约翰
方式截然不同，因为神圣的恩典与他们同在。

尤利安
荒谬！想当年我也曾愚不可及，信奉过这些无意义的仪轨。我还做过你们教会的神父。

约翰
你听见了吗，保罗？你觉得这位神父如何？

===Original===
Paul
Very well—as the devil's chaplain.

Julian
But when I found that there was nothing to be gained from it, I turned to the worship of the true Roman gods, thanks to whom I have been raised to the highest pinnacle of power.

John
You cut us short with this boast to avoid hearing the righteous praised.

Julian
What is it to me?

Paul
Nothing; but we would add something which does concern you. When the world was no longer worthy of those princes, they were summoned to the choir of angels, and this unhappy realm fell under your power.

===Chinese===
保罗
好得很——做个魔鬼的随堂神父。

尤利安
但当我发现从中一无所获，我便转而崇奉真正的罗马诸神，托他们的福，我被推上了权力的至高顶峰。

约翰
你用这番自夸打断我们，无非是免得听见义人受赞颂。

尤利安
与我何干？

保罗
毫无干系；但我们要补充一件与你相干的事。当这世界再不配有那些君王时，他们被召入天使的歌队，这不幸的国土便落入你的掌中。

===Original===
Julian
Why unhappy?

John
Because of the character of its ruler.

Paul
Have you not renounced the true religion and adopted the superstitions of idolatry? Because of this we have shunned you and your court.

Julian
You show yourselves greatly wanting in the respect due to me, yet I am ready to pardon your presumption and raise you to the highest office in my palace.

John
You waste your breath, apostate! We shall yield neither to blandishments nor threats.

===Chinese===
尤利安
何以说不幸？

约翰
因其统治者的人品。

保罗
你岂不是已背弃真宗教，改奉偶像崇拜的迷信？正因如此，我们才回避你和你的宫廷。

尤利安
你们对我如此大不敬，但我仍愿宽赦你们的放肆，并擢升你们担任宫中最高职位。

约翰
省省口舌吧，叛教者！甜言与威吓，我们都绝不屈从。

===Original===
Julian
I will give you ten days' grace, in the hope that you will come to your senses and repent. If you do, you will regain our Imperial favour. If not, I shall do what I have to do. You shall not make a mock of me.

Paul
What you have to do, do now, for you can never make us return either to your court, your service, or your gods.

Julian
You are dismissed. Leave me, but heed my warning.

John
We willingly accept the respite you have granted us, but only that we may spend the time consecrating all our faculties to heaven, and commending ourselves to God in prayer and fasting.

Paul
This is all we have to do now.

===Chinese===
尤利安
我给你们十天宽限，望你们幡然醒悟、悔悟前非。若肯如此，可重获我们的恩宠。否则，我将不得不行我当行之事。你们休想戏弄我。

保罗
你当行的事，现在就行吧，因为你永不能使我们回到你的宫廷、你的役使或你的诸神那里。

尤利安
退下吧。离开我，但要把我的警告放在心上。

约翰
我们乐于接受你所赐的宽限，只是为了利用这段时间，将我们全部心力奉献于上天，在祈祷与禁食中把自己交托给上帝。

保罗
这便是我们现在惟一要做的。

#### Scene VI / 第六场

===Original===
Julian
Go, Terentianus. Take with you a few trusted soldiers and compel John and Paul to sacrifice to Jupiter. If they persist in their refusal, let them be put to death, not publicly, but with the greatest possible secrecy, since they once held office in this palace.

===Chinese===
尤利安
去吧，泰伦提亚努斯。带上几名亲信士兵，强迫约翰与保罗向朱庇特献祭。他们若执意拒绝，便处死他们——不可公开，要竭尽隐秘，因为他们毕竟曾在这座宫中任职。

#### Scene VII / 第七场

===Original===
Terentianus
Paul and John, the Emperor Julian, my master, of his clemency sends you this gold statue of Jupiter, and commands you to burn incense before it. Refuse, and you will be put to death.

John
Since Julian is your master, live at peace with him, and enjoy his favour. But we have no master except our Lord Jesus Christ, for Whose love we ardently desire to die that we may the more quickly taste the joys of eternity.

Terentianus
Soldiers, why do you delay? Draw your swords and strike these traitors to the gods and to their Emperor. When they have breathed their last bury them secretly in this house and remove every trace of blood.

Soldiers
And if questions are asked, what are we to say?

===Chinese===
泰伦提亚努斯
保罗，约翰，我的主上尤利安皇帝大发慈悲，赐给你们这尊朱庇特金像，命你们在像前焚香。若拒绝，你们将被处死。

约翰
尤利安既是你的主上，你就与他相安无事，安享他的恩宠吧。但我们除我主耶稣基督外别无主上，为了对祂的爱，我们热切渴慕死亡，好更快尝到永恒的喜乐。

泰伦提亚努斯
士兵们，还迟疑什么？拔剑砍杀这些背叛诸神与其皇帝的逆贼。待他们断气，将他们秘密埋在这座宅子里，并清除一切血迹。

众士兵
若有人问起，我们怎么说？

===Original===
Terentianus
Say they have been banished.

John
To Thee, O Christ, Who reigneth with the Father and the Holy Ghost, one God, we raise our voices in this dreadful hour! In death as in life we praise Thee.

Paul
O Christ, receive our souls, which for Thy sake are being driven from this dwelling of clay!

===Chinese===
泰伦提亚努斯
就说他们已被放逐。

约翰
基督啊，您与圣父、圣灵同为一位上帝，永远掌权，在这可怕的时刻，我们向您高声呼求！无论在死亡中还是在生命里，我们都赞美您。

保罗
基督啊，求您接收我们的灵魂，它们正为您的缘故被逐出这尘土的居所！

#### Scene VIII / 第八场

===Original===
Terentianus
Christians, Christians, what ails my son?

Christians
He grinds his teeth, foams at the mouth, and rolls his eyes like a madman. He is sure possessed by a devil.

Terentianus
Woe to his father! Where was he stricken?

Christians
Before the tomb of the martyrs John and Paul. He writhes on the ground, and cries out that they are the cause of his torments.

===Chinese===
泰伦提亚努斯
基督徒们，基督徒们，我的儿子怎么了？

众基督徒
他咬牙切齿，口吐白沫，眼球乱转，状若疯癫。他定是被魔鬼附身了。

泰伦提亚努斯
他的父亲有祸了！他在哪里被附身的？

众基督徒
在殉道者约翰与保罗的墓前。他在地上翻滚，喊叫说他们是他痛苦的根源。

===Original===
Terentianus
Mine the fault! Mine the crime! It was at my command that the wretched boy laid his impious hands on those holy martyrs.

Christians
Since you were the partner of his guilt, it is right that you should share his sufferings.

Terentianus
I did but obey the wicked commands of my master, the Emperor Julian.

Christians
He himself has been struck down by the divine wrath.

===Chinese===
泰伦提亚努斯
罪在我！过在我！正是奉我之命，那可怜的孩子才向那些圣殉道者伸出了不敬之手。

众基督徒
你既是他的共罪之人，理当分担他的苦难。

泰伦提亚努斯
我只是遵行我主上尤利安皇帝的邪恶命令。

众基督徒
他自己也已被神圣的震怒击倒。

===Original===
Terentianus
I know, and am the more terrified. I see that no enemy of those servants of God can escape punishment.

Christians
You are right there.

Terentianus
What if in expiation of my crime I threw myself on my knees before the holy tombs?

Christians
You would win pardon if you were first cleansed by baptism.

===Chinese===
泰伦提亚努斯
我知道，因此更加恐惧。我看清了：那些上帝之仆的仇敌，无一能逃脱惩罚。

众基督徒
这你说对了。

泰伦提亚努斯
倘若我跪在圣墓之前，以赎我罪，会如何？

众基督徒
你若先受洗礼得洁净，便可蒙赦免。

#### Scene IX / 第九场

===Original===
Terentianus
Glorious witnesses of Christ, John and Paul, follow the example and commandment of your Master, and pray for your persecutors. Have compassion on the anguish of a father who fears to lose his child! Have pity on the sufferings of the son! Succour us both, and grant that, purified in the waters of baptism, we may persevere in the faith.

Christians
Dry your tears, Terentianus. Here is balm for your anguish. Look! Your son has recovered his health and his reason through the intercession of the martyrs.

Terentianus
Praise to the Eternal King Who covers His servants with such glory! Not only do their souls rejoice in heaven, but in the depths of the sepulchre their lifeless bones work astounding miracles, testifying to their sanctity and to the grace of our Lord Jesus Christ Who liveth and reigneth!

===Chinese===
泰伦提亚努斯
基督光荣的见证者约翰与保罗啊，愿你们效法你们夫子的榜样与诫命，为迫害你们的人祈祷。怜恤一位惧怕失丧孩子的父亲的伤痛！怜悯那孩子所受的苦楚！救助我们父子二人，使我们既在洗礼之水中得洁净，便能持守信仰。

众基督徒
拭去眼泪吧，泰伦提亚努斯。这是医治你伤痛的香膏。看啊！你的儿子已借着殉道者的代祷，恢复了健康与神智。

泰伦提亚努斯
赞美那以如此荣耀覆盖祂仆人的永恒之王！不仅他们的灵魂在天上欢喜，就在坟墓深处，他们无生命的骸骨也行出惊人的奇迹，见证他们的圣洁，并见证我们那活着掌权的主耶稣基督的恩典！

## Introduction / 导言

===Original===
By His Eminence Cardinal Gasquet

===Chinese===
加斯奎特枢机主教（His Eminence Cardinal Gasquet）撰

===Original===
Whatever may be thought of the precise merits of these six short dramas, now translated into English for the first time,1 it will be conceded that a collection of plays bearing the date of the 10th century, authenticated as the work of a woman, and a nun, is a remarkable phenomenon, interesting to students of monasticism and of the drama alike.

At one time, it is interesting to note, it was suggested that the author of these dramas was an Englishwoman. In fact, the English scholar, Laurence Humfrey, who first introduced them to notice in this country, endeavoured to prove that Roswitha was no other than St. Hilda of Northumbria. His theory cannot, of course, be maintained; but the very anxiety shown to identify this talented poetess and dramatist as a native of this country is evidence of the high estimation in which her compositions were held in the 16th century, the time when Laurence Humfrey, an exile from England for his religion, learnt to know them in Germany. It is now an established fact that the plays are the work of a Benedictine nun of Gandersheim, in Saxony, and their merits certainly justify her biographer’s exclamation: “Rara avis in Saxonia visa est.”

It used to be assumed that between the 6th and the 12th century all dramatic representations ceased, but each of these centuries when patiently searched has yielded some dramatic texts. The feudal period, reckoned the most barbarous, and Germania, set down then, as later in history, as the least civilized of countries, have produced the most considerable and least imperfect of these texts in the plays of Hrotsuitha, or Roswitha, a nun of the Order of St. Benedict, who spent her religious life in the Convent of Gandersheim.

===Chinese===
无论人们对这六部短剧的确切价值作何评判——它们此刻是首次被译成英文1——有一点当为世人所公认：一批署着 10 世纪年月的剧作，经确证出自一位女性、而且是一位修女之手，实乃一种非凡的现象，无论研究隐修制度的研究者还是研究戏剧的研究者，都会对之深感兴趣。
